# Shared log (ChatGPT + Claude)

This is the one place both assistants read and write. If it is not here, the other assistant cannot see it.

## Rules
1. At the start of a chat, read `AGENTS.md`, this file, `research_docs/DECISION_LOG.md` and `research_docs/MASTER_SPECIFICATION.md`.
2. Before ending a chat, add an entry at the bottom: date, who, what was done, results, **script path, branch, and workflow run link**.
3. Never quote a number without its script and run. A number with no script is a claim, not a finding (see Entry 3).
4. Mark every entry `CONFIRMED` (reproduced by running committed code), `REPORTED` (stated but not reproduced), or `PROVISIONAL` (known to use a different method than the approved one).
5. Do not edit old entries. Add a correction entry that points to the old one.

## Standing definitions (user's words, 7 Nov 2026)
- **Cluster window:** 15 trading sessions. Four or more distinct officers/directors must have **bought** (transaction date) inside the 15 completed sessions before entry, and their filings must already be public. A May purchase filed in August does not count. Entry = next regular open after the filing that brings the count to four. *Status: stated by the user in chat; not yet written into `DECISION_LOG.md` as a numbered decision.*
- Other rules unchanged: $3M average daily dollar volume floor, Form 4 code P, 10b5-1 and non-discretionary buys screened out, price-only 21-session hold (entry session = day one), results in the stock's fixed pre-entry ATR(14) with SPY restated on the same scale.

Other ChatGPT results: four buyers 21-session excess over SPY +0.74 ATR (95% interval -0.38 to +1.95, raw p=0.196); five-plus +0.44 (-0.51 to +1.40); none passed Holm correction. CFO participation within first four-plus clusters +0.44 ATR more excess (interval -1.30 to +2.42, raw p=0.61). One and two buyers lagged all three references at all five horizons. Broad-market-down bands alone lagged all three references.

Liquid four-plus cluster ($3M floor): 91 trades, stock +0.53, SPY +0.01, excess +0.53, median +0.17, 51 of 91 positive. Below the floor: 46 trades, excess +0.88, median +0.09 (a few thin winners; removing the best under-$1M trade cut +1.56 to +0.69).
Price setup (liquid four-plus, SPY up >= 0.5 of its ATR, stock down 2 to 5 ATR over 15 sessions): 91 -> 39 -> 15 trades, excess +1.42 mean, +1.27 median.
CFO split inside the $3M group: price setup with CFO 7 trades (+1.29 stock, -0.38 SPY, +1.68 excess); price setup no identified CFO 8 trades (+1.31, +0.11, +1.20); outside setup with CFO 25 trades (+0.05, +0.01, +0.04); outside setup no identified CFO 51 trades (+0.55, +0.05, +0.50).
Purchase-size screen: no screen 91 (+0.53); >= $25k 65 (+0.47); >= $50k 43 (+0.76 mean, +0.43 median); >= $100k 23 (+0.42).
Entry timing: filing-day close vs next open can flip the sign of the typical result; next open is the investable rule (D-051).
Caveat on provenance: the script that generated the depth-band rows (132 / 455 / 211) was never committed (see Entry 3).

## Entry 3 | 7 Nov 2026 | ChatGPT (via user) | Reconciliation reply | REPORTED
Why depth-table numbers differed from Claude's: the 132 / 455 / 211 rows classify the stock and SPY over the 21 sessions before the **insider's purchase date** (anchor = first session on or after the latest transaction date; stock context = (Close[p-1] - Close[p-22]) / ATR14(p)). The 15-trade price setup uses the 15 sessions before the **investor entry date** ((Close[e-1] - Close[e-16]) / ATR14(e)). Neither applies the D-055 laggard condition. Two weightings exist with identical counts:
| Cell | Entries (dates) | Equal weight per date: stock / SPY | Equal weight per disclosure: stock / SPY |
|---|---|---|---|
| Down 0.5 to <2, SPY flat | 132 (49) | +0.63 / +0.06 | +0.57 / -0.04 |
| Down 0.5 to <2, SPY up | 455 (97) | +0.26 / +0.06 | +0.40 / -0.02 |
| Down 5+, SPY down | 211 (56) | +0.48 / +0.50 | +0.86 / +0.70 |
Final script that assembled and weighted this table: **not recovered** (status of the table: unreproducible).
Other answers: $50k screen = every one of four buyers needed a single qualifying filing >= $50,000, applied before counting buyers, so the 43 trades are not a subset of the 91. ATR14 = simple mean of the 14 true ranges before the anchor bar. Price setup uses the stock's own SPY regime ((SPY close[j-1] - close[j-16]) / SPY ATR14, >= 0.5). Recovered code committed to branch `recovery/nov7-cohort-provenance`: `research/recovered_20261107/{matrix_warm,wider,liquidity_tiers,purchase_size_screen}.py`.
Cluster selector rules (from `wider.py`): group by issuer CIK + ticker at filing + uppercased security title; sole-owner officer/director, no entity-like names; a filing counts only if at least one transaction date lies in [entry - 15 sessions, entry); trigger when the count crosses from below four to four or more; representative filing = prefer outcome_status ok, then earliest filing date and accession.

## Entry 5 | 7 Nov 2026 | Claude | Chance (data-snooping) test | CONFIRMED
Script `research/claude_tests/chance_test.py` (imports ChatGPT code unchanged), run **42419094111**. Self-check reproduced +1.422 / +1.269 (15 trades) and +1.677 (7 trades). Shuffled the excess among the 91 liquid trades (all, and within calendar week), repeated the best-slice hunt 5,000 times. Share of shuffles where chance matched the real best slice:
| Menu | Measure | Real best | All shuffled | Within-week |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Small (12 slices) | best mean, n>=15 | +1.473 (no CFO, SPY>=0.5, n=23) | 25.5% | 58.3% |
| Small | best t, n>=8 | 2.14 (SPY>=0.5, decline 2-5, n=15) | 24.8% | 17.6% |
| Wide (702 slices) | best mean | +1.473 | 64.8% | 68.0% |
| Wide | best t | 2.74 (SPY>=0, decline>=2, n=25) | 28.1% | 5.3% |
Reading: a hunt over these slices finds results this good by luck often. CFO is not the driver (CFO + SPY up: 16 trades +0.94; no CFO + SPY up: 23 trades +1.47). Not evidence of no edge: 91 trades with SD near 3 ATR cannot detect a modest edge.

## Entry 6 | 7 Nov 2026 | Claude | Stand-alone SPY-rising test | CONFIRMED (single-question p is optimistic)
Script `research/claude_tests/spy_rising_test.py`, run **42419781207**. Question: after SPY rose >= 0.5 of its own ATR over the prior 15 sessions (D-055 cut-off), is excess higher? Rising: 39 trades, +1.25. Not rising: 52 trades, -0.02. Difference +1.27. Shuffle across trades: chance 1.8% of 20,000. Shuffle by entry date: 3.7%. Week-block bootstrap 95% range for the difference: +0.08 to +2.96. Neighbouring cut-offs (0, 0.25, 0.75, 1.0): rising group +1.03 to +1.25, so 0.5 is not a lucky spot.
Concentration: 23 entry dates, 12 calendar weeks. Four consecutive weeks (11 May - 7 Jun 2026; ISO weeks 20-23; 25 trades) hold about 93% of the total gain (about +1.8 ATR per trade); the other 14 rising-market trades average about +0.24. Without the best week (ISO week 22) the mean is +0.69; without the best trade +0.78; without the best three +0.45; median +0.56. 67% of rising-market trades positive vs 48% of the rest.
Caveat: the question was chosen after looking at this same data, so the 2-4% understates the real chance of luck; the best-of-menu figure in Entry 5 (about 25%) overstates it. Treat as an out-of-sample candidate, not an edge.

## Entry 9 | 7 Nov 2026 | Claude | Step 2A: outlier-robust and week-clustered checks on the 91 liquid trades | CONFIRMED
Script `research/canonical/robustness_check.py` (imports the canonical selector, ChatGPT's code unchanged), branch `claude/canonical-selector-20261107`, run **42421864891**. Self-check reproduced 91 trades, mean excess +0.5257 (stock +0.5337, SPY +0.0080). Independent units: 62 entry dates, 30 ISO weeks, 83 tickers. Week-block bootstrap, 10,000 draws, seed 20261107.
| Statistic (excess over SPY, ATR) | Value | 95% range | Share of draws <= 0 |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| Mean | +0.526 | -0.067 to +1.195 | 4.4% |
| Median | +0.165 | -0.151 to +0.588 | 17.5% |
| Trimmed mean (5% each tail) | +0.267 | -0.207 to +0.742 | 12.2% |
| Capped mean (each trade +-5 ATR) | +0.318 | -0.144 to +0.727 | 8.4% |
| Capped mean (+-3 ATR) | +0.199 | -0.192 to +0.537 | 15.3% |
Concentration: ISO week 2026-W22 alone holds 24.14 of the 47.84 total ATR (50%); weeks 22, 25, 20, 21 together hold about 102%. Dropping W22 leaves a mean of +0.269. Only 15 of 30 weeks have positive total excess. Sign test: 51 of 91 beat SPY, one-sided chance under a coin flip 14.7%.
Selector sensitivity (only the options exposed by the recovered `select()`; sole-owner and one-filing-per-cluster are hard-coded and were not varied): entity-name exclusion and same-title matching make no difference to the liquid cohort (91 trades, +0.526). The "previous" count rule gives 89 trades, +0.517. Keeping only the first cluster per issuer gives 81 trades, +0.640 (median +0.256). Capped means +0.30 to +0.41. The cohort is not fragile to these options.
Variants counted so far: 12 selector variants here, 5 summary statistics, 714 slices in Entry 5, 5 cut-offs in Entry 6.
Reading: the unadjusted mean is borderline (4.4% one-sided), and every outlier-robust version is weaker and its range includes zero. Not an edge; a candidate that needs older years. Steps 2B (costs) and 2C (entry timing) still to do.

Self-check: buyer sets rebuilt from the selector's own data match its buyer count and CFO flag on 137 of 137 trades. CEO = any of the four buyers is an officer whose title matches CEO / chief executive officer. |---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| All | 91 | 62 / 30 | +0.534 | +0.008 | +0.526 | +0.165 | +0.318 | 51 |
| CEO among buyers | 47 | 38 / 24 | -0.179 | -0.066 | -0.113 | -0.157 | -0.118 | 22 |

## Entry 11 | 7 Nov 2026 | Claude | Step 2E: chance test on "neither a CEO nor a CFO among the buyers" | CONFIRMED (exploratory; read the correction)
Script `research/canonical/chance_roles.py` (imports the canonical selector, ChatGPT's code unchanged; `add_roles` in `cluster_selector.py` is new and self-checked against the selector on 137 of 137 trades), branch `claude/canonical-selector-20261107`, run **42422870313**. Permutations shuffle outcomes only among trades of the same ISO week (10,000 draws, seed 20261107), so a week's market backdrop cannot create the result.
**Correction to the script text:** it labels Test 1 "the one pre-stated question". That is wrong. The group was picked after seeing the Entry 10 role table, so Test 1's chance figures are optimistic.
Group "neither CEO nor CFO": 36 trades, excess +1.362 (rest -0.021), difference +1.383 ATR, median +0.633 higher than rest. Chance figures: Test 1, same-week shuffle 3.7% (mean), 5.0% (capped +-5), 2.7% (median); shuffle-all 1.2%. Test 2, best of the 8 role groups that have >= 8 trades (the family examined so far): 40.1% chance a best group is at least this good. That family includes an 8-trade group, which inflates it; a version limited to groups of 20+ trades was NOT run.
Concentration: the group holds 102% of the cohort's total gain from 40% of trades. Six of the top 10 trades are in it (OTLK, XRAY, NCLH, SPG, GPGI, ALT); the other four are CEO+CFO (HROW, GSHD, NSP) and CFO-only (RYAN). Group minus rest without the top 1 / 3 / 5 trades: +0.87 / +0.52 / +0.44. Without week 2026-W22: +0.77. Hot weeks (20-23, 25): group +2.68 vs rest +0.78 (16 / 15 trades); all other weeks: +0.31 vs -0.32 (20 / 40 trades). The sign is the same in both regimes; the size is not.
Director count (second question, descriptive): pure directors (director, not officer) among the four buyers vs mean excess: 0 -> +0.40 (n=10); 1 -> -0.21 (14); 2 -> -0.23 (25); 3 -> +0.68 (21); 4 -> +2.89 (13, median +0.92); 5+ -> mixed, tiny groups. Not monotonic. Spearman +0.156, same-week chance 7.6%.
Reading: a candidate, not a result. The single-question figure is optimistic, the best-of-family figure is pessimistic, and the effect rides on a few trades from director-heavy clusters. Keep "no CEO / no CFO among buyers" as a separate candidate factor for the frozen-rule test on older years; do not size or trade on it.

## Entry 19 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Counts-only archive coverage audit and D-061 floor check | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; counts from run log); price matching is symbol-only, so event-level counts are PROVISIONAL
Script `research/canonical/archive_coverage_audit.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/archive-coverage-and-floor-check.yml`, merged to `main` by pull requests #11, #12 and #14. It reads only symbol, date/timestamp, close and volume (close x volume = dollar volume) and computes no returns. Final run **42477554345** (success, 28 jobs; it reused the events artifact from run 42473849197 to skip the slow SEC step). Run 42472587800 and run 42473849197 matched plain tickers only and wrongly reported that Lehman and WaMu were missing; their match counts are superseded and should not be quoted. Run 42473718742 failed on a GitHub runner shutdown, not on code.
**Coverage (CONFIRMED).** stock_daily and etf_daily each have 6,753 files, and every year's file dates equal the NYSE session calendar (no missing or extra dates, no duplicate symbols, no bad rows, every file's date matches its name). Rows per stock file rise from about 2,700 (2000) to about 6,500-7,600 (2020s). Files up to the first 58 of 2026 use a `timestamp` column; later files use `date`. Research code must accept both. SPY is present on every date. IWM starts mid-2000 (151 of 252 dates) and ONEQ in 2003 (64 of 252), so both cover 2006 onward in full.
**Delisted names (CONFIRMED, from the vendor's data-quality page and the files).** Delisted securities are stored as `<TICKER>-DELISTED` (267 to 4,049 such symbols per year). A ticker later reused can exist twice, for example plain `WM` (Waste Management, continuous 2000 to 2026) and a separate delisted series. Research code must match both forms. `LEH-DELISTED` runs to 2008-09-17, the day the NYSE suspended trading (SEC Form 25-NSE); `LEHMQ-DELISTED` continues to 2012-03-05. Also found with sensible end dates: CFC, MER, IMB, WYE, SGP, ROH, XTO, TWX, MON, EKDKQ.
**Not found:** Washington Mutual (no `WM-DELISTED`, `WAMUQ` or `WAMUQ-DELISTED`; plain `WM` is Waste Management at about $35 in Sept 2008), Bear Stearns (`BSC`, `BSC-DELISTED`), and the bankruptcy-era Q-suffix tickers tried (CCTYQ, ENRNQ, WCOEQ, SHLDQ, JCPNQ, GMGMQ, BGPIQ). Q-suffix names trade over the counter and may be excluded on purpose. **Identity collisions exist:** `BBI-DELISTED` closes at about $8,784 in Sept 2008 and runs to 2022, so it is not Blockbuster; `MON-DELISTED` has a gap from 2018-06-06 to 2021-03-16, so two companies are joined. Symbol-only matching can attach the wrong company's prices; the ticker-to-security check remains required.
**Events and the $3M floor (PROVISIONAL: symbol-only match, no returns).** 9,282 four-plus-buyer events (filings only, Entry 15 logic): liquid 2,610; illiquid 3,602; incomplete (fewer than 15 bars in the prior 15 sessions) 1,007; no price found 1,643; blank ticker 297; over-the-counter ticker 123. Of the matched events, 3,316 matched through a `-DELISTED` series and 3,903 through the plain symbol (24 had both in the window). The no-price share falls from 43% in 2006 to 6% in 2026; sample unmatched tickers are mostly small banks and thin names. 2026 gives 98 liquid events, consistent with the pilot's 91 to 96 completed liquid trades.
**D-061 condition (counts only).** Sealed share of 2006-2025 liquid events at the 2020-01-01 cut: 834 of 2,512 = 33.2%, above 30%. Nearest quarter boundary inside 20-30%: 2020-04-01 at 27.1% (680 of 2,512). Recorded as D-063: development through 2019-12-31, gap 2020 Q1, sealed 2020-04-01 to 2025-12-31. No returns were computed for any entry date.
**Michael's decisions this session.** Decline look-back is 21 completed sessions for all new work (D-062).
Not yet done: ticker-to-security identity check (the 578 price-contradicted matches and reused tickers such as BBI and MON); effective-dated benchmark mapping; ask MarketParquet whether Washington Mutual and Bear Stearns exist under other symbols; add D-056 to the decision log.

## Entry 21 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Identity-check script written; "45% delisted" clarified | PROVISIONAL (script self-test only; no workflow run yet)
**Clarification (no new run).** Michael asked whether "45% of events came from delisted securities" means almost half the companies went bankrupt. No. The only committed figure is Entry 19 (run 42477554345): 3,316 matched through a `-DELISTED` series and 3,903 through the plain ticker (24 both), i.e. about 46% of 7,195 matched events (not 45%; the "45%" was not found in the repo), PROVISIONAL because the match is by symbol only. It counts events, not companies. The vendor's `-DELISTED` tag means only that the security stopped trading under that listing; the probe names in Entry 19 that carry it include acquisitions (MER, WYE, XTO, TWX, MON, ROH) as well as failures (LEH). Literature: acquisition/merger delistings behave differently from performance-related ones (Dimson & Stolin, LBS WP 2004); missing delisting returns for negative delistings bias results (Shumway 1997, J. Finance; Shumway & Warther 1999 use -55% for missing performance-related delisting returns).
**Code.** Script `research/canonical/identity_check.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/identity-check.yml`, branch `claude/identity-check-20261108`, pull request #18 (not merged; a workflow can only be dispatched from `main`, so it needs Michael's OK). Method: for each symbol match, compare the insiders' weighted purchase price (Form 4) with the archive close on or within 7 days before the purchase date; median ratio 0.75-1.33 = identity ok, otherwise contradicted; no price = unverifiable. Re-runs the $3M floor split by identity status, with the sealed-share check at 2020Q1 and 2020Q2. Terminal information for `-DELISTED` series (calendar days from entry to last bar, last-close bucket) is produced only for entries <= 2019-12-31 and the 2026 pilot, per D-063 and Entry 17. No returns computed. `events` step asserts its events equal `cluster_counts_by_year.find_events`.
Not run: workflow run link none yet. Self-test passed locally.

## Entry 22 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Identity check and floor re-run: results | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; counts from run log); no returns computed
Script `research/canonical/identity_check.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/identity-check.yml`, merged to `main` by pull request #18. Run **42479461689** (success, events + 21 year jobs + summary). Events: 9,282, identical to `cluster_counts_by_year.find_events`; 9,242 carry at least one purchase price. Test: median of (insiders' weighted purchase price / archive close on or up to 7 days before the purchase date) inside 0.75-1.33 = identity ok.
**A. Identity (8,862 events with a usable ticker).** ok 5,696; contradicted 1,418; unverifiable 105; no price 1,643. Contradicted share of price-matched events: 1,418 of 7,219 = 19.6% (plain-ticker 914, -DELISTED 504). Contradicted is NOT proof of a wrong company: it also flags split-adjusted archive closes or a mis-parsed Form 4 price. Most contradicted symbols: SYBT 112 events and GABC 74 (both plain), then NRIM 20, TURN 18, CPF 17. A single issuer with 112 events needs a look (repeated crossings by the same company, or an adjusted-price series) before the matcher is trusted. Median-ratio histogram: 0.9-1.1 is 5,331 events; 741 events sit below 0.25 (archive close more than four times the insider price).
**B. Delisted share.** -DELISTED is 3,331 of 7,219 matched events = 46.1%; among identity-ok events 2,780 of 5,696 = 48.8%. So the roughly 46% in Entry 19 survives the identity filter. It counts events, not companies, and the tag is not a delisting reason.
**C. $3M floor re-run (counts only).** Identity ok only: 2,212 liquid (2006-2025: 2,115; sealed share from 2020Q2 = 621 = 29.4%; from 2020Q1 = 764 = 36.1%; 2026 pilot 97). All matched (Entry 19 style): 2,615 (sealed from 2020Q2 = 27.0%; 2020Q1 = 33.2%; 2026: 98). D-063's 20-30% condition at 2020-04-01 still holds on identity-ok events (29.4%) but sits near the 30% edge; D-063 unchanged, to be revisited if the matcher changes.
**D. Where -DELISTED series end (development entries <= 2019-12-31 and 2026 pilot only; 2,432 identity-not-contradicted events; sealed entries excluded by design).** Ends within about 21 sessions of entry: 6 (0.25%). Ends later, by last close: under $0.50 208; $0.50-1 62; $1-5 331; $5-20 1,025; $20 or more 800. Last close under $1 (failure-like heuristic): 270 of 2,432 = 11%; $5 or more (acquisition-like heuristic): 1,825 = 75%. These are price heuristics, not delisting reasons.
**Reading.** Delisting inside the 21-session hold is rare (6 of 2,432), so the terminal-price rule matters little for the hold itself, but ruin risk is not absent: about one in nine delisted-matched events ends under $1 eventually. The identity filter removes roughly a fifth of price-matched events; SYBT and GABC must be diagnosed first.
Next: (1) diagnose SYBT, GABC and the 741 low-ratio events (adjusted closes vs wrong company vs Form 4 price parse) by looking at a few events' purchase prices and closes; (2) then rerun with the fix and move to development-period work with the 21-session look-back; (3) ask MarketParquet about Washington Mutual and Bear Stearns (Claude drafts); (4) add D-056 to the decision log.

## Entry 23 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Why 19.6% of price matches "contradict": the archive is split-adjusted; and routine repeat-buyer issuers | CONFIRMED (printed values from committed code); the volume reading is an INFERENCE (marked)
Scripts `research/canonical/identity_check.py` (subcommands `diagnose` and `probe`), workflows `identity-diagnose.yml` (run **42484790920**, 11 sample years) and `split-probe.yml` (run **42484948163**), merged to `main` by pull requests (diagnostic and probe). No returns computed; prints only past purchase prices, closes and volumes.
**1. The archive closes are split-adjusted to today's share count (CONFIRMED from printed rows).** Contradicted events show exact split-like ratios of insider price to archive close: SYBT 1.500 on every event (2006-2016; a 3-for-2 split), FAST 4.000 (2018), CF 5.0 (2006-2010), NRIM 4.0, FFIN about 4-6, PRA 2.0, HOMB 2.0; low ratios are reverse splits: ACFC 0.196-0.201, CPST 0.10 / 0.005, PXLW 0.07-0.08, HMPR 0.04, OESX 0.10, PRTS 0.10, GE 0.20 (2006, 2019), CVM 0.000 (insider 0.58 vs archive 4,650). These are the same company on an adjusted price scale, NOT wrong-company matches. So the Entry 22 "contradicted" count (1,418) is mostly an adjustment artifact, and identity-ok (5,696) understates true matches. Real wrong-company cases (reused tickers such as BBI, MON) are not separated yet.
**2. Volume appears adjusted the same way (INFERENCE, not verified against an as-traded source).** Probe, GE across the 1-for-8 reverse split of 2021-08-02: close 62-66 and volume 10.8M-40M with no jump at the split date. FAST across the 2-for-1 of 2019-05-23: close 15.2-16.2, volume 6M-14M with no discontinuity. If volume is adjusted inversely to price, close x volume (dollar volume) is split-invariant, so the $3M floor counts in Entries 19 and 22 are not distorted by splits (spin-offs not checked). The identity test, which compares prices, is the part that breaks.
**3. Repeat-buyer issuers (CONFIRMED counts per sampled year; cause is an inference).** The same issuer crosses the four-buyer threshold up to 12 times in a year: CIK 0001047862 has 12 events in each of 2008, 2010, 2012, 2014, 2016, 2018, 2019; SYBT (CIK 0000835324) has 8-12 per year; GABC is similar. SYBT rows show seven or more insiders buying at the same price on the same day, month after month. That looks like a routine plan (monthly director or dividend-style purchases), not discretionary buying. The cluster rule excludes only flagged 10b5-1 and non-discretionary buys, so unflagged routine purchases are inside the sample and inflate the event count and the delisted-or-not statistics.
Next: (1) fix the identity test: snap the median ratio to a split grid (1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 10 and their inverses), require the factor to be stable across purchases, and report true wrong-company cases separately; (2) count events per issuer and per issuer-year, and show the share of events from issuers with more than 3 events in a year; (3) decision for Michael: whether repeat or monthly-pattern buyers should be excluded or collapsed to the first event per issuer per year (options will be listed with the counts). Open: ask MarketParquet about Washington Mutual and Bear Stearns; add D-056 to the decision log.

## Entry 25 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Split-grid identity, routine-purchase exclusion (D-064), option C (D-065), sealed-share cut (D-066 -> D-067) | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; counts from run logs); thresholds are Claude's operationalisation of Michael's decisions and are not yet approved in detail; no returns computed
Script `research/canonical/refined_study.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/refined-study.yml`, merged to `main` by pull requests #24 and #25. Run **42487092445** (first), run **42488283630** (adds threshold sensitivity and removed-event diagnostics; every shared number identical). Self-tests passed in both runs. Base events: 9,282, identical to `cluster_counts_by_year.find_events`.
**Corrects Entries 22-23 on identity.** The archive is split-adjusted, so the old "contradicted" count (1,418 of 7,219 = 19.6%) was mostly an adjustment artifact. With a split grid (factors 1.5 ... 1000 and inverses, within 7%) plus stability (at least 75% of purchase ratios within 20% of the median, at least two price points): base events ok 5,696; split_ok 1,102; split_unverified 1; unverifiable 105; mismatch 315 (4.4% of 7,219 price-matched); no_price 1,643. Residual mismatches are the real review list (top: WHLR 9, HK 8, CWBC 7, CTO 6, EQT 5); compound splits, spin-offs and reused tickers are not separated. The grid has a false-accept chance for a truly wrong company that was not measured.
**D-064 routine exclusion.** (a) three or more distinct owners buying the same date within one cent; (b) issuer already showed (a) in two of the three complete months before the filing month; look-back only (self-tests prove a filing arriving after the entry cannot undo the event). Refined events 5,829 vs base 9,282: 5,652 in both, 3,630 base-only (removed), 177 refined-only (a later discretionary crossing). Run stats: 261 crossings had routine rows excluded; 63 dropped by (b). Sensitivity (refined events): exact price 6,026; 0.5 cent 5,955; 1 cent 5,829; 2 cents 5,708; 1 cent with four-owner minimum 6,304; exact price with four owners 6,402. So the removal is not an artifact of the one-cent choice. Removed events are not cheap-stock noise: price level of removed vs kept events, under $2 14% vs 12%, $20+ 35% vs 22%. They concentrate in repeat issuers: 1,450 issuers, 37% inside issuer-years with more than 3 base events; most removed: CIK 0001047862 (ticker ED) 212, SYBT 150, GABC 129, YORW 61, AUBN 58. INFERENCE (not verified): these look like dividend-reinvestment or director purchase plans.
**Events per issuer-year.** Base 9,282 events, 3,402 issuers, 6,480 issuer-years, max 13 in one issuer-year, 18.7% of events in issuer-years above 3. Base + option C 8,588. Refined 5,829, 2,588 issuers, 4,594 issuer-years, max 7, 5.9%. Refined + C 5,439, max 5, 3.2%.
**Tradable = identity fine (ok or split_ok) and $3M floor (15 prior bars).** Base 2,530 (2006-2025: 2,432; 2026 pilot 98); base + C 2,368 (2,274; 94); refined 2,015 (1,931; 84); **refined + C 1,924 (1,844; 80), the primary set.** Option C is applied to tradable events, so an illiquid or unmatched earlier event does not block a later tradable one (Claude's choice).

Entries are bunched in market-wide episodes (2008Q4 66, 2011Q3 74, 2020Q1 135), so the effective number of independent observations is well below the entry counts.

## Entry 30 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Why the refined pilot (+0.333, 80) differs from the Entry 8 pilot (+0.526, 91): reconciliation | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run logs); the reading of D-064 is an INFERENCE (marked)
Scripts `research/canonical/dump_old_trades.py` and `research/canonical/reconcile_pilot.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/reconcile-pilot.yml` (merged by pull requests), runs **42492056750** and **42492198777** (the second only lists every trade; all numbers identical). The first job runs ChatGPT's recovered selector unchanged on its saved inputs and dumps all 137 trades; the second recomputes them from the archive with `refined_baseline.trade_outcome` and compares cohorts against run 42490748366 (new returns) and run 42488283630 (refined events). Only 2026 pilot entries were touched.
**1. Definition: identical.** All 137 old trades recomputed from the archive with the new code: mean absolute difference in excess 0.0000, maximum 0.000, entry-open and ATR ratios exactly 1.0000 (liquid 91: +0.5257 both ways). So the return code and data are not the cause.
**2. Cohort: the whole gap is the cohort.** 74 trades are in both (mean excess +0.3975 under either code). 17 old liquid trades are not in the new set (mean +1.0837, total 18.42 ATR): 13 removed by D-064 (mean +1.260) and 4 collapsed by option C (mean +0.510: NCLH 2026-06-04 +0.09, KKR 2026-03-04 +0.60, AGBK 2026-06-03 +0.08, AGBK 2026-06-04 +1.27). 6 trades are new (not in the old 137; mean -0.4646, total -2.79): BMI, HTGC 2026-03-06, BYRN, THRY, SHAK, BETR. Check: 74 x 0.3975 = 29.42; 29.42 + 18.42 = 47.84 (old total, 91 trades); 29.42 - 2.79 = 26.63 (new total, 80 trades).
**3. One trade is about 90% of the gap.** The D-064 removals are SPG 2026-01-06 +6.05, SPG 2026-04-02 -1.67, BWFG -1.36, PMTS -0.00, INV -1.81, GDV -2.17, HTGC 2026-03-02 +2.20, KOS +1.11, CABA +1.35, OTLK 2026-05-28 **+19.19**, RWT -4.21, CLBK +0.26, FSBC -2.56. OTLK alone is 19.19 of the 21.21 ATR difference in totals (47.84 vs 26.63). Without OTLK and the first SPG the other 11 removed trades average -0.81, so D-064 mostly removed losers apart from those two.
**4. INFERENCE, not checked against filings:** rule (a) (three or more owners, same date, same price within one cent) may be catching group participation in a company financing or offering, not only dividend-reinvestment or director plans (the likely cause for ED, SYBT, GABC in Entry 25). I have not read OTLK's or SPG's Form 4s. D-064's thresholds were set from counts only; changing them because one pilot winner was removed would be tuning on returns, so any change should rest on the filing mechanism or on development-years evidence, not on this pilot.
Reading: the Entry 8 +0.526 and the refined +0.333 differ because of which trades are in, mostly OTLK; neither pilot number is reliable on 80 to 91 trades from 28 to 30 weeks. The development-years baseline (Entry 28, n=1,372) is the better guide.
Next: (1) Michael decides whether to look at the mechanism behind rule (a) (recommended: read the Form 4s for the 13 removed pilot trades and a sample of development-years removals, classifying plan versus financing by a written criterion, no returns); (2) compute returns for the D-064-removed events in the development years as a diagnostic (exploratory, development only) to see whether removed events behave differently from kept ones; (3) factor work on the primary set.

## Entry 31 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Do the D-064 removals behave differently from the kept events? Group check, development years and 2026 pilot | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); PROVISIONAL returns code as in Entry 28
Script `research/canonical/removed_events_check.py` (reuses `refined_baseline.py year` for returns), workflow `.github/workflows/removed-check.yml`, merged by pull request, run **42492703495** (summary job 128768877590; select job 128768319977). Diagnostic only: D-064 is unchanged. D-068 guard applied: no entries from 2021-01-01 to 2025-12-31. Groups require identity fine and the $3M floor: kept_C = the primary set after option C (1,453 allowed entries, matches Entry 28); removed_raw = base events with no refined event on the same issuer and entry date; removed_C = removed_raw after option C.
**Size of the effect on the tradable set.** Of the 3,630 base-only events removed by D-064 (Entry 25), only 542 are tradable (identity fine and floor), 431 of them in allowed years (469 and 370 after option C). So D-064 changes about 22% of the tradable events (542 of 1,924 + 542), not the 39% that the raw event counts suggest; most removed events fail the floor or identity anyway. (Correction of a figure I gave in chat.)
**Development years (entries <= 2020-12-31), excess over SPY in ATR.** kept_C n=1,372 +0.154 (median +0.219); removed_raw n=415 +0.079 (median -0.068); removed_C n=354 +0.211 (median +0.043). Difference removed_C minus kept_C +0.057, week-block 95% range -0.280 to +0.405, 38.1% of draws at or below zero; removed_raw minus kept_C -0.076, range -0.417 to +0.273. Independence: removed_C 159 issuers, 295 entry weeks (all years). Equal weight per issuer: kept 0.118 over 820 issuers; removed 0.374 over 145 issuers.
**Concentration.** Top 8 issuers hold 232 of 415 removed_raw development events (56%); CIK 0001047862 alone has 172 (41%) with mean -0.14, consistent with the routine-plan reading for that issuer (INFERENCE; filings not read). By year the two groups differ in both directions (removed better in 8 of 15 years, worse in 7). Without the three large episodes: kept +0.309 (n=1,097), removed_C +0.147 (n=327).
**2026 pilot (in-sample).** removed n=15, excess +0.539, median -1.357, 6 of 15 beat SPY (the mean is OTLK's +19.19; the typical removed trade lost); kept n=80, +0.333.
**Reading.** No evidence that D-064 removed a special group of good or bad trades: the difference is small and the range spans zero. The test cannot rule out a difference of about 0.3 to 0.4 ATR either way (about 300 removed trades from about 300 weeks). Nothing here supports changing D-064. The earlier worry that one pilot trade (OTLK) shows a flaw in the rule is not borne out by the larger sample.
Next: (1) factor work on the primary set in the development years (SPY regime, price setup, C1-C9), every variant logged in `research_docs/CANDIDATES.md` and shown with and without the large episodes; (2) hypothesis file, then the one sealed run.

**Quintiles (development, n=274 each; excess over SPY in ATR).** A1 total dollars: +0.206, +0.166, +0.196, +0.057, +0.153 (Q1 to Q5); top minus bottom -0.053 (range -0.414 to +0.303). A2 smallest buyer dollars: +0.282, +0.226, +0.141, +0.101, +0.028; top minus bottom -0.255 (range -0.667 to +0.173, 87.6% of draws at or below zero). A3 median buyer dollars: +0.193, +0.193, +0.283, +0.070, +0.040; difference -0.153 (range -0.526 to +0.238). R1 median % increase: +0.258, +0.101, +0.113, +0.164, +0.143; difference -0.115 (range -0.487 to +0.243). R2 smallest % increase: +0.284, +0.132, +0.203, +0.126, +0.033; difference -0.251 (range -0.634 to +0.132). Caveats: 'shares owned following' covers one holding line, so percentages can be overstated for people with several accounts; dollar size also reflects company size. Questions asked on this cohort in this entry: 5 measures plus 9 descriptive cuts.
