## Entry 34 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Role check: do 10% owners sit inside the four? Candidates C10 (10% box) and C7 (pure directors) on the development years | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; exploratory
Script `research/canonical/factor_tenpct.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/factor-tenpct.yml`, merged by pull requests (branches `claude/factor-tenpct-20261108`, `claude/factor-tenpct-fix-20261108`). **Numbers below are from run 42498788338** (summary job 128786786920), which reused the events built in run 42496894096 (selection code unchanged between the two runs; events identical to Entry 25: base 9,282, refined 5,829, primary 1,844 + 80). Returns are the Entry 28 results (run 42490748366). D-068 guard: development (entries <= 2020-12-31) plus the 2026 pilot only.
**Defect in the first run, disclosed.** Run 42496894096 looked for the label "10% Owner"; the SEC bulk data writes it as TenPercentOwner, so that run found zero 10%-box buyers. That zero was a label mismatch, not a finding, and is not used. The script now matches the normalised text (TenPercentOwner or 10%) and prints the raw relationship strings. In those strings there is never a space after a comma, so the Entry 32 CEO/CFO matching was not hurt by that; the bulk data does write some combined values without a comma (for example "Director,OfficerOther", 6 buyer entries of 6,363), which the older `split(',')` code reads as a director without the Officer box. The effect on Entry 32 is at most those 6 entries (not rerun).
**How 10% owners are handled by the selection (read from code).** Events are built through `cluster_counts_by_year.usable()`: one reporting owner per filing, that owner ticks Director and/or Officer, and the owner's name must not match the entity pattern (LLC, Capital, Trust, ...). So a pure 10%-owner filer, a fund, or a joint filing can never be one of the four. Check: 0 buyers with neither the Director nor the Officer box among 6,363 buyer entries. What remains is mixed filers (a Director or Officer who also ticks 10% Owner). The rule "four distinct officers or directors" is therefore respected. Not measured here: how many filings the one-reporting-owner rule drops (joint filings).
**Composition (all 1,452 analysed events).** Buyers recognised as 10%-box: 83 of 6,363. Events with at least one 10%-box buyer: 66 (4.5%); two or more: 11; fewer than four buyers without the box (the event needs a mixed filer to reach four): 56. Events with an Other-box buyer: 11. Pure directors per event (0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7): 188, 327, 412, 303, 179, 27, 10, 6.
**Development 2006-2020 (excess over same-window SPY, ATR; n; SPY beside it).** All 1,372: +0.154 (SPY +0.194), reproducing Entry 28. T1 any 10%-box buyer: n=59, stock +0.695, SPY +0.173, excess +0.523 (week-block 95% range -0.065 to +1.119; 52 weeks) vs no box n=1,313 +0.138. T2 fewer than four buyers without the box: n=50, +0.547 (range -0.107 to +1.204). T3 two or more with the box: n=10, +1.016 (too few). By count of 10%-box buyers (0/1/2/3+): +0.138 (1,313), +0.422 (49), +2.021 (6), -0.491 (4). Without the three large episodes: T1 yes n=46 +0.613 vs no +0.296.
**Pure directors (C7).** Development, by count of pure directors (0,1,2,3,4+): +0.138 (179), -0.077 (313), +0.298 (387), +0.023 (284), +0.426 (209). Three or more pure directors n=493 +0.194 vs +0.132 for the rest; zero pure directors (all officers) n=179 +0.138 vs +0.157. Not monotonic. 2026 pilot: three or more pure directors n=32 +0.956 vs n=48 -0.083; this pilot split did not carry over to 2006-2020.
**Chance test (excess shuffled inside the same entry week, 10,000 shuffles, development).** Single-question chance: T1 56.2%; T2 37.6%; T3 31.1%; C7a 73.6%; C7b 90.2%; C7c Spearman +0.023, 42.6%. Best of the six: smallest chance 31.1%, chance the best of six is at least that small by luck 79.0%.
**Reading.** (1) The selection does not let pure 10% owners in; 4.5% of events include a director or officer who also ticks the 10% box. (2) Those events did better in 2006-2020 (+0.523 on n=59), the opposite of the "10% owners carry little signal" literature, but the group is small, the range includes zero, and the shuffle chance is 56%, so it is a weak lead at most; the literature concerns pure 10% holders, who are excluded here by design. (3) The number of pure directors shows no relationship with the result (rho +0.023); the pilot's 3-and-4-director pattern did not replicate. Questions asked on this cohort in this entry: 5 binary splits + 1 rank correlation + 4 descriptive bucket tables.
Next: decline look-backs (C8/C9) on the development years with several windows (21, 63, 126, 252 sessions), raw and stock-minus-SPY, crossed with SPY regime; definitions to be committed in `research_docs/CANDIDATES.md` before the returns are joined.

## Entry 35 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Candidates C11-C13 on the development years: treasurer, controller / accounting officer and other (non-C-suite) officers among the buyers | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; exploratory
Script `research/canonical/factor_officers.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/factor-officers.yml`, branch `claude/factor-officers-20261108` merged as PR #41, run **42498734437** (events job 128786632485, summary job 128789542483). Same event rebuild, returns (Entry 28, run 42490748366) and shuffle test as Entries 32 to 34; events identical to Entry 25 (primary 1,844 + 80). D-068 guard: development (entries <= 2020-12-31) plus the 2026 pilot only. Classes and the five-question menu were written into the script and `research_docs/CANDIDATES.md` before the run. A buyer counts only when his filing ticks Officer. Treasurer = title contains "treasurer" (not "assistant"); controller = controller, comptroller, chief or principal accounting officer, CAO; C-suite other = chief ... officer, COO/CTO/CIO/CMO/CLO/CRO, president (not vice president), chairman; other officer = any other titled officer (VPs, SVPs, EVPs, general counsel, secretary).
**Coverage.** 1,452 events analysed; no officer-buyer has a blank title (0 untitled events). Events with a treasurer 78, a controller 113, at least one other officer 661. Zero assistant treasurers or assistant controllers appear among the buyers, so the "assistant" exclusion changed nothing. Small groups: treasurer 72 development events, controller 111.
**Development 2006-2020 (excess over same-window SPY, ATR; n; SPY).** All 1,372: +0.154 (SPY +0.194). Treasurer among buyers 72: -0.148 (SPY +0.544; week-block 95% range -0.670 to +0.387); no treasurer 1,300: +0.171. Controller among buyers 111: +0.315 (SPY +0.055; range -0.144 to +0.757); no controller 1,261: +0.140. Treasurer or controller 170: +0.162 vs +0.153. Any other officer 634: +0.095 (SPY +0.246; range -0.100 to +0.296) vs none 738: +0.205. By number of other officers 0/1/2/3+: +0.205 (738), +0.122 (396), -0.006 (164), +0.179 (74). Crossed with Entry 32: neither CEO nor CFO with other officer present 160: +0.250; without 218: +0.265; CEO and/or CFO with other officer 474: +0.043; without 520: +0.179.
**Without the three large episodes.** Treasurer +0.031 (55) vs +0.324; controller +0.704 (83, SPY +0.120) vs +0.277; other officer present +0.201 (498) vs none +0.398 (599); treasurer or controller +0.424 vs +0.294.
**Chance test (excess shuffled inside the same entry week, 10,000 shuffles, development).** O1 treasurer -0.319 gap (alone 10.9%); O2 controller +0.175 (31.6%); O3 other officer -0.109 (23.9%); O4 treasurer or controller +0.008 (95.3%); O5 Spearman with number of other officers -0.011 (57.1%). Best of the five: smallest single chance 10.9%, chance the best of five is that small by luck 35.6%.
**2026 pilot (in-sample, tiny).** Treasurer 6: +0.825; controller 2: +0.473; other officer present 27: +0.259 vs none 53: +0.370. Too few to read.
**Reading.** None of the three groups separates from chance. The treasurer group looks worse, but it is 72 events, the range spans zero by a wide margin, and its SPY window is unusually strong (+0.544), so SPY-relative results there depend on a few market episodes; it nearly vanishes without the large episodes (+0.031 vs +0.324, still the wrong side of average). Controller looks better (+0.315, and +0.704 without the large episodes, n=83), but its range includes zero and shuffling gives a gap that size 31.6% of the time. Other officers: slightly worse than clusters without them, and no dose-response (rho -0.011). The most notable cell is "controller present" with SPY only +0.055 over the same windows, which fits a story that these events cluster in quiet markets rather than a controller effect; not tested. C11, C12 and C13 stay leads at most, not supported. Caveats: officer-title matching is by pattern (1,224 distinct title strings; the census is in the run log); "see remarks" titles (63 buyer entries) fall into other officer; one person can carry two flags (CFO and treasurer: 54 entries). Questions asked on this cohort in this entry: 4 binary splits + 1 rank correlation (menu), plus 1 bucket table and 4 crossed cells.
Next: the decline look-backs (C8/C9/C1) already registered in PR #44, then the one hypothesis file.

## Entry 36 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Candidates C8/C9/C1 on the development years: decline look-backs (5, 21, 63, 126, 252 sessions), down-session share, SPY regime at entry | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run logs); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; exploratory (menu declared before the run; section G was asked after seeing the result)
Script `research/canonical/factor_decline.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/factor-decline.yml`, merged by pull requests (branches `claude/factor-decline-20261108`, `claude/factor-decline-confound-20261108`). Menu run: **42499472150** (features jobs per year, summary job 128789430184). Section G (post hoc) run: **42499875799** (summary job 128790094609; reused the stored features of 42499472150, feature code unchanged). Returns are the Entry 28 results (run 42490748366), joined by (issuer, symbol, entry); the development baseline reproduces Entry 28 exactly (n=1,372, +0.154; without the three episodes n=1,097, +0.309; pilot n=80, +0.333). D-068 guard: development plus the 2026 pilot only; features use only sessions strictly before the entry session; no returns are computed in this script.
**Measures (declared before the run).** Windows of 5, 21, 63, 126 and 252 completed sessions. rawW = stock close change over W sessions in entry-ATR units; relW = rawW minus SPY's move restated in the stock's ATR units; spyW = SPY's own percent change (C1 regime); hiW = (last close minus highest close in W sessions) / ATR for W = 21, 63, 126, 252; dnW = share of down-close sessions for W = 21, 63 (C9). 21 measures x 2 statistics (Spearman; 'smile' = outer fifths vs middle three) = 42 statistics in the chance menu. Coverage (development): raw21 1,370; raw63 1,350; raw126 1,329; raw252 1,275; hi252 1,291; dn63 1,357.
**Main result: only the long windows matter.** Spearman of 21-session excess with the measure (Q1 = lowest value = biggest fall), development, single-question chance from shuffling inside the same entry week (10,000 shuffles): raw252 -0.111 (chance 0.0%, fewer than 1 in 10,000); rel252 -0.106 (0.0%); hi252 -0.121 (0.0%); raw126 -0.049 (1.2%); rel126 -0.058 (0.9%); hi126 -0.068 (1.9%). Best of the menu of 42: smallest single chance 0.0%; chance the best of 42 is at least that small by luck 0.0%. Without the three large episodes: raw252 -0.099, rel252 -0.077, hi252 -0.092, raw126 -0.033, hi126 -0.045.
**Quintiles, 12-month stock decline raw252 (excess over SPY, ATR; n; weeks).** Q1 (fell most; cut at -15.3 ATR) +0.593 (255, 163w); Q2 +0.316 (255); Q3 +0.061; Q4 -0.036; Q5 (least fall) +0.015. Without episodes: Q1 +0.595 (214, 147w), Q2 +0.440, Q3 +0.349, Q4 +0.216, Q5 +0.149. Distance below the 12-month high hi252: Q1 +0.568 (258, 171w), Q2 +0.350, Q3 +0.116, Q4 +0.159, Q5 -0.246. rel252 (stock-specific): Q1 +0.440, Q2 +0.363, Q3 +0.187, Q4 -0.018, Q5 -0.023.
**Crossing with the market (raw252, development; all | without episodes).** Stock in the lower half, SPY down over the same 252 sessions: +0.598 (n=279, 93w) | +0.834 (n=187, 78w). Lower half, SPY up: +0.123 (359) | +0.244. Upper half, SPY down: +0.004 (171) | +0.370. Upper half, SPY up: +0.065 (466) | +0.215. rel252 lower half, SPY down: +0.720 (222, 87w) | +0.837.
**Short windows and the other candidates: nothing.** raw5 rho +0.023 (chance 88.3%); raw21 +0.032 (83.9%); raw63 -0.008 (65.9%); rel21 +0.012 (91.1%); hi21 +0.015 (95.6%); C9 down-session share dn21 -0.020 (75.8%), dn63 +0.026 (31.4%); SPY regime spy5 +0.028, spy21 +0.061 (73.6%), spy63 -0.020, spy126 +0.001, spy252 -0.046 (5.2%); the only SPY statistic below 10% is the spy126 smile (+0.327, 5.5%, inside the menu's luck). Fixed bands for raw21 (down >3 ATR / 1 to 3 / within 1 / up >1): +0.103 (713), +0.026 (314), +0.099 (154), +0.562 (189); not significant (Spearman +0.032).
**2026 pilot (in-sample, small; development cut points).** raw252 Spearman -0.153 (n=74), rel252 -0.172, hi252 -0.112, raw63 -0.161; same direction as the development years, small n.
**Section G, post hoc: is it an ATR-scaling or price-level artifact?** No. Both the decline and the excess are divided by the entry ATR, so this was checked directly. In plain percent units (decline in percent vs excess in percent over the hold, no ATR scaling on either side): pct252 vs excess_pct Spearman -0.129 (chance 0.0%); pct126 -0.086 (0.2%); pct63 -0.038 (5.4%); pct21 -0.008 (77.2%). By fifth of the 12-month percent decline: Q1 (fell more than 55%) +7.22% / +0.454 ATR (n=255); Q2 (37% to 55%) +2.61% / +0.417; Q3 -0.15% / +0.007; Q4 -0.30% / +0.051; Q5 +0.83% / +0.019. Daily-range percent of price (ATR / open) is unrelated to the ATR-unit excess (rho +0.026); the long-window relation holds in every tercile of it (pct252 vs excess_pct: low -0.059, middle -0.130, high -0.167) and of entry price (low <= $10.75 -0.151; middle -0.085; high > $22.11 -0.080). The relation is stronger in the volatile and low-priced stocks.
**Reading.** (1) The question 'was the stock down over x days' has a clear answer in this data: over 1 week, 1 month and 3 months, no; over 6 and 12 months, yes, and the 12-month fall is the strongest. The pattern sits in the worst-hit two fifths (fell more than about 37% over a year), is not made by the three large episodes, appears in the pilot, and survives a percent-unit check and tercile checks. It is largest when SPY was also down over the year. (2) Caveats that cut against it: 16.5% of refined events have no price series (Entry 25) and deep fallers are the ones most likely to be missing (delisted, bankrupt), which inflates the deep-fall group; the effect is concentrated in volatile, low-priced stocks where the assumed cost (0.10 to 0.30 ATR, Entry 2, unverified) is a larger share; weeks stand behind each fifth (about 150 to 170) but the same issuers can recur across years (option C allows a new event after 21 sessions); and the 12-month decline is one of 21 measures chosen before looking but after the earlier pilot work. (3) Status: C8 long windows (126, 252) become a lead with real support in 2006-2020, not an edge; C8 short windows and C2's 15-session setup are not supported; C9 and C1 are not supported. Questions asked on this cohort in this entry: 42 statistics in the menu, descriptive quintile, band, crossing and pilot tables, plus the post-hoc section G (8 chance-tested correlations).
Next: (a) survivorship bound: re-run the 12-month result with the no-price events included at assumed outcomes (for example zero and a large loss) to see how much of Q1/Q2 survives; (b) per-issuer repeat check and a cost-realism look at the deep-fall group; (c) alternative 12-month definitions (for example 189 and 315 sessions, drawdown from the 12-month high, time since the 12-month low), each declared first; then C7/other factors and the hypothesis file.

## Entry 37 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Candidates C14-C17 on the development years: market regime at entry (SPY and ONEQ volatility, drawdown, trend, Nasdaq leadership) and the controller crossing | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; exploratory (menu declared before the run; the controller-by-volatility cells in section E were looked at after the menu result and are descriptive)
Script `research/canonical/factor_regime.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/factor-regime.yml`, branch `claude/factor-regime-20261108` merged as PR #49, run **42501167019** (summary job 128794484224; 16 feature jobs by year). Returns are the Entry 28 results (run 42490748366); events with buyers reused from run 42498734437 (Entry 35). D-068 guard: development (entries <= 2020-12-31) plus the 2026 pilot only. Features use only sessions strictly before entry (self-test checks no look-ahead). ONEQ stands in for the Nasdaq Composite (D-009). Coverage: all 7 measures for all 1,372 development events; 898 distinct entry dates, 450 weeks, 820 issuers.
**New chance test.** Market-level measures are almost identical for every trade on a date, so the within-week shuffle of Entries 32 to 35 would be invalid here. Part 1 uses an exact circular shift over the 898 entry dates (each trade is paired with another date's market features, keeping the market's own persistence). The shift test was checked on synthetic data in the sandbox (no signal: chance 43%; planted signal: chance 0.4%); that check is NOT committed, only a determinism self-test is, so the test's calibration is REPORTED, not CONFIRMED. Because regimes last months, the number of independent market episodes behind any result is far smaller than 898.
**Part 1 (14 statistics: 7 measures x Spearman and smile).** Rank correlations are all small: spyvol21 -0.037 (chance 44%), spydd252 -0.022 (61%), spyt200 -0.003 (95%), onvol21 -0.021 (66%), ondd252 -0.030 (51%), ont200 -0.013 (81%), lead63 -0.026 (53%). Smile statistics: onvol21 -0.256 (17%), ont200 +0.261 (19%), spyt200 +0.152 (46%), others 70% to 87%. The one exception is **lead63 smile +0.546, chance 0.6% alone; best of the 14 by luck 4.8%.** Quintiles of lead63 (ONEQ 63-session move minus SPY's; excess over SPY, ATR; n=275 each): Q1 (Nasdaq lagging most) +0.599, Q2 -0.083, Q3 +0.113, Q4 -0.223, Q5 (Nasdaq leading most) +0.364. Without the three large episodes: Q1 +0.666, Q2 +0.033, Q3 +0.174, Q4 +0.124, Q5 +0.545. So both extremes beat the middle, a U-shape, not a trend.
**Volatility and the large episodes.** Full sample, SPY 21-session volatility fifths: +0.265, +0.122, +0.299, +0.133, -0.047 (highest vol worst). Without the three episodes the order reverses: +0.294, +0.137, +0.237, +0.420, +0.456 (highest vol best); ONEQ volatility without episodes +0.101 ... +0.638. The high-stress result flips with the episodes, so no volatility conclusion; neither pattern passes the chance test.
**Trend (descriptive).** SPY above its 200-session average: +0.192 (n=715) vs at or below +0.113 (657); without episodes +0.242 (701) vs +0.427 (396). ONEQ above vs below: +0.154 vs +0.154; without episodes +0.240 (750) vs +0.458 (347). Not in the chance menu except through the continuous t200 measures (rho chance 95% and 81%).
**Part 2 (controller crossing, 111 controller events, 103 dates, 84 weeks).** Percentile-rank gaps, controller minus rest (chance, controller label shuffled within the same entry quarter): spyvol21 +0.044 (92%), spydd252 -0.075 (48%), spyt200 -0.074 (63%), onvol21 +0.051 (86%), ondd252 -0.077 (43%), ont200 -0.065 (62%), lead63 -0.039 (29%). Best of 7 by luck 62.9%. The calm-market prediction (lower volatility, shallower drawdown, higher trend) is NOT supported: controller events sit, if anything, slightly toward higher volatility and weaker trend, all inside chance. Share of events inside the three large episodes: controller 28/111 (25.2%) vs rest 247/1,261 (19.6%).
**Descriptive cells, looked at after the Part 2 result (not in the menu; no chance test run).** Splitting at the median SPY 21-session volatility: calmer half, controller n=48 stock +0.771, SPY -0.136, excess +0.907 (median +1.173) vs rest n=638 excess +0.130; stressed half, controller n=63 excess -0.136 vs rest n=623 +0.150. This is the third slicing of the controller group that looks good (whole group +0.315 in Entry 35; +0.704 without episodes; calm half +0.907), so it is a lead to be tested once with a rule written first, not a finding. Questions asked on this cohort in this entry: 14 statistics + 7 gaps (menu), plus the descriptive tables and these two cells.
**Hold direction (descriptive, NOT tradable).** SPY up during the 21-session hold: n=892, stock +1.136, SPY +0.780, excess +0.356. SPY down or flat: n=480, stock -1.117, SPY -0.896, excess -0.221. Without episodes: up +0.447 (722), down +0.043 (375). The stocks move with the market more than one for one, so most of the raw result is market direction after entry.
**2026 pilot (in-sample, small).** 79 of 80 entries fall in the calmer half by SPY volatility, so the pilot sits in one regime and cannot test the volatility or trend measures. lead63 split at the development median: below +(-0.222) (n=43) vs at or above +0.977 (n=37), consistent with the right arm of the U only.
**Reading.** Market regime at entry does not predict the result through any simple monotone measure (volatility, drawdown, trend, SPY or ONEQ). The one signal is lead63: entries when the Nasdaq had been moving far from SPY in either direction did better than entries when the two had moved together. It is 0.6% alone, 4.8% as the best of 14, holds without the three episodes, and has no obvious mechanism; the extremes may simply pick out a few market episodes (not checked), and with the 40-plus questions this project has now asked, a 4.8% best-of-menu result is a lead, not an edge. The calm-market story for controllers is not supported. Caveats: the shift test is new and its calibration is only checked on synthetic data; market features have few independent episodes; the returns method is PROVISIONAL.
Next: (1) lead63 by year and by episode, and the count of separate market periods behind each extreme fifth, before any other use; (2) one pre-declared test of the controller-by-calm-market cell (rule written first); (3) the hypothesis file.

## Entry 38 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Survivorship check on the 12-month-fall lead (C8): census of events with no price series, break-even table, Q1 breadth | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); exploratory diagnostics, nothing tested
Script `research/canonical/survivorship_check.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/survivorship-check.yml`, branch `survivorship-check` merged as PR #51, run **42501675381** (inputs: events/year files run 42488283630, returns run 42490748366, decline features run 42499472150). Checks were declared in the script docstring before running. Returns come only through `factor_decline.load` (D-068 guard).
**A. Census (refined events, before the $3M floor and before option C).** 5,829 events; 5,706 with a usable ticker; 944 (16.5%) have no price series in the archive. The share is NOT even across years: 2006 42% (92 of 219), 2007 36%, 2008 31% (208 of 670), 2009 24%, 2010 22%, 2011 15%, 2012-2013 about 10-12%, 2014-2020 about 5-9%. 854 of the 944 fall in 2006-2020. Frequent unmatched symbols look like small banks and thinly traded names (GTWN, NSBC, PABK, CMOH, JFBC...) plus some written with parentheses, e.g. (PULB), (CHFC), which is a ticker-cleaning gap on my side and fixable.
**B. Break-even, worst-hit 12-month fifth (n=255, +0.593 ATR).** If missing events were at the equal rate (51 events) averaging 0 / -1 / -2 / -3 / -5 ATR: +0.494 / +0.327 / +0.161 / -0.006 / -0.339. At double the rate (102): +0.423 / +0.138 / -0.148 / -0.434 / -1.005. To fall to the all-event +0.154 would take about 97 missing at -1, 52 at -2, 35 at -3, 22 at -5, 14 at -8. Caveats: the 16.5% overstates the primary-set loss because many missing small names would fail the $3M floor anyway (not measured), but the rate is far higher than average in 2008-2009, exactly where Q1 is concentrated (53 and 33 of 255 events), so the equal-rate row is probably too kind.
**C. Q1 breadth (raw252, development).** Mean +0.593; median +0.634; 62.0% beat SPY; mean without the 5 best +0.478; 10%-trimmed +0.627; 163 weeks. By year (n/mean): 2008 53/+0.36, 2009 33/+1.29, 2015 28/-0.12, 2019 26/+0.69, 2013 5/-1.30; most years positive, two negative. 204 distinct issuers; with the earliest event per issuer +0.635 (n=204), so repeat issuers do not drive it.
**Reading.** The lead is broad (not a few winners, not repeat issuers) but its survivorship exposure is real and concentrated in the crisis years. It survives mild assumptions (missing events about 0 to -2 ATR at the equal rate) and does not survive severe ones (-3 or worse, or double the rate). It is not yet clean.
Next: (1) fix the ticker cleaning (parentheses etc.), rerun the census, and test how many missing events would have passed the $3M floor using volume-free information if any exists; (2) spot-check a sample of 2008-2009 missing names (delisted/acquired) for their actual outcomes; (3) only then alternative long-window definitions, each declared first.

## Entry 39 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Follow-up on the Entry 37 lead (C16, lead63 = ONEQ minus SPY over 63 sessions): by year, separate market periods, leave-out checks, quarter-block range | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; descriptive follow-up on one lead already found in Entry 37
Script `research/canonical/factor_regime.py lead`, workflow `.github/workflows/factor-regime-lead.yml`, branch `claude/regime-lead-20261108` merged as PR #50, run **42501865261** (job 128796161573). Reuses the Entry 37 market features (run 42501167019), Entry 28 returns and the Entry 35 events. Development years only (D-068). Register note: the "before the run" line in `research_docs/CANDIDATES.md` for this follow-up was cut off mid-sentence by a shell formatting error when it was written; the rule is in the script and PR #50, and the register line is corrected in this entry's pull request.
**Reproduced.** Same cut points as Entry 37 (lowest fifth of lead63 <= -0.0176, highest fifth >= +0.0391). Q1 (Nasdaq lagging SPY most): n=275, stock +0.880, SPY +0.281, excess +0.599. Q5 (Nasdaq leading most): n=275, stock +0.718, SPY +0.354, excess +0.364. Middle three fifths: n=822, stock +0.046, SPY +0.111, excess -0.065. Smile = extremes +0.481 (n=550) minus middle -0.065 = **+0.546**, as in Entry 37.
**Separate market periods ("spells": extreme-fifth entry dates with no gap over 42 calendar days).** Q1: 275 events in 16 spells; 13 of 16 spells have a positive mean excess; each spell counted once: mean +0.633, median +0.698. Largest spells: 2008-10-15 to 2009-01-07 (57 events, +0.306), 2018-10-25 to 2019-01-02 (49, +0.593), 2008-01-11 to 2008-04-02 (47, +0.838), 2016-01-28 to 2016-04-28 (37, +0.787), 2014-05-01 to 2014-06-17 (22, +0.776), 2006-05-11 to 2006-08-25 (20, +0.763), 2012-11-14 to 2012-12-20 (19, -0.118). The three largest hold 56% of Q1 events (+0.561); the other 13 spells +0.646. Q5: 275 events in 18 spells; 11 of 18 positive; spell means mean +0.604 but median +0.190. Largest: 2020-02-05 to 2020-09-11 (122 events, +0.171), 2009-02-12 to 2009-05-07 (44, +1.370), 2008-05-27 to 2008-08-25 (33, -0.359). The three largest hold 72% of Q5 events (+0.348).
**By entry year.** Q1 is positive in most years with entries (2006 +0.64, 2007 +1.85 on 4, 2008 +0.53 on 103, 2014 +0.78, 2016 +0.79, 2018 +0.55; 2012 -0.07 on 23 the one clear negative; 2015, 2017 and 2020 have none). Q5 is mixed: 2009 +1.37 (44), 2018 +1.22 (7), 2020 +0.22 (123); negative in 2008 (-0.36 on 33), 2006, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2017. The middle fifths are weakest in 2020 (-1.36 on 66) and 2008 (-0.59 on 27).
**Leave-out checks on the smile (+0.546).** Leaving out any single year: +0.469 (2009) to +0.616 (2019). Leaving out the three large episodes: +0.538. Leaving out the three largest Q1 spells: +0.515; the three largest Q5 spells: +0.622; the three largest of both: +0.618.
**Quarter-block bootstrap (60 quarters, 10,000 resamples).** Smile +0.546, 95% range +0.251 to +0.818 (0.0% of resamples at or below zero). Q1 mean +0.599 (+0.382 to +0.834); Q5 mean +0.364 (-0.091 to +0.930); middle -0.065 (-0.349 to +0.214). Q1 minus middle at or below zero in 0.0% of resamples; Q5 minus middle in 3.3%.
**Reading.** The lead survives the question it was built to answer: it is not two or three episodes. The Q1 arm (Nasdaq lagging SPY by about 1.8 points or more over 63 sessions) is the sturdy one: 16 separate periods, 13 positive, spread over most years, unchanged without its largest spells. The Q5 arm (Nasdaq leading) is weaker: its range includes zero, 11 of 18 spells positive, and it leans on 2009 and 2020. So the U-shape is mostly one-sided plus a weak middle. Cautions: the 14-statistic best-of-menu chance was 4.8% (Entry 37) and this follow-up is a second look at the same data, so it does not add independent confirmation; the fifths' cut points are in-sample; 16 periods is the real sample for Q1; the mechanism is unknown and Q1 periods may coincide with the 12-month stock declines of Entry 36 (not yet crossed); in the 2026 pilot the median split points the other way (below median -0.222, n=43), but the pilot sits in one regime and is too small to count. Status: C16 stays a lead, now with a clear shape (Nasdaq-lagging periods), not an edge. The sealed years would hold only a few separate periods, so they can confirm little.
Next: (1) cross lead63 Q1 with the Entry 36 12-month decline measures (is it the same effect?); (2) decide whether lead63 Q1 is one of the 1 to 3 hypotheses for the sealed run, or recorded as a descriptive lead only; (3) the pre-declared controller calm-market cell.

## Entry 40 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Why 854 development-year events have no price series: ticker formatting vs real archive gaps | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run logs); diagnostics only, no returns
Scripts `research/canonical/missing_names_check.py` (run **42502228005**, workflow `missing-names-check.yml`, PR merged from branch `missing-names-check`) and `research/canonical/archive_probe.py` (run **42502351415**, workflow `archive-probe.yml`, branch `archive-probe`). Checks declared in each docstring before running. Archive symbols were sampled on about every 10th session of each year, so a name that traded only briefly could be missed.
**Formatting is a minor cause.** Of 854 no-price refined events (2006-2020), 25 (2.9%) are recoverable by mechanical cleaning: 3 by stripping punctuation, 22 by stripping punctuation and adding the -DELISTED suffix (e.g. (PULB) -> PULB-DELISTED, (CHFC), (PBKS), (BSX)). 829 stay missing. Raw shapes: 4-letter 611, 3-letter 173, parenthesised 26. One more cleaning error seen: 'LEE ENT' is Lee Enterprises, whose symbol LEE is in the archive on all sampled sessions.
**Real archive gaps, including survivors.** Probe, 2008 vs 2012: C (Citigroup, event 2008-11-17) is absent on 0/27 sampled 2008 sessions but present on 26/26 in 2012, so the archive has holes in 2008 even for a stock that kept trading. MI, ALD, CVH, STKR, CTT, EGR, MBP, PRM, MIG, ILI, AFT, PABK, NSBC are absent in both years, with no plain or -DELISTED symbol under their names, although several (Marshall & Ilsley, Allied Capital, Coventry Health) were large listed companies that were later acquired. So the missing names are a mix of delisted names the archive never stored and listed names it skipped; this is not random noise and it is concentrated in 2006-2009 (89, 166, 201, 83 still-missing events).
**Consequence.** The Entry 38 break-even stands and the equal-rate row stays too kind for 2008-2009. This cannot be repaired inside the archive; it needs either a second price source for the missing names or an explicit bound. The WaMu / Bear Stearns archive question (set aside earlier) is the same problem.
Next: decision needed from Michael (options in chat): (a) accept the bound and carry 'survivorship-exposed' as a standing caveat on C8; (b) test C8 on 2011-2020 only, where the missing rate is 5-15%; (c) find a second source for the 2006-2010 missing names and re-price them.


## Entry 41 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Cross of the two live leads: C16 lead63 Q1 (Nasdaq lagging SPY) with the C8 12-month decline measures | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; exploratory (menu declared in the script docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md before the run; section B/C readings below are descriptive)
Script `research/canonical/factor_cross.py cross`, workflow `.github/workflows/factor-cross.yml`, branch `claude/cross-lead63-decline-20261108` merged as PR #59, run **42502640234** (job 128798525429, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42502640234). Inputs reused, no new features or returns: decline features run 42499472150 (Entry 36), regime features run 42501167019 (Entry 37), returns run 42490748366 (Entry 28). D-068 guard: development entries <= 2020-12-31 only (`factor_decline.load` asserts it); the 2026 pilot is not used. Excess = stock minus SPY over the same 21 sessions in the stock's ATR units; the run printed excess only, not the stock and SPY legs separately (D-053 gap: benchmark is built into the number but not shown beside it; add the legs in the next run).
**Cohort differs from Entry 39.** 1,273 development events have lead63 and all four decline measures (1,372 in Entry 39). The 99 missing lack a decline measure (reason not checked; likely too little price history before entry). The lead63 fifths are recomputed on the 1,273: Q1 <= -0.0195 (Entry 39: -0.0176), Q5 >= +0.0391 (same). Q1 n=255, excess +0.596 (Entry 39: +0.599). All development 1,273: +0.189.
**A. Overlap: the two leads pick different events.** Spearman(lead63, decline measure): raw252 -0.009, rel252 -0.015, hi252 +0.031, raw126 -0.007. Share of lead63-Q1 events inside the fell-most fifth (20% if unrelated): raw252 20.4%, rel252 16.9%, hi252 20.0%, raw126 24.7%.
**B. Decline group x lead63 band, raw252 (excess over SPY, ATR; n / entry weeks / separate entry spells).** Fell most: Q1 +1.434 (52 / 30 / 8), middle band +0.194 (148 / 109 / 41), Q5 +0.871 (55 / 28 / 11). Middle decline: Q1 +0.426 (158 / 61 / 14), middle band -0.102 (428 / 207 / 33), Q5 +0.352 (177 / 56 / 17). Fell least: Q1 +0.227 (45 / 31 / 11), middle band -0.018 (183 / 136 / 35), Q5 -0.121 (27 / 24 / 12). rel252, hi252, raw126 give the same shape (fell-most x Q1: +1.320, +1.297, +0.921).
**C. Within-band decline relation, Spearman(decline, excess), negative = deeper fall better.** raw252: Q1 -0.176 (n=255), middle band -0.041 (n=759), Q5 -0.202 (n=259); rel252 -0.196 / -0.062 / -0.172; hi252 -0.238 / -0.039 / -0.225; raw126 -0.142 / +0.038 / -0.207. Inside the fell-most fifth (raw252): Q1 +1.434 (52 events, 8 spells) vs not-Q1 +0.377 (203, 41 spells). Among all other events: Q1 +0.382 (203, 15 spells) vs not-Q1 +0.015 (815, 24 spells).
**D. Regression (excess = a + b*fall + c*Q1 + e*Q5; fall 0 = fell least, 1 = fell most; quarter-block 95% range, 60 quarters).** raw252: decline alone fall +0.748 [+0.097, +1.310]; lead63 alone Q1 +0.620 [+0.241, +1.010], Q5 +0.437 [-0.022, +0.942]; both: fall +0.674 [+0.053, +1.219], Q1 +0.591 [+0.213, +0.995], Q5 +0.390 [-0.030, +0.864]. Each coefficient shrinks only 5-10% when the other is in the model. rel252 and hi252 similar (hi252 both: fall +0.849 [+0.039, +1.561], Q1 +0.593). raw126: fall +0.346 [-0.402, +1.044] in the joint model, range includes zero. Chance (exact circular shift of lead63 over 845 entry dates, decline held fixed, alone, not corrected): Q1 coefficient lead63 alone +0.620, chance 1.8%; with raw252 in the model +0.591, chance 2.0%. The Q1 chance of 1.8% to 2.0% is the same data and the same statistic family as Entry 37 (best-of-14 there: 4.8%), so it is not independent confirmation. The shift test code in this script is new; it was checked only on noise in the sandbox (chance about 39%, not committed), so its calibration is REPORTED, not CONFIRMED.
**E. Without the three large episodes (n=1,010, 57 quarters).** Fell-most x Q1 +1.885 (29 events, 7 spells). Joint model: Q1 +0.550 [+0.208, +0.885] holds; fall +0.523 [-0.171, +1.092], range includes zero.
**Reading.** (1) The two leads are not the same events and not the same effect in a linear sense: near-zero overlap, and each coefficient survives the other being in the model. (2) They are not cleanly separable either: the decline relation is weak in the middle three fifths of lead63 (Spearman -0.04 to +0.04 in three of four measures, 759 events, about 60% of the sample) and strong in the two extreme bands, and fell-most events in the middle band earn only +0.194, about the all-event mean. The regression has no interaction term (not in the declared menu), so this is a reading of declared table cells, not a tested interaction. (3) The best cell (fell most and Nasdaq lagging, +1.434) rests on 8 separate entry spells and 30 entry weeks; the real sample for any cross is a handful of market periods. (4) Without the three large episodes the lead63 Q1 coefficient is intact (range +0.208 to +0.885) while the decline coefficient's range includes zero, so Q1 is the sturdier of the two in this test; the Entry 36 decline result used a different (weekly shuffle) test and still stands as stated there. (5) The decline lead keeps the survivorship exposure of Entries 38 and 40: deep fallers are the likeliest to be missing from the price archive, and 2008-2009 holes sit exactly where the fell-most x Q1 cell is concentrated (not measured here). (6) Costs bite harder in this cell (volatile, low-priced names); not measured. Status: C16 (Nasdaq-lagging periods) and C8 (12-month decline) both remain leads, now known to be largely separate selections of events; neither is an edge. Nothing here is sealed-year evidence: no returns for 2021-2025 were read.
Next (recommended): decide Entry 40's open question first, because it governs how far C8 and this cross can be trusted. Recommendation: (b), re-run the cross and the Entry 36 decline result on 2011-2020 only, where the missing-price rate is 5-15%, as a descriptive check with existing artifacts and no new data; keep (a), the survivorship caveat, standing regardless. Then (2) decide whether lead63 Q1 is a candidate for the 1-3 hypotheses of the sealed run or stays descriptive; the sealed years would hold only a few separate Nasdaq-lagging periods, so they can confirm little. (3) The pre-declared controller calm-market cell is still untested.


## Entry 42 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Entry 40 option (b), chosen by Michael: the Entry 41 cross and the Entry 36 decline result repeated on development entries from 2011-01-01 only (missing-price share 5-15%) | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; survivorship-exposure check, menu declared before the run in the `factor_cross.py` docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md
Script `research/canonical/factor_cross.py cross ... --start 2011-01-01` (new `--start` option and section F added in PR #61), workflow `.github/workflows/factor-cross.yml` (new `start` input), branch `claude/cross-2011-2020-20261108` merged as PR #61, run **42503853623** (job 128802139423, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42503853623). Inputs as Entry 41 (runs 42499472150, 42501167019, 42490748366). D-068 guard: development entries <= 2020-12-31 only; no 2021-2025 return was read; pilot not used. Lead63 and decline cut points are recomputed on the subset: lead63 Q1 is now <= -0.0075 (Entry 41: -0.0195), Q5 >= +0.0377, so "Q1" here means a milder Nasdaq lag and is not the same group as in Entries 39 and 41. Stock and SPY legs are now printed beside every excess (ATR), closing the D-053 gap noted in Entry 41.
**Subset.** 872 events (of the 1,273 in Entry 41), 288 entry weeks. All events: excess +0.095 (stock +0.360, SPY +0.265).
**F. The Entry 36 decline result holds in this subset.** Spearman of 21-session excess with the measure (chance = excess shuffled inside the same entry week, 10,000 draws, as Entry 36): raw252 -0.125 (chance 0.00%), rel252 -0.117 (0.00%), hi252 -0.127 (0.03%); Entry 36, all years: -0.111, -0.106, -0.121. Fifth that fell most over 12 months (raw252): n=175, 117 weeks, 33 spells, excess +0.565 (stock +0.823, SPY +0.258) vs fell-least fifth n=175 excess -0.128 (stock +0.056, SPY +0.184). rel252 fell-most +0.398 vs -0.199; hi252 +0.452 vs -0.506. The pattern is not confined to 2006-2010, where 22-42% of events had no price series.
**D. Regression, raw252 (excess; quarter-block range; 40 quarters).** Decline alone fall +0.923 [+0.347, +1.472]; lead63 alone Q1 +0.598 [+0.156, +1.002]; both: fall +0.913 [+0.370, +1.461], Q1 +0.589 [+0.172, +0.985]. rel252 and hi252 similar (hi252 both: fall +1.100 [+0.222, +1.888]); raw126 fall +0.358 [-0.420, +1.190], range includes zero. Chance for the Q1 coefficient (circular shift over 564 entry dates, decline fixed, alone, not corrected): 6.4% alone and 6.4% with raw252 in the model (Entry 41, all years: 1.8% and 2.0%).
**E. Without the three large episodes (n=675, 38 quarters).** Fall +0.829 [+0.233, +1.380] (excludes zero here; in Entry 41 all years it included zero), Q1 +0.426 [+0.045, +0.806] (lower end barely above zero).
**A-C. Cross.** Overlap is again near nil: Spearman(lead63, decline) +0.022 to +0.100; lead63-Q1 events in the fell-most fifth 19.4% to 22.3%. Decline group x lead63 band, raw252 (excess; stock / SPY; n, weeks, spells): fell most x Q1 +1.292 (stock +1.542, SPY +0.250; 39, 31, 13); fell most x middle band +0.247 (+0.473 / +0.226; 115, 79, 28); middle x middle band -0.117 (288, 136, 24); fell least x middle band -0.229 (119, 85, 25). Within-band Spearman(raw252, excess): Q1 -0.205, middle -0.074, Q5 -0.219. rel252, hi252, raw126 give the same shape.
**Reading.** (1) Survivorship does not look like the sole driver of the decline lead: it is as strong or stronger where most price series exist (rho about -0.12, fell-most fifth +0.565 over SPY +0.258 in the stock's ATR units). It is not cleared: 5-15% of events are still missing, the missing names are probably the deepest fallers, and no bound was computed for this subset. (2) The Entry 41 ranking of the two leads is not stable. All years: Nasdaq-lagging was the sturdier lead without the episodes. 2011-2020: the decline lead is the sturdier (its range excludes zero without episodes, the Q1 shift chance is 6.4% and its no-episode range barely clears zero). Treat the two as comparably fragile leads, not as a ranked pair. (3) The Entry 41 reading that the decline relation is nearly absent in the middle lead63 band is weaker here: in 2011-2020 the middle band has a gradient (+0.247, -0.117, -0.229 from fell most to fell least), smaller than inside Q1 or Q5. (4) The best cell is less thin than in Entry 41 (13 spells, not 8) but the Q1 definition is milder, so the cells are not comparable. (5) Costs in volatile, low-priced names are still unmeasured. Status: C8 stays a lead, now with the survivorship check partly passed; C16 stays a lead, weaker in this subset; neither is an edge.
Next (recommended): (1) a survivorship bound for the 2011-2020 fell-most fifth in the Entry 38 style, using the Entry 38 census rates (2011 15%, 2012-2013 about 10-12%, 2014-2020 about 5-9%): how many missing events at what assumed outcome would erase +0.565; the existing `survivorship_check.py` can be given a start date. It fits next because it answers the largest remaining threat to C8 with data already in hand. (2) Cost realism for the deep-fall group (spread and slippage in ATR for volatile, low-priced names). (3) Alternative long-window definitions, each declared first. (4) Hypothesis-file decision stays open; Michael is not ready for the sealed run.

## Entry 44 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Survivorship bound for the 2011-2020 fell-most fifth (Entry 42 next-step 1): year-matched break-even using the Entry 38 census rates | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; exploratory diagnostics, nothing tested (numbered 44 because another chat's workflow run is labelled Entry 43; renumber if needed)
Script `research/canonical/survivorship_check.py` (new optional `start` argument, sections D and E, menu declared in the docstring before the run), workflow `.github/workflows/survivorship-check.yml` (new `start` input), branches `claude/survivorship-2011-2020-20261108` (PR #63) and `claude/survivorship-yamlfix-20261108` (YAML fix, the first merge put the input in the wrong block), run **42504640557** (job 128804517909, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42504640557), `start=2011-01-01`. Inputs as Entry 38 (runs 42488283630, 42490748366, 42499472150). D-068 guard via `factor_decline.load`; no 2021-2025 return read. The fifth reproduces Entry 42: n=175, +0.565 ATR (SPY same window +0.258 in Entry 42; this run printed the excess only). Subset 874 events (Entry 42 printed 872; I did not trace the 2-event difference), all-event mean +0.096.
**D. Year-matched break-even.** Census no-price shares by entry year (share of usable-ticker refined events): 2011 14.8%, 2012 12.5%, 2013 11.0%, 2014 9.8%, 2015 12.7%, 2016 9.9%, 2017 8.5%, 2018 8.1%, 2019 7.1%, 2020 5.3%. Expected missing events matched to the fifth's own years: 18.6 (1x), 37.2 (2x). Mean of the fifth if those missing events averaged X = 0 / -1 / -2 / -3 / -5 ATR: 1x +0.511 / +0.415 / +0.319 / +0.222 / +0.030; 2x +0.466 / +0.291 / +0.115 / -0.060 / -0.411. Missing events needed (each at X = -1 / -2 / -3 / -5 / -8) to pull the fifth to the subset all-event mean +0.096: 75 / 39 / 27 / 16 / 10; to zero: 99 / 49 / 33 / 20 / 12.
**E. Concentrated scenario (declared as a stress case).** If ALL 92 expected missing events of the whole subset were fell-most names: +0.370 / +0.024 / -0.321 / -0.667 / -1.358 at X = 0 / -1 / -2 / -3 / -5.
**C (same fifth).** Median +0.612; 60.6% beat SPY; mean without 5 best +0.396; 10%-trimmed +0.554; 117 weeks; 135 distinct issuers, earliest event per issuer +0.711. By year (n/mean): 2011 8/+0.66, 2012 14/+1.13, 2013 7/-0.66, 2014 11/+0.43, 2015 31/-0.03, 2016 20/+0.45, 2017 10/+0.02, 2018 23/+0.95, 2019 32/+0.99, 2020 19/+0.84.
**Reading.** (1) At the year-matched rate the lead survives losses of 0 to -5 ATR on the missing events (+0.03 even at -5, 1x) and survives -2 at double the rate (+0.115); it fails at double the rate with -3 or worse. (2) The stress case, where the whole subset's missing events sit in the fell-most fifth, erases it at about -1 ATR. That case is deliberately extreme (missing events are spread over all fifths, though the deepest fallers are probably over-represented). Truth lies between the two; no direct measure of the missing events' outcomes exists. (3) The Entry 38 spot-check of named missing names (delisted/acquired outcomes) is still the only way to narrow it. (4) The by-year row shows 2013, 2015 and 2017 near or below zero: the fifth is positive in 7 of 10 years but not uniformly. (5) Costs in volatile, low-priced names are still unmeasured and are not in any number here. Status: C8 stays a lead; survivorship is bounded under mild assumptions, not cleared.
Next (recommended): (1) spot-check a sample of the missing 2011-2020 names (delisted/acquired) for actual outcomes using an outside source Michael can supply or a source reachable here, because it is the only step that replaces an assumed X with a measured one; (2) cost realism for the deep-fall group (spread and slippage in ATR); (3) alternative long-window definitions, each declared first. The sealed run stays closed.

## Entry 43 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Longer holds of 63, 126, 189 and 252 sessions (3, 6, 9, 12 months) for the C8 12-month-fall and C16 lead63-Q1 leads, development years | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run logs); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (asked by Michael after the 21-session results; menu declared in the script docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md before any long-hold return was computed; sections G and H added after the first run showed a corrupt row)

**Data problem found and handled.** At 189 and 252 sessions the unclipped mean excess of ALL and NEITHER is about +5,000 ATR. Cause: one row, SUNE (SunEdison), entry 2015-08-26: the exit price comes from a different company that reused the ticker after the bankruptcy (stock +174,086,278% at 189 sessions). It is in neither the fell-most fifth nor Q1, so FELL, Q1, BOTH and EITHER are unaffected; ALL and NEITHER at 189 and 252 unclipped are INVALID and the clipped means (section H) or medians must be used. This is the known ticker-reuse identity gap (Entry 38/40 follow-ups); other rows in the extreme lists look genuine (OSTK, APPS, MTDR, AMG). No row was removed from any table.
**A. Census.** Trades whose stock stopped trading inside the hold: 0.4% at 63, 0.9% at 126, 1.6% at 189, 2.7% at 252, the same rate inside the fell-most fifth as outside it. Complete-only and complete+incomplete tables are nearly identical. Long holds themselves add little survivorship loss; the archive gaps of Entries 38 and 40 remain.
**B. Excess over SPY in ATR (stock / SPY legs); percent legs; share beating SPY.** Fell-most fifth (FELL, n=255): 21 sessions +0.593 (+0.814 / +0.221), 62% beat; 63 +1.160 (+1.693 / +0.533), stock +19.5% vs SPY +4.2%, median excess +5.7%, 59%; 126 +1.500 (+2.130 / +0.630), +33.7% vs +6.8%, median +5.3%, 57%; 189 +2.225 (+3.192 / +0.968), +47.7% vs +10.4%, median +10.6%, 57%; 252 +2.563 (+4.159 / +1.596), +61.1% vs +15.2%, median +12.1%, 57%. Means sit well above medians (skew from large winners; clipped at the 1st/99th percentile: +1.073, +1.149, +1.935, +2.293). Nasdaq-lagging (Q1, n=255): +0.596, +0.595, +0.686, +0.787, +0.659 (252 sessions: stock +2.512, SPY +1.853, +32.3% vs +9.7%, median excess +4.1%). BOTH (n=52, 8 spells): +1.434, +1.803, +2.804, +3.561, +4.037. EITHER (n=458): +0.499, +0.772, +0.895, +1.264, +1.332. FELL minus fell-least fifth: LEAST is +0.015, -0.226, +0.274, -0.492, -0.594, so the gap widens from 0.58 to 3.16. The rest of the base-rule events (NEITHER, clipped): +0.035, -0.204, -0.312, -0.634, -0.642 (median percent excess -3.6% at 189, -5.1% at 252): the unfiltered rule has no edge at long holds (ALL clipped +0.132, +0.049, -0.002, +0.023).
**Ranges (10,000 draws; quarter-block / calendar-year-block).** FELL: 63 [+0.297, +2.012] / [+0.139, +2.127]; 126 [-0.177, +2.952] / [-0.709, +3.343]; 189 [+0.135, +4.048] / [-0.277, +4.469]; 252 [+0.077, +4.766] / [-0.453, +5.198]. BOTH: 63 [+0.791, +3.957] / [+1.289, +3.927]; 126 and longer include zero (7 quarters, 5 years behind it). Ranges widen sharply with the hold and, on year blocks, include zero for FELL from 126 sessions on.
**D. Constant cohort (events complete at all horizons, n=1,239; increments between horizons).** FELL +0.593, +1.213, +1.608, +2.222, +2.563 (increments after the first month +0.620, +0.395, +0.614, +0.341: it keeps accumulating). Q1 +0.615, +0.572, +0.626, +0.642, +0.659 (increments about zero after month 1: the Nasdaq-lagging effect is a one-month effect that does not grow). BOTH +1.438, +1.774, +2.727, +3.329, +4.037.
**E. Chance (Spearman raw252 vs excess, excess shuffled inside the same entry week, 10,000 draws; complete only).** 21 sessions rho -0.112 (0.00%); 63 -0.092 (0.04%); 126 -0.068 (3.38%); 189 -0.103 (8.05%); 252 -0.115 (1.51%). With incomplete trades added: 0.00%, 0.09%, 4.36%, 10.84%, 2.36%. CAUTION: a week-level shuffle treats weeks as independent, which is not true for 126-252 session holds that overlap almost completely, so the long-hold chances are too optimistic; the block ranges above are the more honest guide.
**F. Trade counts (15 development years).** ALL 84.9 entries/yr (7.1/month); FELL 17.0/yr (1.4/month); Q1 17.0/yr; BOTH 3.5/yr (0.3/month); EITHER 30.5/yr (2.5/month). Average open positions at a 252-session hold: ALL 85, EITHER 31, FELL 17, BOTH 3.5; at 63 sessions: EITHER 7.6, FELL 4.2.
**Reading.** (1) The 12-month-fall lead does not fade with time: the excess keeps growing through 12 months (about +0.4 to +0.6 ATR per added quarter on a constant cohort), the spread against the fell-least fifth widens, and the fell-most fifth stays ahead of SPY in about 57% of trades. (2) The Nasdaq-lagging lead is a one-month effect: it stops adding after the first month, so holding longer only adds market exposure. (3) The unfiltered rule earns nothing at long holds; all of the pay is in the two filters. (4) Cautions: long holds from 2008-2009 and 2020 entries capture the rebounds that followed (the survivorship-exposed years of Entries 38, 40 and 44); episode-excluded and 2011-2020-only versions were NOT run; independent observations are far fewer than n (44 spells for FELL, 8 for BOTH); the mean is skewed by a few large winners (OSTK, APPS and similar); costs are not measured (an assumed 0.10 to 0.30 ATR round trip per Entry 2 would be a small part of a 1.2 to 2.6 ATR excess at long holds but is unverified); capital tied up grows in proportion to the hold. Status: C8 stays a lead, stronger in size but wider in uncertainty at long holds; C16 stays a lead at one month only; neither is an edge. Nothing here is sealed-year evidence.
Next (recommended): re-run sections B, D and E with the three large episodes removed and on entries from 2011-01-01 only (existing artifacts, `--start` style option, menu declared first), because the long-hold gain is the most exposed to the 2009 and 2020 rebounds and to the 2006-2010 archive gaps; then decide whether the 1 to 3 hypotheses for the sealed run should name a hold length.

## Entry 45 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Cost realism for the C8 fell-most fifth (Entry 44 next-step 2): tradability profile and assumed percent-of-price costs converted to ATR units | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; every COST is an ASSUMPTION (no bid/ask data exists in the repo or archive); exploratory, menu declared in the script docstring before the run
Script `research/canonical/cost_realism.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/cost-realism.yml`, branches `claude/cost-realism-20261108` (PR #69) and `claude/cost-realism-fix-20261108` (fix: first run 42506167993 failed on KeyError `stock_pct` because the Entry 28 returns files carry `stock_atr`/`spy_atr`, not percent legs; its partial output matched the final run), final run **42506287640** (job 128809527140, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42506287640). Inputs: returns run 42490748366, decline features 42499472150. D-068 guard via `factor_decline.load`; development entries only; no archive read. The fifth reproduces Entries 42 and 36: (i) entries >= 2011-01-01 n=175, +0.565 (stock +0.823, SPY +0.258); (ii) all development years n=255, +0.593 (stock +0.814, SPY +0.221).
**A. Profile, 2011-2020 fell-most fifth vs the other four fifths.** Median entry price $12.02 vs $16.49; share under $5 13.7% vs 7.2%, under $10 44.0% vs 25.3%; ATR as % of price median 6% vs 5% (quartiles 4-9% vs 3-9%); 252-session change median -58% vs -19%, 68.6% of the fifth down more than 50% (quartiles -70% to -46%).
**B/C/E. Flat assumed round-trip cost (2011-2020 fifth; all-years in brackets).** Mean cost in ATR / mean net: 0.5% 0.095 / +0.470 [+0.511]; 1% 0.191 / +0.375 [+0.429]; 2% 0.381 / +0.184 [+0.265]; 3% 0.572 / -0.006 [+0.101]; 5% 0.953 / -0.388 [-0.226]. Break-even flat cost 2.97% of price [3.62%]. At the Entry 2 flat 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.30 ATR: +0.465 / +0.365 / +0.265 [+0.493 / +0.393 / +0.293]. The other four fifths are negative at every cost level (mean excess -0.021, so they never cover a cost).
**D. Price-tiered assumed cost (>= $20 0.5%; $10-20 1%; $5-10 2%; < $5 4%; ASSUMED).** 2011-2020 fifth, x1: stock +0.823, SPY +0.258, excess +0.565, cost 0.246, NET +0.320; net > 0 in 58.3%; net without 5 best +0.153; median +0.330. Doubled table: NET +0.074, 51.4% positive, without 5 best -0.092. All years: x1 NET +0.360 (without 5 best +0.247), x2 +0.128 (+0.016). By price band, 2011-2020 (n / excess / net at x1): < $5 24 / +1.26 / +0.81; $5-10 53 / +0.83 / +0.52; $10-20 51 / +0.60 / +0.41; >= $20 47 / -0.12 / -0.26. All years: < $5 49 / +1.29 / +0.94; $5-10 76 / +0.34 / +0.05; $10-20 76 / +0.78 / +0.61; >= $20 54 / +0.04 / -0.09.
**Reading.** (1) The lead's excess is concentrated in low-priced names, which are exactly where the assumed costs are largest; the >= $20 names in the fifth earn about zero before costs (-0.12, n=47). So the cost question and the "which stocks" question are the same question. (2) Under a flat cost the lead survives up to roughly 3% of price round trip; under the tiered assumption it keeps about +0.32 (2011-2020), and at double the tiered costs it is about zero (+0.07, and -0.09 without the 5 best trades). (3) What is NOT known: the true spread and slippage of these names (no quotes), position size and market impact (not modelled), borrow/halts/limit orders on volatile names, and the missing-price names of Entries 38 and 44 (survivorship and cost stack: at the census rate and -2 ATR for the missing the 2011-2020 fifth was +0.319 BEFORE costs, so after the x1 tiered cost it would be near +0.07). (4) The price-band split is a descriptive cut chosen to expose where cost bites, not a tested factor, and the < $5 band alone is n=24 (2011-2020). Status: C8 stays a lead; with costs and survivorship stacked at mild assumptions the margin is thin; not an edge.
Next (recommended): (1) get any real cost evidence, for example historical quoted spreads for a sample of these names, or a paper-trading record of fills in this kind of stock; without it the cost axis stays an assumption. (2) Test a pre-declared price floor (for example >= $5) as a rule written first, since it removes the highest-cost names; note it also removes the best-performing band, so it is a trade-off to declare before running. (3) Alternative long-window definitions, each declared first. The sealed run stays closed.

(2) The Entry 43 headline that the C8 excess keeps growing to +2.2 / +2.6 ATR at 9 and 12 months rests mostly on the three large episodes (2008Q4, 2011Q3, 2020Q1). Without them the 12-month figure is about +1.1 (S1) and about +0.04 (S3, 2011-2020 without 2011Q3 and 2020Q1), the median is near zero or negative from 126 sessions, and every range from 126 sessions includes zero. (3) At 63 sessions C8 holds in S1 (quarter-block range excludes zero, year-block just includes it) and weakens in S2 and S3 (ranges include zero). (4) C16 Q1 holds at 21 and 63 sessions and is negative at 126 and longer once episodes and early years are out; it was only ever a one-month lead. Status: C8 and C16 remain leads at one month only; no longer-hold lead survives. Nothing here is sealed-year evidence.
Next (recommended): keep the 21-session hold as the working rule and do not add a longer hold to the hypothesis file; continue with Entry 45's cost realism, then alternative 12-month-fall definitions (for example 189 and 315 sessions), each declared first, because cost and definition are the two untested weak points of the one-month lead.

**Per 21 sessions of capital tied up (mean excess / (hold/21)), FELL.** FULL +0.593 / +0.567 / +0.387; S1 +0.595 / +0.566 / +0.364; S2 +0.552 / +0.319 / +0.219; S3 +0.598 / +0.272 / +0.126. Q1: FULL +0.596 / +0.419 / +0.198. The 21-session hold is the best per unit of capital in every set.
**Reading.** (1) Unlike 126+ sessions (Entry 46), the 21 to 42 gain does not depend on the three large episodes: S1 FELL +1.131 vs FULL +1.135, and the S1 ranges at 42 sessions exclude zero on both block types. The extra month adds about +0.5 ATR and nothing after that (63 adds +0.03 or less). (2) The gain does depend on the early years: in 2011-2020 (S2) the 42-session mean is only +0.64 and the year-block range includes zero, and in S3 it is +0.54 with ranges that include zero, i.e. no better than 21 sessions. 2006-2010 holds the most missing prices (Entries 38, 40), so that part of the sample is the least trustworthy. (3) Q1 behaves the same way: best at 42 sessions in FULL and S1, no gain in 2011-2020. (4) Per unit of capital the 21-session hold is better everywhere; 42 sessions only helps if capital is not the constraint. (5) Status: 42 sessions is a candidate second hold, supported in FULL and S1, not in the 2011-2020 data. It adds a hold length chosen after looking, so it is a lead, not an edge. Not done: costs (a longer hold pays the round-trip cost once, so the cost drag per month is lower), survivorship for the 42-session hold, the sealed years.
Next (recommended): keep 21 and 42 as the two candidate holds for the hypothesis file (drop 63 and longer unless a rule written first supports them); cost realism for both (Entry 45 gives the 21-session figures); then the pre-declared market-drawdown rule for holding longer, since Michael's mechanism (insiders buying into selloffs) is the open question.

## Entry 48 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C3/C5 (CEO and/or CFO among the buyers) at a two-month hold: the base four-insider rule with no CEO and no CFO, held 42 sessions | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (asked by Michael; menu declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md before the run; same menu as Entry 32)
Script `research/canonical/factor_roles.py` (unchanged), workflow `.github/workflows/factor-roles-hold.yml` (new, reuses artifacts), branch `claude/roles-hold42-20261108` (PR #77, merged), run **42508393413** (job 128815924214, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42508393413). Inputs: events with buyers from the Entry 32 run 42493259014, year files 42488283630, 42-session returns 42507694879 (Entry 47). The 2026 pilot has no 42-session returns (n=0). Analysed 1,371 development events (82 of 1,453 primary-allowed have no complete return). Excess over same-window SPY in ATR; price only, no costs.
**Development 2006-2020 at 42 sessions (n; excess; SPY; week-block 95% range for the mean).** All 1,371: +0.157 (SPY +0.377). Neither CEO nor CFO 378: **+0.304** (stock +0.626, SPY +0.322; range -0.076 to +0.703; 227 weeks; median +0.189; beat SPY 202/378). CEO among buyers 806: +0.118 (range -0.152 to +0.400); no CEO 565: +0.212. CFO among buyers 562: +0.037; no CFO 809: +0.241. Both 375: +0.042; CEO only 431: +0.185; CFO only 187: +0.026. Without the three large episodes: all 1,096 +0.272; neither 319 **+0.486**; CEO +0.224; CFO +0.128; CFO only +0.018.
**Chance (labels shuffled inside the same entry week, 10,000 draws).** Neither vs everyone else +0.203, chance 35.1% alone; CFO -0.204 (25.5%); CEO -0.094 (62.4%); both -0.158 (46.4%); CEO only +0.041 (75.8%); CFO only -0.152 (42.3%). Best of six: |0.204|, chance 68.2% by luck.
**Comparison with Entry 32 (21 sessions).** All +0.154; neither +0.259 (range +0.013 to +0.507; chance 31.9%; best of six 67.5%). So the two-month hold changes almost nothing for the base rule (+0.154 to +0.157) and raises the neither group from +0.259 to +0.304; the range at 42 sessions now includes zero.
**Reading.** (1) The base four-insider rule alone has about +0.16 ATR of excess over SPY at both 21 and 42 sessions; holding two months does not help it. (2) Excluding events with a CEO or CFO among the buyers gives +0.30 at two months (+0.49 without the three large episodes), but the gap against the rest is +0.20 with a 35% chance of arising by shuffling, and the best-of-six chance is 68%; it is not distinguishable from luck. (3) After an assumed 0.10 to 0.30 ATR round-trip cost (Entry 2, unverified) the neither group would keep roughly 0.0 to +0.2 ATR. (4) No edge. C3 stays a weak lead found in the pilot data that did not replicate in 2006-2020 (Entry 32) and does not strengthen at two months. The much larger two-month figures (+1.1 ATR, Entry 47) come from the 12-month-fall filter, not from role mix. Not done: crossing the 12-month-fall fifth with the CEO/CFO split (would be a new question on small groups, to be declared first).
Next (recommended): cost realism for the 21- and 42-session holds of the fell-most fifth (Entry 45 has the 21-session figures); then, if wanted, a declared cross of the fell-most fifth with the no-CEO/CFO split.

**Reproduction.** 1,372 development events, +0.154 (Entry 28); the new file's raw252 / pct252 equal the Entry 36 file in every row (0 differences); 9-month feature present for all rows with history. Per-window cohorts: 1m n=1,370, 3m 1,350, 6m 1,329, 9m 1,303, 12m 1,275.
