**Headline.** Falls of 6 to 8 ATR or less show nothing consistent at any look-back. The pay sits at the deep end and only for long look-backs: stock down 20+ ATR (excess over same-window SPY; stock / SPY legs; n; separate entry spells): 6m +0.957 (+1.323 / +0.366; 77; 24), 9m +0.808 (+1.083 / +0.275; 113; 33), 12m +0.673 (+1.003 / +0.329; 156; 44); beat SPY 66-70%; medians +0.69 to +0.97. Next band down 15-20 ATR: 6m +0.264 (73), 9m +0.441 (112), 12m +0.497 (113; quarter-block range [+0.186, +0.847]); 10-15 ATR: +0.321, +0.281, +0.300 (n 212-227). At 1 month the deepest buckets are thin (8-10 ATR +0.57 on n=40; 10-15 +0.68 on n=19; 15-20 n=2). At 3 months only 11 events are down 20+ (+1.12). Percent family: down 50%+ is the same story (n 194-315; 3m +0.31, 6m +0.519, 9m +0.536, 12m +0.474 with stock +0.754 / SPY +0.280, beat SPY 61%); percent buckets under 35% are noise (cells of n 15-100 swinging -0.85 to +0.79 with no order; most under 30 are thin).
**Cumulative "down at least X ATR".** 12m: >=2 +0.24 (950), >=6 +0.34 (696), >=10 +0.47 (481), >=15 +0.60 (269), >=20 +0.67 (156). 9m: >=10 +0.45 (452), >=15 +0.63 (226), >=20 +0.81 (113). 6m: >=10 +0.44 (377), >=20 +0.96 (77). Monotone with depth at 6, 9 and 12 months; flat at 1 and 3 months below 10 ATR.
**Chance (within-week shuffle, 10,000 draws; |z| = cell vs rest of its window cohort).** Single-question: 6m 20+ 0.3%, 9m 20+ 0.2%, 12m 20+ 0.3%, 12m percent 50+ 0.4%, 9m percent 50+ 1.8%. As the best of the 82-cell menu none clears: the strongest cell by |z| is a NEGATIVE one, 9m down 2-4 ATR, -0.489 (z -3.81, 45% beat SPY, best-of-menu chance 5.6%, quarter-block range [-0.991, +0.098]); the best positive cell, 6m 20+ (z +3.25), is 32.8% as best-of-82 (26.2% within the ATR family). Neighbouring cells are correlated (same stocks fall at every look-back), so best-of-menu is conservative but the single-cell chances are optimistic.
**Stability of the deep cells.** Without 2008Q4 / 2011Q3 / 2020Q1: 6m 20+ +1.002 (61), 9m 20+ +0.774 (101), 12m 20+ +0.611 (136), 12m percent 50+ +0.564 (215). By era: 12m 20+ 2006-10 +0.685 (66) vs 2011-20 +0.665 (90); 9m 20+ +1.111 (54) vs +0.530 (59); 6m 20+ +0.782 (42) vs +1.167 (35). Quarter-block ranges exclude zero for 20+ at 6m [+0.063, +1.566], 9m [+0.036, +1.353], 12m [+0.039, +1.149], with lower ends near zero. Entry weeks 57 / 89 / 113 and spells 24 / 33 / 44, so the independent sample is tens of market periods, not 77 to 156.
**Reading.** (1) Your bucket idea works as a map: the signal is not in "down a little"; it begins around 10-15 ATR on a 6-12 month look-back, grows with depth, and the 20+ ATR group is where the pay is. 20 ATR is roughly a 60-70% fall at a typical 5-6% daily range, so it overlaps the 12-month fell-most fifth of Entry 36 (median -58%): this is the same lead cut finer, not a new one. (2) Short look-backs (1 and 3 months) give no usable gradient; the 1-month deep cells are too thin to read. (3) The small-fall buckets contain the only clearly negative cell (9m down 2-4 ATR, -0.49), which is as likely chance as structure; it also appears best-of-menu at 5.6%, so do not trade on it. (4) Nothing here beats the best-of-menu bar; it is a lead, post hoc, not an edge. Cautions that still apply: deep fallers are where the price archive is likeliest to have holes (Entries 38, 40, 44), so survivorship probably flatters the 20+ cells; costs are not in any number and are largest in these low-priced names (Entry 45: about +0.32 net of assumed costs for the fell-most fifth); the 21-session hold is the only hold these data support (Entry 46); buckets were fixed before the run but the headline was chosen after seeing the grid.
Next (recommended): (1) decide with Michael whether the pre-declared rule for the hypothesis file should be a depth threshold (for example, down >= 15 or 20 ATR over 6-12 months, written first, tested once on a held-out slice such as 2011-2020 vs 2006-2010 as an in-development split) instead of the fifth; it costs no new data. (2) Survivorship bound for the 20+ ATR cells in the Entry 44 style, because they are the likeliest to be missing. (3) The cost tiering of Entry 45 applied to the 20+ group. The sealed run stays closed.

## Entry 50 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | ATR-to-percent conversion of the Entry 49 decline buckets (Michael's question: what percent falls are "12+ ATR over 6-9 months"?) | PROVISIONAL (derivation from logged numbers; no new code, no workflow run)
Inputs read, not re-run: Entry 49 (run 42508173058, job 128815833560) and Entry 45 (run 42506287640). No script, no branch beyond this documentation entry, no run link of its own. Michael's question named "Entry 39"; Entry 39 is the lead63 follow-up, and the question matches Entry 49.
**Formula (derived by Claude).** ATR is measured at entry, after the fall, so percent fall = D*a / (1 + D*a), D = fall in entry-ATR units, a = entry ATR / price. At a = 6% (Entry 45 median): 12 ATR = 42%, 15 ATR = 47%, 20 ATR = 55%. Across a = 4% to 10%: 12 ATR = 32% to 55%; 20 ATR = 44% to 67%. So 12 ATR is typically a 35-50% fall and 20+ ATR typically 50-65%.
**Check against Entry 45.** Fell-most fifth by 12-month fall (cut 15.3 ATR): median -58%, quartiles -70% / -46%, median ATR% 6%. At a = 6%, -58% = about 23 ATR and -46% = about 14 ATR, consistent with a group cut at 15.3. Entry 49's sentence "20 ATR is roughly a 60-70% fall" looks high by this formula (50-60% at typical ATR%); this is an inference, not confirmed on the event data.
**Premise check (Entry 49 values, excess over SPY in ATR).** No cell is cut at 12 ATR. 10-15 ATR: 6m +0.32 (n=227), 9m +0.28 (227); 15-20: +0.26 (73), +0.44 (112); 20+: +0.96 (77), +0.81 (113). No 6- or 9-month cell shows excess above +1.0; the 20+ cells' stock legs are +1.32 and +1.08 (SPY +0.37, +0.28). Percent family: down 50%+ 6m +0.52 (274), 9m +0.54 (299); 35-50% 6m -0.01 (212), 9m +0.26 (243); 20-35% about zero.
**Status.** C8 stays a lead; Entry 49's caveats stand (best-of-menu chance 23-58% for the deep cells, 24-44 separate spells, survivorship, costs, headline chosen after seeing the grid).
Not done: the exact percent-fall and ATR% quartiles of the 6- and 9-month falls of at least 12, 15 and 20 ATR (needs a small script on the Entry 36 and Entry 49 feature artifacts, which cannot be downloaded from the sandbox).
Next (recommended): that percent-quartile script (no new data), then the Entry 49 next-step 2 survivorship bound for the 20+ ATR cells.

## Entry 51 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Michael's scoring idea: unit-point score (no CEO/CFO + Nasdaq lagging + deep 12-month fall) vs the 21-session excess; plus a new file for Michael's own determinations | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (menu declared in the `score_scan.py` docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md before any score was computed); equal one-point weights are a placeholder, not Michael's decision
**New file.** `research_docs/MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS.md` (linked from AGENTS.md; read at the start of every chat) holds what Michael himself has decided or proposed, in his words; item 1 is the scoring idea (a score for the buyers; a bonus for no CEO and no CFO, or perhaps no CFO alone; bonuses for Nasdaq lag/lead versus SPY; bonuses for the stock being down a certain amount). Not yet decided by Michael: weights, R wording, whether the leading side counts, the size and window of the fall.
Script `research/canonical/score_scan.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/score-scan.yml`, branch `claude/score-scan-20261108` merged as PR #82, run **42516534558** (job 128840671450, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42516534558). Inputs reused: roles events 42493259014 (Entry 32), year files 42488283630, decline features 42499472150 (Entry 36), regime features 42501167019 (Entry 37), returns 42490748366 (Entry 28). Development entries only (D-068); pilot not used; no 2021-2025 return read. 21-session hold, price only, no costs.
**Cohort.** n=1,275 development events with roles, lead63 and raw252 (Entry 41 had 1,273: it also required rel252, hi252 and raw126; the lead63 cut is -0.0194 vs -0.0195, so the Nasdaq-lagging group is the same to rounding). Mean excess +0.190 (stock +0.390, SPY +0.200). raw252 fifth cut = -15.32 ATR.
**Flags.** R1 neither CEO nor CFO; R2 no CFO; N lead63 lowest fifth; D1 raw252 fell-most fifth; D2 down 20+ ATR over 12 months (D2 chosen after the Entry 49 scan). Variants A = R1+N+D1, B = R2+N+D1, C = R1+N+D2, D = R2+N+D2.
**Variant A (the one Michael described), excess over same-window SPY in ATR (stock / SPY legs; n; separate entry spells).** Score 0: -0.041 (+0.114 / +0.156; 595; 35). Score 1: +0.294 (+0.520 / +0.227; 509; 39), quarter-block [+0.057, +0.548]. Score 2: +0.626 (+0.932 / +0.307; 154; 34), [+0.276, +0.918]. Score 3: +1.209 (+1.220 / +0.011; n=17; 6 spells), [+0.343, +2.577]; without the three large episodes +2.077 on n=9; 2006-10 +0.484 (10), 2011-20 +2.246 (7). Score >= 2: 171 events, +0.684 vs +0.113 for score <= 1, difference +0.570, range [+0.178, +0.896]. The score rises step by step in all four variants.
**Variants B-D, score 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 (n at 3).** B: +0.043 / +0.063 / +0.480 / +1.521 (39). C: -0.027 / +0.377 / +0.456 / +1.720 (12, THIN). D: +0.029 / +0.132 / +0.426 / +1.954 (26). Score >= 2 minus <= 1: B +0.556 [+0.197, +0.877]; C +0.422 [-0.060, +0.754]; D +0.491 [+0.101, +0.846].
**Which part carries it (regression, each flag given the other two; quarter-block 95% range).** Variant A: R1 (no CEO and no CFO) +0.108 [-0.137, +0.364]; N (Nasdaq lagging) +0.505 [+0.113, +0.901]; D1 (fell most) +0.496 [+0.040, +0.858]. Variant B: R2 (no CFO) +0.038 [-0.213, +0.272]; N +0.504; D1 +0.496. Variant C: R1 +0.103; N +0.501 [+0.112, +0.885]; D2 +0.532 [-0.159, +1.046]. Variant D: R2 +0.046; N +0.499; D2 +0.535 [-0.157, +1.046]. So N and D1 each add about half an ATR; the no-CEO/CFO bonus adds about +0.1 (no-CFO-alone about +0.04) and its range straddles zero in every variant.
**Trades per month (15 development years).** Variant A: score >= 1 3.78, >= 2 0.95, >= 3 0.09 (17 trades). B: 5.10 / 1.71 / 0.22. C: 3.44 / 0.76 / 0.07. D: 4.94 / 1.39 / 0.14.
**Reading.** (1) Michael's score idea is supported as a way to organise the leads: the excess climbs from about zero at score 0 to +0.63 at score 2, and score >= 2 beats the rest by +0.57 with a range above zero. (2) Almost all of that comes from N (Nasdaq lagging) and the deep fall; the no-CEO/CFO point adds little once those two are in (+0.1, range includes zero), and no-CFO-alone adds less. A score that drops R, or gives it a small weight, would likely look the same; this was not tested. (3) The top score is a handful of events: 17 in variant A, 6 separate entry spells, about one trade every 11 months; its mean (+1.2) is a description, not an estimate. (4) Caveats: each flag was found after looking at the same data (C3 in the pilot, lead63 and the 12-month fall in Entries 36-37, D2 in Entry 49), unit weights are a placeholder, N has no known mechanism and is a one-month effect (Entries 43, 46), cut points are in-sample, the 12-month fall carries survivorship exposure (Entries 38, 40, 44) and costs fall hardest on these names (Entry 45), no chance test was run for the score (best-of-4 variants not quantified), and the D2 variants are mostly an overlap of D1. A score >= 2 rule is a lead, not an edge.
Next (recommended): (1) Michael decides the open items in research_docs/MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS.md item 1 (weights, R wording, leading side, fall size and window), because a score must be declared in full before any held-out test. (2) Declare ONE score and test it once on a slice written down first (for example fit nothing; apply it to 2011-2020 and to 2006-2010 separately), with a proper chance test for the combined rule. (3) Survivorship bound and cost tiering for the score >= 2 group (about one trade a month), the same two weak points as Entries 44 and 45. The sealed run stays closed.

## Entry 52 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Measured percent falls of the 12+ ATR groups at 6 and 9 months; CORRECTS Entry 50's percent estimate | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; descriptive, post hoc (follow-up to Entry 50, asked by Michael); no chance test, no new hypothesis
Script `research/canonical/decline_percent_profile.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/decline-percent-profile.yml`, merged as PR #85 (branch `claude/decline-percent-profile-20261108`), run **42517745314** (job 128844383068, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42517745314). Inputs reused: Entry 36 features 42499472150, Entry 49 9-month features 42508173058, Entry 28 returns 42490748366; development entries only (D-068), no sealed return read; reproduces n=1,372, +0.154, 0 feature differences. Excess = stock minus same-window SPY over 21 sessions in the stock's ATR units, stock and SPY legs beside it; "% of price" = excess ATR x (ATR / entry open), the Entry 36 definition. No costs.
**Correction to Entry 50.** The formula percent = D*a/(1+D*a) is right (measured vs predicted mean gap 0.4 points in every group), but Entry 50 used a = 4-6%. Measured ATR is 7.0-7.4% of the entry open (median) in these groups, so a 12-ATR fall is NOT 35-50%. Entry 50's statement that Entry 49's "20 ATR is roughly 60-70%" looked too high is WRONG: measured, 20+ ATR is mean 70% (6m) / 67% (9m), median 74% / 70%. Entry 50's 12-ATR "35-50% (42% at 6% ATR)" should read as below.
**Percent fall by group (mean; p25 / median / p75; share down >50%), 6m then 9m.** >=10 ATR: 52% (38/53/65; 54%) then 54% (40/53/66; 55%). >=12 ATR: 56% (42/57/69; 64%; n=266, 40 spells) then 57% (44/56/69; 61%; n=346, 36 spells). >=15 ATR: 63% (54/62/77; 79%) then 61% (48/62/74; 72%). >=20 ATR: 70% (60/74/81; 91%; n=77, 24 spells) then 67% (60/70/78; 85%; n=113, 33 spells). Bands: 10-12 ATR 43% / 44% (median 39% / 44%); 12-15 ATR 48% / 48%; 15-20 ATR 55% / 55%.
**Excess over SPY (stock / SPY legs; % of price), 6m then 9m.** >=12 ATR: +0.466 (+0.715 / +0.249; +6.2%) then +0.481 (+0.702 / +0.220; +5.1%), beat SPY 60% / 61%. >=15: +0.620 (+8.9%) / +0.626 (+7.6%). >=20: +0.957 (+1.323 / +0.366; +11.8%) / +0.808 (+1.083 / +0.275; +11.0%). Bands: 12-15 ATR only +0.268 / +0.208; 15-20 +0.264 / +0.445. No >=12 ATR group at 6 or 9 months reaches +1.0 ATR excess; only the 20+ ATR group comes near it.
**Same >=12 ATR events split by measured percent fall (excess ATR; % of price; n; spells).** 6m: under 35% -0.012 (+0.0%; 33; 22); 35-50% +0.027 (-0.1%; 62; 30); 50-65% +0.562 (+4.1%; 87; 32); 65%+ +0.880 (+15.3%; 84; 19). 9m: under 35% +0.362 (+0.7%; 28; 21); 35-50% +0.225 (+1.1%; 106; 36); 50-65% +0.525 (+3.5%; 107; 37); 65%+ +0.727 (+11.8%; 105; 24).
**Reading.** (1) A 12+ ATR fall over 6-9 months is typically a 55-60% decline (middle half 42-69%); 20+ ATR is typically 70%. (2) The 12-ATR line is not where the pay starts: at 6 months the pay sits in falls of 50% or more (about 171 of 266 events), while the 12+ ATR events that fell under 50% earn about nothing (-0.01 to +0.03 ATR, n=95); at 9 months the gradient is the same but gentler. Percent depth and ATR depth are separated by the stock's own volatility (high-ATR stocks reach 12 ATR with a smaller percent fall). (3) The largest percent excess (+15% of price at 65%+ falls, 6m) rests on 19 separate spells and is the most exposed to the Entry 38/40/44 survivorship holes and to costs in very low-priced names (Entry 45). Status: C8 unchanged, a lead; nothing here is a test.
Next (recommended): the Entry 49 next-step 2 survivorship bound for the 20+ ATR and 50%+ cells, then decide with Michael whether a rule written first should use percent depth (for example 50%+ over 6-12 months) rather than ATR depth, since the percent cut separates the pay more cleanly in this data.

## Entry 53 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | Oversold scan: RSI (5, 14, 28, 63 sessions) and distance below the 20/50/100/200-session moving average in ATR vs the next 21-session excess, and whether they add to Nasdaq-lag (N) and the 12-month fall (D1) | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (asked by Michael; menu declared in the `oversold_scan.py` docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md before any value was joined to a return)
Script `research/canonical/oversold_scan.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/oversold-scan.yml`, branch `claude/oversold-20261108` merged as PR #86, run **42517764100** (scan job 128844889486, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42517764100). Inputs reused: Entry 36 decline features 42499472150, Entry 37 regime features 42501167019, Entry 28 returns 42490748366. New features computed from sessions strictly before entry (no-lookahead checked in the self-test). Development entries only (D-068); pilot not used; no 2021-2025 return read. RSI here is the simple-average (Cutler) form, not Wilder's smoothed RSI.
**Reproduction.** 1,372 development events, +0.154 (Entry 28); entry ATR identical to the Entry 36 file in every row (0 differences); oscillator features present for all 1,372 (rsi28 1,368, rsi63 1,357, ma50 1,361, ma100 1,344, ma200 1,316).
**Headline.** Oversold, on its own, does not predict the next 21 sessions at any horizon. Spearman of excess with the measure (low = oversold; week-shuffle chance alone): rsi5 +0.009 (92%), rsi14 +0.030 (77%), rsi28 +0.056 (55%), rsi63 +0.037 (45%), ma20 +0.040 (80%), ma50 +0.037 (77%), ma100 -0.008 (73%). The one exception is ma200 (distance below the 200-session average) at -0.056 (0.2% alone): more below the 200-day average, better excess. Best of the 16-statistic menu: 3.0% by luck (a borderline result, driven by that one measure). ma200 correlates +0.77 with the 12-month fall (raw252) and ma100 +0.53, so ma200 is largely the Entry 36 lead seen through a different ruler.
**Lowest fifth (most oversold; stock / SPY legs; n).** rsi5 +0.259 (+0.568 / +0.309; 275); rsi14 +0.111 (+0.459 / +0.348; 275); rsi28 -0.010 (+0.323 / +0.333; 274); rsi63 -0.097 (+0.296 / +0.393; 272); ma20 +0.042 (275); ma50 +0.080 (+0.389 / +0.309; 272); ma100 +0.292 (+0.547 / +0.254; 269); **ma200 +0.511 (+0.781 / +0.270; 263; 163 entry weeks; beat SPY 63%; no-episodes +0.549 on 214)**. All-event mean +0.154. Highest fifths are often as good or better (rsi14 Q5 +0.415, rsi28 Q5 +0.553): there is no oversold premium in the short and medium measures.
**Fixed thresholds.** RSI14 <= 10: -0.321 (n=48); RSI14 <= 20: +0.047 (254); RSI5 <= 10: +0.335 (201); ma50 <= -4 ATR: +0.121 (501); ma50 <= -6 ATR: +0.194 (166). ma200 <= -4 ATR: +0.242 (843, beat SPY 57%); <= -6 ATR: +0.248 (608). Nothing short-term shows a gradient; ma200 shows a mild one.
**Overlap (Spearman with raw21 / raw252 / lead63).** rsi14 +0.78 / +0.13 / -0.05; ma50 +0.89 / +0.34 / -0.02; ma200 +0.46 / +0.77 / 0.00. Short RSI and short averages are almost the 21-session fall (which Entry 36 already showed carries nothing); ma200 is the 12-month fall; none overlaps the Nasdaq-lag measure.
**Does it add to N + D1? (cohort n=1,275; points 0 / 1 / 2 = +0.016 (817) / +0.379 (406) / +1.434 (52)).** Regression excess = a + bN*N + bD*D1 + bO*O, O = lowest fifth, quarter-block 95% range for bO: rsi5 +0.091 [-0.113, +0.414]; rsi14 -0.101 [-0.327, +0.221]; rsi28 -0.238 [-0.474, +0.104]; **rsi63 -0.455 [-0.683, -0.166]**; ma20 -0.110 [-0.322, +0.250]; ma50 -0.238 [-0.541, +0.147]; ma100 -0.053 [-0.469, +0.384]; ma200 +0.231 [-0.237, +0.668] (with ma200 in, D1 falls to +0.368 [-0.084, +0.771]: they carry the same information). N stays +0.49 to +0.53 and D1 +0.37 to +0.57 in every model. Among events with 0 points the oversold names did worse than the rest (rsi63 O -0.422, n=136; rsi28 -0.267; ma20 -0.254; ma50 -0.224); at 2 points the oversold subset is not better than the group (n 6 to 41). By era, O in 2011-2020: rsi5 +0.137, rsi14 +0.004, rsi28 -0.043, rsi63 -0.251, ma20 -0.097, ma50 +0.013, ma100 +0.258, ma200 +0.514 (2006-10 was higher for the short measures: +0.38 to +0.66, where most missing prices sit).
**Reading.** (1) Oversold, as RSI or as distance below a short or medium average, is NOT an additional lead: it adds nothing to N and D1, and RSI63 (3-month) oversold is significantly worse once they are in (-0.455, range below zero). Buying oversold stocks that carry neither Nasdaq-lag nor a deep 12-month fall did not pay (0-point oversold groups -0.22 to -0.42). This matches Entry 36 (5-, 21- and 63-session declines show nothing). (2) The distance below the 200-session average is a different ruler for the same 12-month-fall lead: strong alone (+0.511 in the lowest fifth, chance 0.2% alone, 3.0% best-of-16) but it does not add once D1 is in (bO +0.231, range includes zero). It might be a usable substitute for the 12-month fall; that was not tested as a rule. (3) Cautions: RSI is the simple-average form (a Wilder version could differ at the margin); the 2006-10 half has the most missing prices; no costs; best-of-16 3.0% is borderline and comes from a measure that overlaps the existing lead. Status: C18 (RSI) and C19 (distance below short and medium averages) not supported; ma200 is a restatement of C8, not new. Nothing here is sealed-year evidence.
Next (recommended): leave the short and medium oversold measures out of the score. If Michael wants, test ma200 distance as an alternative definition of the deep-fall point D (declared first, alongside the 189- and 315-session look-backs already queued in START_HERE), because it needs no new data and may be less noisy. The survivorship bound and cost tiering for the deep-fall group still come first for the score, and Michael's open items in research_docs/MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS.md item 1 remain open.

## Entry 57 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C21: 15-session average dollar volume vs the 21-session excess, and the D1 / N leads inside dollar-volume bands | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (proposal 3 approved by Michael; menu declared in the `context_scan.py` docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md, PR #93, before any dollar-volume value was joined to a return)
Script `research/canonical/context_scan.py dv`, workflow `.github/workflows/context-scan.yml`, branch `claude/context-scan-20261108` merged as PR #93, run **42524139173** (scan job 128863830580, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42524139173). Development entries only; pilot not used; no 2021-2025 return read. 21-session hold, no costs. Reproduces n=1,372, +0.154 (Entry 28); all 1,372 events have all 15 dollar-volume bars. Flag cohort n=1,275 (N cut -0.0194, D1 cut -15.32 ATR, D1 n=255, N n=255, as Entries 51 and 53). Every event already passed the $3M floor.
**Chance tests (week-shuffle; 12 on the tally).** Whole cohort: dv_mean rho -0.082 (chance alone 12.2%), smile -0.270 (1.4%); dv_median rho -0.087 (12.2%), smile -0.264 (4.5%); best of 4 by luck 3.8%. Inside D1: smallest chance 16.2%, best of 4 35.2%. Inside N: smallest 24.8%, best of 4 50.5%. The negative smile means the outer fifths (thinnest and most liquid) did worse than the middle three.
**Bands of 15-session average dollar volume, all events (excess; stock / SPY legs; n).** Under $5M +0.068 (+0.418 / +0.350; 217); $5-10M +0.326 (+0.522 / +0.195; 308); $10-25M +0.365 (+0.515 / +0.150; 330); $25-100M -0.014 (+0.092 / +0.106; 348); $100M and over -0.114 (+0.143 / +0.257; 169).
**D1 inside each band (with flag vs without; difference and quarter-block range).** Under $5M +1.109 (n=38) vs -0.084 (154): +1.193 [+0.376, +1.950]. $5-10M +0.699 (60) vs +0.307 (219): +0.392 [-0.745, +1.211]. $10-25M +0.711 (51) vs +0.283 (254): +0.428 [-0.406, +1.089]. $25-100M +0.275 (76) vs -0.041 (260): +0.316 [-0.207, +0.763]. $100M and over +0.330 (30) vs -0.187 (133): +0.516 [-0.325, +1.359]. **N inside each band.** Under $5M +0.590 (43) vs +0.026 (149): +0.565 [-0.172, +1.308]. $5-10M +1.053 (51) vs +0.243 (228): +0.810 [-0.147, +1.284]. $10-25M +0.767 (64) vs +0.245 (241): +0.522 [-0.111, +1.480]. $25-100M -0.041 (65) vs +0.048 (271): -0.089 [-0.659, +0.421]. $100M and over +0.828 (32) vs -0.316 (131): +1.145 [+0.306, +1.762]. The 255 N events fall in only 77 entry weeks (D1: 163 weeks).
**Liquidity floors on the leads (excess; without the three large episodes; trades a month over 180 months).** D1: all +0.593 (255; +0.595; 1.42); dv >= $10M +0.427 (157; +0.515, n=136; 0.87); dv >= $25M +0.290 (106; +0.411, n=92; 0.59). N: all +0.596 (255; +0.689; 1.42); >= $10M +0.453 (161; +0.603; 0.89); >= $25M +0.246 (97; +0.356; 0.54).
**Reading.** (1) The base rule pays in the middle of the liquidity range ($5-25M a day, about +0.33 to +0.37) and about nothing above $25M (-0.01 and -0.11); the inverted U is 1.4% alone and 3.8% as best of 4, so it is a lead, not a finding. The most tradable names carry the least. (2) The deep-fall lead (D1) is positive in all five bands, but the gap is largest (+1.193, range above zero) in the thinnest band, under $5M a day (38 events), which is also where an assumed cost (Entry 45) matters most; in the other four bands the range includes zero. With a $10M floor D1 is +0.427 (about 0.9 trades a month); with $25M it is +0.290 (about 0.6 a month), still above SPY but the size of a modest edge before costs. (3) Nasdaq-lag (N) is positive in four of five bands and about zero in $25-100M. (4) Dollar volume does not predict inside D1 or N (best-of-4 35% and 51%). Status: C21 weak lead (liquidity inverted U), and a capacity warning: part of the deep-fall result comes from the thinnest names. No spreads, impact or position size are measured; costs not in any number.
Next (recommended): see Entry 58.

## Entry 58 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C22: SPY below its 200-session average at entry as a gate on the deep-fall (D1) and Nasdaq-lag (N) leads | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (proposal 1 approved by Michael; menu declared in the `context_scan.py` docstring and research_docs/CANDIDATES.md, PR #93, before any gate number was computed)
Script `research/canonical/context_scan.py gate`, workflow `.github/workflows/context-scan.yml`, branch `claude/context-scan-20261108` merged as PR #93, run **42524145053** (scan job 128863850005, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42524145053). Inputs reused: decline features 42499472150, regime features 42501167019 (spyt200 = SPY close / its 200-session average - 1), returns 42490748366. Development entries only; pilot not used; no 2021-2025 return read. 21-session hold, no costs. Flag cohort n=1,275 (as Entries 51 and 53). B = spyt200 < 0.
**B alone (excess; stock / SPY legs).** SPY below its 200-day average +0.144 (+0.421 / +0.277; n=627, 146 weeks) vs above +0.234 (+0.359 / +0.125; n=648, 308 weeks); difference -0.090 [-0.445, +0.332]. Without the three large episodes: below +0.457 (n=378), above +0.290 (n=634).
**D1 x B.** D1 and below +0.725 (+0.937 / +0.212; n=136; 14 entry spells); D1 and above +0.442 (+0.673 / +0.231; n=119; 41 spells); below minus above +0.283 [-0.424, +0.948]. Not D1 and below -0.017 (n=491); not D1 and above +0.187 (n=529). **N x B.** N and below +0.598 (+0.853 / +0.254; n=192; 8 spells); N and above +0.590 (+0.988 / +0.399; n=63; 12 spells); below minus above +0.009 [-0.742, +0.713]. Not N and below -0.056 (435); not N and above +0.196 (585). 192 of the 255 N events (75%, derived) entered when SPY was below its 200-day average. **Points (N + D1) x B.** 0 points: below -0.165 (346), above +0.150 (471); 1 point: +0.348 (234) vs +0.423 (172); 2 points: +1.405 (47) vs +1.702 (5).
**Eras.** D1 2006-10 below +0.823 (n=85), above -0.297 (n=15); 2011-20 below +0.561 (51), above +0.548 (104). N 2006-10 below +0.534 (111), above +1.617 (15); 2011-20 below +0.687 (81), above +0.269 (48).
**Chance (circular shift of B over 847 entry dates; 2 statistics).** DiD for D1 +0.487 (chance alone 32.2%); DiD for N +0.261 (49.4%); smallest 32.2%; best of 2 by luck 53.6%.
**Entry 36 crossing restated (stock raw252 lower half x SPY down over the same 252 sessions).** Lower half, SPY down +0.598 (n=279, 93 weeks); lower half, SPY up +0.123 (359); upper half, SPY down -0.005 (170); upper half, SPY up +0.069 (467). Matches Entry 36 (+0.598 and +0.123) except one event moves between the two upper-half cells (Entry 36 printed +0.004 with n=171 and 466); cause not investigated, no effect on conclusions.
**Reading.** (1) SPY below its 200-day average is NOT a gate: the deep-fall lead is positive either way (+0.725 below, +0.442 above), the difference's range includes zero, the interaction chance is 32% (best of 2: 54%), and in 2011-2020 the two cells are the same (+0.561 vs +0.548). The 2006-10 gap rests on 15 events above the line and on the 2008-09 stretch. (2) The Entry 36 "stock fell most x SPY down" table reproduces, but its SPY-down half is mostly the same few long market stretches (14 spells for D1 and below). Most Nasdaq-lag events (75%) coincide with SPY below its 200-day average, so N is partly a bear-market marker, but N still pays at +0.590 when SPY is above the line (n=63, 12 spells, thin). Status: C22 NOT supported as a gate; C15 (SPY trend stand-alone) unchanged, not supported. Caveats: SPY below the line is a handful of long stretches (see spell counts), so the effective sample is small; no costs; survivorship (Entries 38, 40, 44).
**Next (recommended), after Entries 56-58.** (1) Cost and survivorship for the deep-fall group, tiered by the dollar-volume bands in Entry 57: the thin names (under $5M a day) carry the largest D1 gap and are the most exposed to costs and to missing delisted prices, so the Entry 45 cost assumptions and the Entry 49 survivorship bound should be run by band before any weights are set. (2) Michael's open items in research_docs/MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS.md item 1 (weights; "no CEO and no CFO" vs "no CFO"; whether Nasdaq-leading counts; size and window of the fall). The score should not include a bounce term (Entry 56) or an SPY-200-day gate (this entry); a liquidity floor ($10M a day) is a design choice for Michael. (3) Alternative long-window definitions (189, 315 sessions), declared first. The sealed run stays closed.

## Entry 59 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C23 stage 1: consolidation features (base width, efficiency, breakout gap) over base windows of 10, 20, 40 and 60 sessions, distributions and state counts inside the deep-fall group D1; NO returns joined | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); features only, nothing return-related; POST HOC (Michael's idea; menu registered as C23 in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md and the `base_scan.py` docstring before the run)
Script `research/canonical/base_scan.py` (`baseyear`, `basedist`), workflow `.github/workflows/base-scan.yml`, branch `claude/base-scan-20261108` merged as PR #96, run **42527256841** (dist job 128873947771, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42527256841). Inputs: entry list from run 42490748366 and raw252 from the Entry 36 feature files (run 42499472150); development entries only (D-068); no return of any kind was read or printed. The cohort is the 1,276 development events with usable features (Entry 36 features exist for 1,276 events; the returns cohorts of Entries 51-58 have 1,275 or 1,273, so D1 here is n=257 against 255 there: the extra events lack a complete return). D1 = raw252 <= -15.32 ATR.
**Definitions.** Base = W sessions ending 5 sessions before entry; breakout window = the last 5 completed sessions. width = (highest high - lowest low of the base) / ATR; eff = |first-to-last close change| / sum of absolute daily close changes; topgap = (last close - highest close of the base) / ATR (> 0 = closed above the top of the box). A new low inside the base is allowed.
**Inside D1: medians (width / eff / topgap) and share closed above the top of the box.** W=10: 3.7 / 0.44 / -3.24, 12.1%. W=20: 5.67 / 0.38 / -5.23, 6.6%. W=40: 8.99 / 0.32 / -8.36, 1.9%. W=60: 10.9 / 0.24 / -10.3, 1.2%. Width and efficiency overlap (Spearman inside D1 +0.70, +0.71, +0.66, +0.62 for W=10/20/40/60); width grows with the window by construction.
**D1 state counts at tentative cutoffs (state 1 not basing / 2 basing, no breakout / 3 basing then breakout).** Tight = lowest third of width AND lowest third of eff inside D1: W=10: 198 / 49 / 10; W=20: 197 / 52 / 8; W=40: 199 / 54 / 4; W=60: 208 / 46 / 3. Looser = lowest two thirds of both: W=10: 114 / 121 / 22; W=20: 113 / 129 / 15; W=40: 116 / 136 / 5; W=60: 118 / 136 / 3. D1 tercile cuts (width / eff): W=10 3.13, 4.41 / 0.302, 0.56; W=20 4.78, 6.76 / 0.272, 0.486; W=40 7.55, 10.6 / 0.232, 0.376; W=60 9.43, 13.1 / 0.2, 0.295.
**Reading.** (1) A true breakout (close above the highest close of the base) is rare in D1: 12% at W=10, 6.6% at W=20, 1.9% at W=40, 1.2% at W=60, because deep fallers sit far below the top of any long base; state 3 has 3 to 22 events, too few for any return comparison. (2) Basing versus not basing is testable: 46 to 54 events (tight) or 121 to 136 (looser) against the rest. (3) Width and efficiency are largely the same information. Nothing here says whether basing helps; that is stage 2. Stage 2 cutoffs are to be chosen with Michael and written in the log before any return is joined.
Next (recommended): Michael chooses (a) tight or looser cutoff (or both), (b) one tightness measure or both, (c) a softer definition of 'moving up' that leaves enough events (for example topgap >= -1 ATR, or a net rise over the last 5 sessions), then stage 2 joins returns once.

## Entry 60 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C23 stage 2: consolidation (base) and 'moved up' states inside the deep-fall group D1, base windows of 10, 20, 40 and 60 sessions, vs the next 21-session excess | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); returns code PROVISIONAL as in Entry 28; POST HOC and exploratory (cutoffs and menu written in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C23, PR #100, before any return was joined; Michael: 'do what you think is best')
Script `research/canonical/base_scan.py stage2`, workflow `.github/workflows/base-scan.yml`, branch `claude/base-stage2-20261108` merged as PR #100, run **42528535571** (job 128877378235, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42528535571). Inputs reused: base features from stage 1 run 42527256841 (Entry 59), decline features 42499472150, regime features 42501167019, returns 42490748366. Development entries only (D-068); pilot not used; no 2021-2025 return read. 21-session hold, no costs. Cohort: D1 of the flag cohort, n=255 (as Entries 51-58), excess +0.593 (stock +0.814, SPY +0.221), all 255 have all four windows.
**Declared cutoffs.** Choppiness (efficiency) is the primary tightness measure. Basing-TIGHT = efficiency in the lowest third inside D1, basing-LOOSE = lowest two thirds (cutoffs on this cohort: W=10 0.30 / 0.56; W=20 0.272 / 0.484; W=40 0.23 / 0.375; W=60 0.199 / 0.294). MOVED-UP = last close within 1 ATR of the highest close of the base, or above it. States: 1 not basing; 2 basing, not moved up; 3 basing and moved up.
**Chance menu (12 statistics; mean excess with the flag minus without it inside D1; excess shuffled inside the entry week).** TIGHT10 -0.155 (chance alone 76.0%); TIGHT20 +0.349 (4.1%); TIGHT40 +0.295 (27.5%); TIGHT60 +0.328 (17.1%). LOOSE10 +0.281 (39.0%); LOOSE20 -0.081 (52.7%); LOOSE40 +0.547 (9.2%); LOOSE60 +0.630 (15.6%). UP10 -0.315 (11.8%, n=52); UP20 +0.065 (85.0%, n=27); UP40 +0.735 (87.1%, n=12); UP60 -0.471 (17.0%, n=7). Smallest single chance 4.1%; chance the best of 12 is at least that small by luck **31.8%**.
**Basing vs not basing (excess; n; quarter-block range of the difference; eras).** W=60 loose: basing +0.831 (n=170 incl. moved up; state 2 alone +0.831, n=164) vs not basing +0.173 (n=85, beat SPY 54%); difference +0.630 [+0.057, +1.156]; 2006-10 +0.869 vs +0.221, 2011-20 +0.760 vs +0.142; without the three large episodes +0.793 vs +0.164. W=40 loose: basing state 2 +0.736 (n=159) vs not basing +0.228 (n=85, beat SPY 51%); difference +0.547 [-0.198, +1.164]; 2006-10 +0.917 vs +0.098, 2011-20 +0.680 vs +0.306. W=40 tight: +0.295 [-0.221, +0.856]; 2011-20 +0.890 vs +0.361. W=20 tight: +0.349 [-0.267, +0.975]; W=20 loose -0.081 [-0.671, +0.462]. W=10 tight -0.155 [-0.676, +0.278]; W=10 loose +0.281 [-0.200, +0.765]. The not-basing group (a stock still sliding in an orderly way over 40-60 sessions) is the weak one: +0.17 to +0.23 and beating SPY only 51% to 54% of the time (W=40 / 60 loose, n=85).
**Moved up (state 3, and 'moved up' alone).** State 3 under the loose rule: W=10 +0.316 (n=46); W=20 +0.408 (23); W=40 +1.336 (11); W=60 +0.022 (6). Under the tight rule: W=10 -0.424 (24); W=20 +0.705 (17); W=40 +0.745 (9); W=60 +0.022 (6). 'Moved up, any base': W=10 +0.342 (n=52, beat SPY 62%) vs +0.657 for the rest; W=20 +0.651 (27) vs +0.586; W=40 +1.293 (12) vs +0.558; W=60 +0.135 (7) vs +0.606. Signs flip with the window and counts are 6 to 52.
**Descriptive, not chance-tested (width and width-plus-choppiness, tight vs rest).** Width alone, tight third vs rest: W=10 +0.472 vs +0.653; W=20 +0.725 vs +0.527; W=40 +0.852 vs +0.463; W=60 +0.822 vs +0.478. Both measures tight: W=20 +0.960 (n=59) vs +0.482; W=40 +0.888 (57) vs +0.508; W=60 +0.796 (49) vs +0.544.
**Reading.** (1) Not supported as a tested result: the best single chance is 4.1% and best of 12 is 31.8%; the windows overlap and D1 is 255 events. (2) A weak lead: among deep fallers, those that were still in an orderly slide over the last 40-60 sessions did worse (+0.17 to +0.23) than those whose last 2-3 months were choppy or sideways (+0.74 to +0.83); the direction holds in both eras, without the three large episodes, and for the width-based definitions at 40 and 60 sessions, and the W=60 loose range stays above zero. It is the same lead in two rulers (not repeatedly independent). (3) The 'moved up' half of the idea has no support: a close near or above the top of the base is not better than the rest (W=10 -0.315; W=40 and W=60 groups of 12 and 7 events), and the strict breakout is rare (Entry 59). In this data, buying at the filing was not worse than waiting for a move up. (4) Caveats: post hoc; 12 overlapping statistics; D1 survivorship and costs (Entries 38, 40, 44, 45) not in any number; the thin names (Entry 57) are mixed into every cell.
Status: C23 basing: weak lead (long-window choppiness, 'not still sliding'); C23 moved up: not supported.
Next (recommended): see the chat summary; the stop and trailing-stop exit engine is next, then one declared system.

## Entry 62 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C24: four-insider entry with a 2 ATR initial stop and a 10-day trailing stop (Michael's request), development years | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL; returns use the Entry 28 entry and ATR definitions; exploratory (rule declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C24 and the docstring of `stop_test.py` before any stop-managed return was computed)
Script `research/canonical/stop_test.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/stop-test.yml`, branch `claude/stop-test-20261108` merged as PR #103, run **42532088270** (summary job 128888968498, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42532088270). Inputs: refined-study year artifacts 42488283630 (primary set rebuilt, Entry 25 counts asserted). Development entries only (D-068; exits may fall in 2021 as in Entry 43); pilot not run. Price only, no costs except the extra columns.
**Rule.** Entry at the open of the entry session (the next open after the filing that makes the fourth distinct buyer); 1R = 2 ATR (ATR14 before entry); initial stop = entry open - 2 ATR from the entry session; trailing stop = lowest LOW of the 10 completed sessions before each session (pre-entry sessions count), never lowered, never below the initial stop; a session whose low touches the stop exits at the stop, or at the open if it gaps below; 252-session maximum; a series that ends early is closed at its last close.
**Reproduction check.** The 21-session control on the same events gives n=1,372, excess +0.154 (stock +0.348, SPY +0.194): identical to Entry 28.
**Result, 1,373 events (stock / SPY / excess in ATR; R = stock ATR / 2).** Mean R +0.142, median R -0.163, wins 39% (average win +1.09R, average loss -0.45R), profit factor 1.51. Stock +0.284, SPY +0.099, excess +0.185 (median -0.278; beat SPY 547 of 1,373). Average hold 15.2 sessions (median 12). Week-block 95% range of the mean excess +0.018 to +0.407 (1.1% of draws <= 0); of mean R +0.024 to +0.283 (0.7%). 821 issuers, 898 entry dates, 450 entry weeks. Excluding the 2 delist exits: mean R +0.132, excess +0.166.
**Exit reasons.** Trailing stop 1,321; initial 2 ATR stop 50 (mean -1.03R, 4 sessions); time 0; delist 2 (KITE +14.2R, 99 sessions, acquired; one other). So the 10-session low, not the 2 ATR stop, ends almost every trade: with pre-entry sessions in the window the trail sits above the initial stop from about the second session. Losses worse than -1R (gaps through the stop): 12 of 1,373 (0.9%), worst -7.62R (CBC 2007-04-27).
**Same events, three exits (constant cohort n=1,372).** 21-session hold excess +0.154 over 21 sessions; stop-managed +0.186 over 15.2 sessions on average; per 21 sessions of capital tied up +0.154 vs +0.257. The 252-session no-stop control (stock +4,332 ATR) is INVALID: ticker reuse (SUNE, as in Entries 43 and 46); not used.
**Costs (extra columns).** At 0.10 ATR round trip: excess +0.085, mean R +0.092. At 0.25: excess -0.065, mean R +0.017.
**Eras and years.** 2006-10 mean R +0.097 (n=428, excess +0.186); 2011-20 +0.162 (n=945, excess +0.185). Without 2008Q4, 2011Q3, 2020Q1: R +0.161, excess +0.170 (n=1,098). Episodes: 2008Q4 R -0.091 (66), 2011Q3 -0.243 (74), 2020Q1 +0.315 (135). Mean R positive in 11 of 15 years (negative 2007, 2011, 2015, 2018); the worst year 2007 -0.176R (n=120).
**Concentration.** Top trade OSTK 2020-03-23 +63.5R (113 sessions); then KITE +14.2R, HLX +6.7R, HBI +6.0R, LLY +5.8R. Mean R without the top 1/3/5/10: +0.096 / +0.081 / +0.072 / +0.055. Clipped at the 1st and 99th percentile: +0.091 (unclipped +0.142). Mean excess without the top 10: +0.031. One trade over +1,000% (OSTK). Average open positions about 5.5 (91.5 events a year x 15.2 sessions / 252).
**Reading.** (1) The stop-managed rule has a positive mean (+0.14R, +0.19 ATR over SPY) with a range above zero, similar in size to the 21-session hold's +0.154 but earned in about 15 sessions instead of 21, which is why per unit of time it looks better. (2) It is a few-big-winners shape: 39% wins, median trade negative, and a third of the mean from one trade; without the top 10 the mean is +0.06R. (3) Costs matter: at 0.25 ATR round trip the excess is negative (-0.065) and mean R is +0.017. (4) The declared rule made the 10-session low the binding stop in 96% of trades, so the realised risk was far below 2 ATR (average loss -0.45R). A variant where the 2 ATR stop holds until the trail is formed from post-entry sessions only was NOT run and would be a new question on the tally. (5) Not crossed with the leads (FELL, Q1, BOTH); not an edge; no 2021-2025 entry read; sealed run stays closed. Caveats: price only; stop fills at the stop price are optimistic for thin names; survivorship (deepest failures missing from the archive) cuts the loss tail; the delist exit rule flatters acquisitions (KITE).
Next (recommended): (1) decide whether to run the one declared variant of the stop (initial 2 ATR stop held for the first 10 sessions, trail from post-entry lows only) and the cross with FELL and Q1, because the current rule rarely uses the 2 ATR stop and the leads are the live part of the study; (2) cost realism for the stop-managed trades in the thin names (Entry 45 style). The sealed run stays closed.

## Entry 63 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C25: four-insider entry with a FIXED 2 ATR stop and time exits at 1, 2, 3 and 6 months (Michael's request), development years | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL; exploratory (rule declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C25 before any return was computed)
Script `research/canonical/stop_fixed.py` (imports block_range/clipped/wk from `stop_test.py`), workflow `.github/workflows/stop-fixed.yml`, PR #105 (branch `claude/fixed-stop-20261108`), run **42532801761** (summary job 128891163251, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42532801761). Inputs: primary set rebuilt from refined-study run 42488283630. Development entries only (D-068); pilot not run. Price only.
**Rule.** Entry at the open of the entry session (unchanged from Entry 28). 1R = 2 ATR (ATR14 before entry). Stop = entry open - 2 ATR, set at entry, never moved. A session whose low touches the stop (<=) exits at the stop, or at the open if it opens below. Otherwise exit at the close of session 21, 42, 63 or 126. Series that end early close at the last close ('delist'). Control: same events, same horizon, no stop.
**Reproduction check.** 21-session no-stop control: n=1,372, stock +0.348, SPY +0.194, excess +0.154 = Entry 28.
**Results, n=1,373 (stock / SPY same window / excess, ATR; R = stock ATR / 2).**
| Horizon | Stopped out | Mean R (median) | Win | Stock | SPY | Excess | Week-block range of excess (draws <= 0) | No-stop control: stock / SPY / excess | Held (mean sessions) |
| 1 mo (21) | 31.1% | +0.138 (+0.101) | 53% | +0.276 | +0.125 | +0.151 | +0.027 to +0.279 (1.0%) | +0.348 / +0.194 / +0.154 | 17.3 |
| 2 mo (42) | 46.0% | +0.176 (-0.274) | 46% | +0.352 | +0.229 | +0.123 | -0.047 to +0.301 (7.8%) | +0.534 / +0.377 / +0.157 | 30.2 |
| 3 mo (63) | 54.0% | +0.191 (-1.000) | 40% | +0.383 | +0.289 | +0.094 | -0.103 to +0.295 (17.0%) | +0.705 / +0.585 / +0.120 | 40.7 |
| 6 mo (126) | 63.7% | +0.355 (-1.000) | 34% | +0.711 | +0.502 | +0.208 | -0.079 to +0.515 (8.1%) | +1.216 / +1.104 / +0.112 | 66.0 |
(Control columns are on the constant cohort where the control is complete: 1,372 / 1,371 / 1,367 / 1,360 events; the stop version on those events: excess +0.151 / +0.124 / +0.094 / +0.187.) Mean R week-block ranges all exclude zero (+0.037 to +0.238; +0.044 to +0.319; +0.041 to +0.354; +0.145 to +0.577), but mean R includes the market drift; excess does not.
**Stopped-out versus survivors.** Stopped trades average about -1.04R (stock -2.09 ATR). Survivors to the time exit look great (1 mo: +0.67R, 77% wins; 6 mo: +2.81R, 92% wins) but that is selection: the losers were removed by the stop. Not a result.
**Gaps.** Losses worse than -1R: 4.4% / 5.5% / 7.4% / 8.2% of trades; worst -7.62R (CBC 2007-04-27).
**Costs (ATR round trip).** At 0.10: excess +0.051 / +0.023 / -0.006 / +0.108. At 0.25: -0.099 / -0.127 / -0.156 / -0.042.
**Eras (excess).** 2006-10: +0.213 / +0.513 / +0.402 / +0.857. 2011-20: +0.122 / -0.054 / -0.045 / -0.085 (SPY +0.657 over six months in 2011-20 against +0.160 in 2006-10). Without the three episodes: +0.241 / +0.179 / +0.114 / +0.160. Episodes (excess): 2008Q4 +0.090 / +0.279 / -0.093 / +0.632; 2011Q3 -0.356 / -0.679 / -0.717 / -0.559; 2020Q1 -0.276 / +0.028 / +0.467 / +0.817. Mean R positive in 11 / 10 / 9 / 8 of 15 years.
**Concentration.** Mean R without the top 1 / 3 / 10: 1 mo +0.134 / +0.128 / +0.110; 2 mo +0.169 / +0.157 / +0.126; 3 mo +0.180 / +0.168 / +0.130; 6 mo +0.318 / +0.293 / +0.227. Excess without the top 10: +0.102 / +0.035 / -0.017 / -0.041. Top trade OSTK 2020-03-23: +4.4R / +10.7R / +15.2R / +51.9R. Clipped excess (1st/99th): +0.152 / +0.101 / +0.078 / +0.092.
**Reading.** (1) A fixed 2 ATR stop does not raise the excess over SPY at 1, 2 or 3 months (+0.151 vs +0.154; +0.124 vs +0.157; +0.094 vs +0.120); it lowers the stock return and cuts the drawdown shape (no-stop clipped stock +0.363 vs stop +0.280 at one month). Only at six months does the stop version show higher excess than its control (+0.187 vs +0.112), and that is a market-beta effect (the stop sells many positions before SPY's gains) plus a few big winners (OSTK +51.9R). (2) Only the one-month version has a week-block range of excess above zero (1.0% of draws <= 0); at 2, 3 and 6 months the range includes zero. (3) The effect sits in 2006-10; in 2011-20 the 2, 3 and 6 month versions have slightly negative excess. (4) The 2 ATR stop is touched often: a third of trades inside one month, 64% inside six, because a 2 ATR stop is inside normal movement for these stocks; each stop-out costs -1R plus gaps. (5) Costs: at 0.25 ATR every horizon is negative in excess. (6) The three declared horizons are one family on the same events, not four confirmations; no best-of selection is claimed. Compared with C24 (Entry 62; excess +0.185 in 15 sessions), the fixed 1-month stop gives +0.151 in 17 sessions; the 10-day trail beat it on this measure. Arithmetic from the table (not a script output): excess per 21 sessions of capital tied up is about +0.18 (1 mo), +0.09 (2 mo), +0.05 (3 mo), +0.07 (6 mo).
Caveats: price only; survivorship (deepest failures missing) flatters stop results less than hold results but still matters; stops at the stop price are optimistic in thin names; no 2021-2025 entry read; leads not crossed.
Next (recommended): do not add more stop variants; the stop is a risk tool and does not lift excess here. The one useful pre-declared question is to cross the best candidate exits (C24 trail and the 1-month 2 ATR stop) with the Nasdaq-lagging arm of C16 and the deep-fall lead, since those are where the study's excess is concentrated; one family, declared first. Alternatively resolve the open scoring items in MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS item 1 first.

## Entry 65 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C26a: how big is the survivorship risk for the deep-fall lead D1? In-archive hazard, imputed terminal loss, break-even hazard, dose-response | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL for the grid (the hazard h is an assumption, not a measurement); descriptive, no chance test (menu declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C26a and the docstring before the run)
Script `research/canonical/survivorship_hazard.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/survivorship-hazard.yml`, branch `claude/survivorship-hazard-20261108` merged as PR #108, run **42535630908** (job 128899188246, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42535630908). Inputs: Entry 28 returns 42490748366, Entry 36 features 42499472150, Entry 38 census year files 42488283630. Development entries only (D-068); no 2021+ return read.
**Reproduction.** D1 cut -15.32 ATR, n=255, +0.593 (= Entry 36); all complete development trades n=1,372, +0.154 (= Entry 28); 2011+ subset fifth cut -14.39, n=175, +0.565 (= Entry 44).
**A. In-archive hazard (series ended before session 21).** 1 incomplete trade among 1,276 development events with a feature (0.08%); D1 0 of 255; non-D1 1 of 1,021; 2006-10 0 of 401; 2011-20 1 of 875. So the lead tables lose nothing by using complete trades only, but the archive also holds almost no trades that were wiped out inside the hold. B (imputed terminal loss on incomplete D1 rows) has nothing to impute: no incomplete D1 row.
**C. Unobserved-hazard grid (m = h x n extra D1 events, each wiped out; excess x = -p x avg(entry open/ATR) - avg SPY leg; p = 30% or 55% terminal loss, Shumway and Warther).** 2006-2020 (n=255, +0.593; -55% = -9.0 ATR on average): mean at h = 1% / 2% / 3% / 5% / 8% / 10%: p=55% +0.495 / +0.400 / +0.307 / +0.125 / -0.135 / -0.300; p=30% +0.536 / +0.480 / +0.426 / +0.320 / +0.168 / +0.072. h needed to pull D1 to the all-event +0.154: 4.7% at p=55% (about 12 events), 8.3% at p=30%; to zero: 6.4% / 11.5%. 2011-2020 subset (n=175, +0.565; -55% = -10.5 ATR): p=55% +0.453 / +0.344 / +0.236 / +0.027 / -0.272 / -0.462; p=30% +0.501 / +0.437 / +0.375 / +0.254 / +0.081 / -0.029; to zero: 5.3% (p=55%, about 9 events) / 9.5% (p=30%). NOTE: the subset's all-event comparator here is +0.067 on 944 complete trades (all trades from 2011, with or without a 252-session feature), not Entry 44's +0.096 on 874; the break-even to zero is the cleaner reading.
**D. Dose-response (D1 at the full-development cut, by entry year; census no-price share of that year in brackets).** 2006 +0.86 (2 events, 42%), 2007 +0.09 (11, 36%), 2008 +0.36 (53, 31%), 2009 +1.29 (33, 24%), 2010 +0.99 (1, 23%), 2011 +0.66 (8, 15%), 2012 +1.13 (14, 13%), 2013 -1.30 (5, 11%), 2014 +1.29 (9, 10%), 2015 -0.12 (28, 13%), 2016 +0.45 (20, 10%), 2017 +0.23 (9, 9%), 2018 +1.20 (18, 8%), 2019 +0.69 (26, 7%), 2020 +0.68 (18, 5%). Spearman over the 12 years with 8+ D1 events: D1 excess vs missing share -0.31; D1-minus-non-D1 gap vs missing share -0.24. Survivorship bias predicts a POSITIVE link; the sign is negative. 2006-10 (D1 n=100, census share 31%) +0.655 vs 2011-20 (n=155, 10%) +0.552. Derived from the table, not a script output: 2018-2020 pooled +0.84 (n=62) vs 2011-2017 +0.36 (n=93).
**Reading.** (1) Nothing in the archive measures the wipe-out rate: only 1 of 1,276 trades ended inside the hold, so the grid's h is an ASSUMPTION. For scale only: Shumway and Warther report about 5.6% of Nasdaq stocks delisted per year and nearly 3% a month in the smallest 5%; those are all-stock and micro-cap rates from 1977-94, not rates for $3M+ names with four insiders buying, so they bound nothing here. (2) If 1% to 3% of D1 entries were wiped out inside the hold at -55%, D1 would sit near +0.31 to +0.50 (2006-2020) or +0.24 to +0.45 (2011-2020). It takes about 5% to 6% (about 9 to 12 events) to erase the edge at -55%, or 8% to 12% at -30%. (3) No sign of survivorship in the pattern across years or eras: the link with the missing share is negative and the high-missing era is only slightly better (+0.655 vs +0.552), on 12 years confounded with market regime. This does not clear survivorship: missing names are missing for every era. (4) Not covered: names that would pass the $3M floor but are absent from the archive at entry; costs (Entry 45); the Entry 38/40 holes in 2008-2009 where D1 is concentrated (53 and 33 events).
Status: C8 / D1 stays a lead. Survivorship is a real but bounded risk: the lead survives wipe-out rates of 1% to 3% and fails at about 5% or more. Only a second price source with delisting history can replace the assumed rate with a measured one.
Next (recommended): decide whether to buy or find a second price source with delisted history (Michael's data vendors?), then re-price the 2011-2020 missing names; otherwise carry 'survivorship-exposed, break-even about 5% wiped out' as the standing caveat and run the C26 percent-drawdown definition (research_docs/LEADS_DIGEST.md open item 1).

**What dd252 is.** 1 - last close / highest close of the 252 sessions before entry (0.55 = 55% below the 12-month high). Median 41%; inside D1 median 67%, and 94% / 82% / 55% of D1 events are down 40% / 50% / 65% or more. Spearman with raw252 -0.72, with ret252 +0.89, with dd504 +0.94.
**Bands of dd252 (excess over SPY, ATR; stock / SPY legs; n; quarter-block 95% range).** Under 30% +0.043 (425); 30-40% +0.002 (200); 40-50% +0.070 (204, range -0.284 to +0.477); 50-60% +0.547 (155; stock +0.777 / SPY +0.231; range +0.132 to +1.005); 60-70% +0.237 (132; range -0.133 to +0.572); 70%+ +0.585 (157; stock +0.831 / SPY +0.246; range +0.085 to +1.076). So the pay is a STEP near 50%, not a gradient: nothing under 50%, and the 60-70% band is weaker than both neighbours (non-monotone).
**Cumulative.** Down at least 50%: +0.468 (n=444, 208 weeks, 37 spells; stock +0.732 / SPY +0.264; beat SPY 62%; range +0.155 to +0.814; without 2008Q4 / 2011Q3 / 2020Q1 +0.638, n=312; 2006-10 +0.622, 2011-20 +0.370), about 2.5 trades a month. At least 40%: +0.343 (648; range +0.081 to +0.640). At least 65% (G4 = 3): +0.488 (220; range +0.001 to +0.939). At least 70%: +0.585 (157).
**Chance menu (6 statistics; excess shuffled inside the entry week, 10,000 draws; n=1,223).** Spearman with dd252 +0.088 (chance alone 0.12%); ret252 +0.095 (0.01%); dd504 +0.106 (0.02%); mean excess of dd252 >= 40% minus the rest +0.270 (0.48%); >= 50% +0.408 (0.22%); >= 65% +0.388 (3.27%). Chance the best of the 6 is this extreme by luck 0.02%. Caution: the six are highly correlated (one lead in six rulers), the tiers come from Entry 52, and the independent sample is tens of spells, so these chances are optimistic.
**Graded points G4 (0 under 40%, 1 for 40-50, 2 for 50-65, 3 for 65%+).** G4=0 +0.030 (625); G4=1 +0.070 (204); G4=2 +0.449 (224; range +0.126 to +0.827); G4=3 +0.488 (220). G4 >= 2 +0.468 (444) vs <= 1 +0.040 (829); difference range +0.035 to +0.771. The continuous comparator Gc (rounded 0..3) gives the same shape: +0.002 / +0.112 / +0.433 / +0.497. Graded points above 2 add nothing; the first point (40-50%) earns about zero. A one-point step at 50% fits the data; the 0/1/2/3 ladder does not.
**G4 vs D1 (the Entry 36 fifth).** D1 and G4 >= 2: +0.646 (n=210; stock +0.890 / SPY +0.244). D1 only (down under 50%): +0.343 (45). G4 >= 2 but not D1: +0.308 (234; stock +0.591 / SPY +0.283). So the percent rule is broader but weaker per trade than D1; the ATR-based D1 carries information the percent drawdown lacks (the high-ATR-depth names fell steadily relative to their normal movement). Inside D1 by dd252 band: under 40% +0.403 (n=15), 40-50% +0.485 (30), 50-60% +0.823 (49), 60-70% +0.172 (50), 70%+ +0.782 (111).
**Score variants (R1 = neither CEO nor CFO among the buyers; N = Nasdaq lagging).** A = R1+N+D1 (Entry 51 reproduces): scores 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 = -0.043 (593) / +0.294 (509) / +0.626 (154) / +1.209 (17); score >= 2 +0.684 (171) vs rest +0.112, difference range +0.179 to +0.898. A' = R1+N+G4 (scores 0-5): -0.190 (365) / +0.291 (346) / +0.136 (240) / +0.452 (207) / +0.746 (97) / +0.600 (18); score >= 2 +0.372 (562) vs rest +0.044, difference range -0.053 to +0.680 (includes zero). Regression excess = a + R1 + N + D1: R1 +0.110 [-0.135, +0.365]; N +0.507 [+0.113, +0.902]; D1 +0.497 [+0.041, +0.859] (= Entry 51). With G4 as a linear term: R1 +0.123 [-0.118, +0.375]; N +0.487 [+0.094, +0.938]; G4 +0.159 per point [-0.013, +0.326]. So swapping D1 for the graded drawdown makes the score worse; N is unchanged.
**Liquidity (G4 >= 2, Entry 54 dollar-volume bands; excess; n).** Under $5M +0.690 (68); $5-10M +0.640 (110); $10-25M +0.492 (107); $25-100M +0.165 (114); $100M+ +0.423 (45). G4 = 3: +0.931 (29) / +0.747 (63) / +0.294 (52) / +0.236 (54) / +0.239 (22). The pay is largest where costs and wipe-out exposure are largest. Derived from these rows, not a script output: $25M and above pooled about +0.24 (n=159).
**Wipe-out break-even (Entry 65 method, terminal loss 55% of the entry open).** G4 >= 2 (n=444, +0.468): 33.4 extra wiped-out events, h = 7.5%, pull the mean to zero; G4 = 3 (n=220, +0.488): 21.0 events, h = 9.6% (Entry 65: D1 6.4%).
**Crash clustering (the declared caution).** G4 >= 2 events by year (n / mean): 2008 98 / +0.24, 2009 47 / +1.48, 2020 88 / +0.43; negative in 2011 (21 / -0.25), 2013, 2014, 2015 (33 / -0.11), 2017 (10 / -0.52). Derived: the three years 2008, 2009 and 2020 hold 233 of the 444 events (52%).
**Reading.** (1) A fixed rule without any ranking exists and shows the same lead: down at least 50% from the 12-month closing high earned +0.468 ATR over SPY (n=444, range +0.155 to +0.814; +0.638 without the three episodes), about 2.5 trades a month. It confirms the deep-fall lead in a second ruler (same stocks as C8 in large part, so one lead, not two). (2) The "score according to the drawdown" does not hold up as a gradient: the effect is a step at about 50%, not a ladder. The second and third points add nothing, the 40-50% band earns about zero, and the 60-70% band is weaker than 50-60% and 70%+ (small bands, noisy). (3) D1 (the ATR-based fall) remains the stronger selector: +0.593 vs +0.468, and the score with D1 beats the score with G4 (+0.684 vs +0.372 for >= 2). Replacing D1 by the percent drawdown loses pay; adding it as a second condition (D1 and down 50%: +0.646, n=210) gains little. The quintile's problem is that its cut came from the sample; the fixed number behind it (about -15 ATR over 12 months, Entry 49 bucket 15+ ATR +0.60) is already an absolute rule and has not been tested as one against the percent rule. (4) Caveats: post hoc (tiers from Entry 52); the six chance statistics are one lead in six rulers; the pay is largest in the thinnest names and in 2008-2009; half of the events come from three years; survivorship (Entry 65) and costs (Entry 45) not in any number except the break-even.
Status: C8 confirmed as a lead in a second definition (C26 dd252 >= 50%); graded 0/1/2/3 points NOT supported (step at 50% instead); D1 not replaced.
Next (recommended): one declared 2x2 (C27): dd252 >= 50% vs a FIXED -15 ATR 12-month fall (not the quintile), each alone and both, in the base rule and inside the score (R1 + N + one deep-fall point), with the dollar-volume bands and the wipe-out break-even, so the deep-fall term in the score is a fixed number with no ranking and we see which of the two rulers carries the pay.

## Entry 67 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C27: which ruler carries the deep-fall pay - fixed percent drawdown P (dd252 >= 50%) vs a FIXED ATR fall F (raw252 <= -15.0 ATR), alone and both, and inside the score R1 + N + one deep-fall term (Michael: "Yeah, go ahead") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL (joins and tabulates, reuses Entry 66 features; returns code as Entry 28); POST HOC and exploratory (menu declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C27 and the docstring before the run; both cut numbers were chosen after Entries 36, 49, 52 and 66)

**The quintile cut is not the issue: a fixed -15 ATR rule is the same group.** F (raw252 <= -15.0 ATR): n=269, +0.599 (stock +0.817 / SPY +0.218; range +0.193 to +0.936). All 255 D1 events are inside F (D1 +0.593); the fifth's cut of -15.32 ATR was simply close to -15. So the deep-fall term already is a fixed number that needs no ranking.
**2x2 (excess over SPY, ATR; stock / SPY legs; n; quarter-block range).** P and F +0.662 (stock +0.914 / SPY +0.252; n=218; range +0.136 to +1.072; without 2008Q4 / 2011Q3 / 2020Q1 +0.681; 2006-10 +0.773, 2011-20 +0.576). P only (down 50%+, fell under 15 ATR) +0.281 (n=226; range -0.009 to +0.696; no episodes +0.582). F only (fell 15+ ATR, down under 50%) +0.333 (n=51; range -0.273 to +0.978; thin). Neither +0.020 (n=778). P alone +0.468 (444); P or F +0.454 (495).
**Chance menu (5 statistics; excess shuffled inside the entry week, 10,000 draws).** P minus rest +0.428 (chance alone 0.38%); F minus rest +0.520 (0.04%); P-and-F minus rest +0.570 (0.28%); best of these three 0.52%. Inside F, P minus not-P +0.328 (chance alone 66.75%; the not-P cell has only 51 events, so this cannot tell whether P adds anything). Inside P, F minus not-F +0.380 (chance alone 0.50%): among stocks down 50%+, the ones that also fell 15+ ATR did clearly better. Same caveat as Entry 66: correlated rulers, post hoc numbers.
**Threshold sensitivity (cumulative, descriptive).** raw252 <= -10 / -12 / -15 / -20 / -25 ATR: +0.467 (481) / +0.513 (390) / +0.599 (269) / +0.673 (156) / +0.565 (91). dd252 >= 40 / 50 / 60 / 70%: +0.343 (648) / +0.468 (444) / +0.426 (289) / +0.585 (157). The ATR measure rises smoothly to -20 ATR; the percent measure is a step at 50% (Entry 66).
**Score = R1 + N + one deep-fall term; score >= 2 vs the rest (quarter-block range of the difference).** S_F +0.710 (n=176, ~0.98 trades a month; range +0.217 to +0.928); S_PF +0.690 (162; +0.179 to +0.895); S_P +0.570 (244; +0.134 to +0.785); S_or +0.590 (258; +0.176 to +0.809); S_2 (P and F as two points) +0.521 (371; +0.055 to +0.805); A = R1+N+D1 (Entry 51) +0.684 (171; +0.179 to +0.898). S_2 by score 0-4: -0.130 (450) / +0.234 (452) / +0.325 (254) / +0.901 (102) / +1.251 (15). S_F by score 0-3: -0.047 (585) / +0.280 (512) / +0.659 (158) / +1.154 (18). Regression excess = a + R1 + N + P + F: R1 +0.116 [-0.128, +0.371]; N +0.498 [+0.112, +0.933]; P +0.262 [-0.120, +0.639]; F +0.359 [-0.078, +0.689] (collinear: each range includes zero once both are in). Alone: P +0.417 [+0.017, +0.796]; F +0.513 [+0.051, +0.881].
**Liquidity (dollar-volume bands; excess; n).** F: under $5M +1.184 (41); $5-10M +0.683 (61); $10-25M +0.662 (56); $25-100M +0.299 (80); $100M+ +0.326 (31). P and F: +1.109 (36) / +0.715 (60) / +0.678 (44) / +0.388 (58) / +0.457 (20). P: +0.690 / +0.640 / +0.492 / +0.165 / +0.423 (Entry 66). Derived from rows, not a script output: $25M and above pooled F about +0.31 (n=111), P-and-F about +0.41 (n=78).
**Wipe-out break-even (Entry 65 method, terminal loss 55%).** F: 17.2 extra wiped-out events, h = 6.4% (n=269); P and F: 19.9 events, h = 9.1% (n=218); P: 33.4 events, h = 7.5% (n=444).
**Crash clustering.** P and F events by year (n / mean): 2008 51 / +0.36, 2009 34 / +1.49, 2020 17 / +0.44; negative in 2013 (4), 2015 (23 / -0.26), 2017 (6 / -0.47). F alone has 54 (2008) and 36 (2009) events; 2020 has 18 for F against 88 for P.
**Reading.** (1) F, a fixed -15 ATR 12-month fall, carries the deep-fall pay: it reproduces D1 (+0.599 vs +0.593), needs no ranking and is the better single rule than the percent drawdown (+0.599 vs +0.468). (2) P adds a possible +0.33 on top of F (P and F +0.662 vs F only +0.333) but the F-only cell has 51 events, so the data cannot say whether it is real; P alone, without F, is clearly weaker (+0.281, range just touching zero). (3) In the score, F gives the same result as D1 (S_F >= 2 +0.710 vs A +0.684); P and F gives +0.690 on fewer trades but a larger wipe-out cushion (9.1% vs 6.4%); P alone is the weakest of the one-term versions (+0.570). (4) Giving P and F one point each (S_2) shows a gradient only at the top (score 3 +0.901, n=102), not a ladder. (5) The pay is largest in the thinnest names (F under $5M +1.18, n=41) and about +0.31 at $25M a day and above; costs (Entry 45) and survivorship (Entry 65) are the open risks. (6) Caveats: post hoc cut numbers; the five statistics are correlated; half the events come from 2008, 2009 and 2020.
Status: C8 / D1 restated as the fixed rule F (down 15+ ATR over 12 months, +0.599, n=269); C26 percent drawdown (P) is a weaker second ruler, with an unproven add-on value on top of F. Still a lead, not an edge.
Next (recommended): decide the deep-fall term for the score (my pick: F alone, with P and F as an optional named stricter version), then run the cost and survivorship tiers by dollar-volume band for F and for P and F together (Entry 45 and Entry 65 methods) before any weights are set, since the pay concentrates under $10M a day.

## Entry 68 | 8 Nov 2026 | Claude | C28: same-day-close entry - if the filing that brings the count to four was ACCEPTED before the close (16:00 ET), enter at that day's close instead of the next open (Michael's request) | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); new returns arm PROVISIONAL (new code, self-tested); baseline arm is the Entry 28 code and reproduces Entry 28 exactly; POST HOC and exploratory; a labelled variant, NOT a change to D-051
Scripts `research/canonical/same_day_close.py` and `research/canonical/acceptance_probe.py`; workflows `.github/workflows/same-day-close.yml` and `.github/workflows/acceptance-probe.yml`; branch `claude/same-day-close-20261108` merged as PR #97 (probe) and PR #99 (test); probe run **42527384557**; test run **42527688355** (events job 128874837306, summary job 128905349302, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42527688355). Inputs reused: primary set from run 42490748366 (Entry 28, `primary_allowed`), SEC database and price archive in R2. Development entries (<= 2020-12-31) and the 2026 pilot only (D-068); no 2021-2025 entry was fetched or priced. The menu was declared in the script docstring before any number was computed.
**Source of the time.** The bulk database holds a filing DATE only (D-051 says timestamps are not needed for the standing rule). The probe (run 42527384557) showed GitHub Actions can read the SGML header ACCEPTANCE-DATETIME (Eastern local time) of the original filings: 24 of 24 sampled filings from 2006-2020 returned one; the test then read the headers of all 10,685 trigger-date filings (HTTP Range request, SEC_USER_AGENT secret, about 1.7 requests a second, 6,300 seconds, 0 missing).
**Event set unchanged.** Selection is still by filing date; the refined selector (D-064, `find_events_refined`) produced 4,712 allowed events, every one with an acceptance time, every buyer count matching the selector, and a crossing filing found in all of them. The crossing filing is the one that brings the distinct-buyer count to four when the trigger-date filings are taken in acceptance-time order on top of the buyers already public. Only the entry price and time of events whose fourth buyer's filing was accepted before the cutoff change.
**Rules (declared first).** Rule A (Michael's): filing date is a session and acceptance before 16:00:00 ET -> enter at that day's close; otherwise the baseline next open. Rule B (sensitivity): cutoff 15:50:00 ET. Exit = close of the session 21 sessions after the entry session, which is the SAME exit session the baseline uses for the same event (baseline enters at the next open and exits at the close of its 21st session), so the variant holds one extra overnight. ATR = 14 sessions completed before the filing session (the baseline's ATR ends one session later). SPY on the same window in the stock's ATR units; excess = stock minus SPY. Price only, no costs.
**Timing of the crossing filing (1,453 allowed primary entries).** Accepted after 16:00 on the filing date: 824 (57%); intraday 09:30-16:00: 500; before 09:30: 127; filing date not a session: 2. So 43% of events can change.
**Baseline arm reproduces Entry 28.** Development n=1,372, stock +0.348, SPY +0.194, excess +0.154; pilot n=80, excess +0.333 (as Entry 28).
**Development 2006-2020, Rule A (excess over SPY in ATR; stock / SPY legs; n).** Events that change: 605 of 1,372 (439 issuers, 481 entry dates, 311 entry weeks). On those same events: baseline next open +0.235 (+0.400 / +0.165) vs variant close +0.287 (+0.448 / +0.161). Paired difference (variant minus baseline) +0.052 ATR, week-block 95% range +0.026 to +0.079; in stock return +0.36% mean, +0.20% median. The close of the filing day to the next open averaged +0.31% (median +0.19%): stocks tended to gap UP overnight after the filing, which is what the close entry saves. Unchanged events (767, accepted after the close) +0.091. Whole set under the rule: +0.177 (+0.369 / +0.192) vs baseline +0.154. By time of day (changed events, baseline -> variant): before the open n=121 +0.273 -> +0.341; intraday n=484 +0.225 -> +0.273.
**Rule B (15:50).** 586 events change; baseline arm +0.226 -> variant +0.280; paired +0.054 [+0.028, +0.083]; whole set +0.177. Essentially Rule A.
**Eras and episodes (whole set under Rule A vs baseline).** 2006-10 +0.347 -> +0.374 (n=428); 2011-20 +0.067 -> +0.088 (n=944); without 2008Q4/2011Q3/2020Q1 +0.309 -> +0.334 (n=1,097). Same sign in both eras.
**2026 pilot (in-sample, thin).** 21 of 80 change; paired -0.030 [-0.117, +0.061], gap -0.10% mean (median 0.00%). No gain visible; far too few events to tell.
**Reading.** (1) The idea works in the direction expected in the development years: entering at the close when the fourth buyer's filing is already public adds about 0.05 ATR (about 0.4% of price) on the 44% of events it touches, about +0.02 ATR on the whole set; the gain is almost exactly the average overnight gap (+0.31%). (2) It is small next to the cost range (0.10-0.30 ATR round trip, Entries 28/45) and one tail event can move it, so it does not turn a lead into an edge. (3) Separate observation, NOT tested: events accepted before the close did better at the baseline too (+0.235 on 605) than events accepted after it (+0.091 on 767), a post hoc split that needs a chance test before it means anything. (4) Cautions: the close fill is an assumption (a filing accepted at 15:58 cannot realistically be filled at the 16:00 print; Rule B only partly covers that); the pilot shows no gain; the variant's ATR window and hold length differ by one session from the baseline; survivor bias and missing prices of the baseline carry over; best of 2 declared rules, which are nearly the same rule. Status: C28 a small lead, post hoc, development-years only.
**Next (recommended), after Entry 67.** Options: (a) keep the next open as the primary entry (D-051) and record the close entry as an optional execution refinement for the live scanner, which sees the acceptance time anyway - recommended, because the gain is small and rests on an assumed closing fill; (b) adopt the close entry in the research rules (needs Michael's decision and a D-number, since it supersedes D-051 for pre-close filings); (c) test the before-close versus after-close split on its own with a chance test. The standing next steps are unchanged: Michael's open items in research_docs/MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS.md item 1, the deep-fall term for the score, and cost and survivorship tiers by dollar-volume band.

Stops, F (n=269), price only, 252-session cap, fills at the stop or the open on a gap:
- T20 (2 ATR initial, 20-session low trail): mean R +0.544, median R -0.216, win 37%, avg win +2.25R, avg loss -0.47R, PF 2.86, worst -1.11R. Stock +1.088 ATR (+18.2% mean, -2.7% median) vs SPY +0.197, excess +0.891; net of 0.10 / 0.25 ATR cost +0.988 / +0.838 (stock). Held mean 26.8 sessions; exits trail 231, gap 26, initial 12. Stock range [+0.692, +2.271]. 2006-10 +2.299 (n=108), 2011-20 +0.903 (n=161), no episodes +1.342 (n=228).
- T20 2006-10 +1.218, 2011-20 +1.002, no episodes +0.682.
- No-stop controls on F: close_21 +0.817, close_42 +1.559 (SPY +0.218, +0.435). So on F neither stop beats the fixed 2-month hold in stock ATR (+1.559 vs +1.464 A4); T20 trades about 27 sessions for +1.088 vs +0.817 at 21 sessions.
- ALL (n=1,275): T20 stock +0.334 ATR, excess +0.157 (net +0.234 / +0.084 stock at cost); A4 stock +0.698, SPY +0.581, excess +0.117. NOT F: T20 excess -0.039, A4 excess -0.092. SF2: T20 +0.894 (excess +0.676), A4 +1.270 (excess +0.865).
