F by dollar-volume band, 1 month (n; close_21 stock ATR, SPY): under $5M (41; +1.540, +0.355), $5-10M (61; +0.856, +0.174), $10-25M (56; +0.855, +0.193), $25-100M (80; +0.476, +0.177), $100M+ (31; +0.598, +0.272). Median MAE_21 widens with size (-0.73 to -1.76 ATR).

Reading (not a claim of edge): the F pay is not a stop artefact; a stop cuts the average loss to about -0.5R but the win rate falls to 37% (T20) or 54% (A4) because the trail exits most trades early, so the pay comes from a minority of large winners (median stock return under the stop is negative for T20). Wide stops (4 ATR) cut few winners (3% of up-closers went -4 ATR against at 2 months; 2% at 1 month), so a stop at 3-4 ATR costs little relative to no stop; a 2 ATR stop would have cut 10% (1 month) to 21% (2 months) of the winners. Gaps hit 26-28 of 269 trades (10%), so fills at the stop price are optimistic. Sample: 269 events; effective independent weeks not recomputed here; F is post hoc; crashes dominate the sample (Entries 66-67); the archive holds almost no wipe-outs (Entry 65), so real worst cases are worse than the rows above; costs were not applied to the excursion tables; the 0.25 ATR cost columns assume a flat per-trade cost and thin names (<$10M a day) carry most of the pay. Status: lead, post hoc, NEW CODE.

Next (not done): decide the exit rule for F (hold 21, hold 42, or A4) before any out-of-sample use; cost tiers by dollar-volume band; second price source for delisted history.

## Entry 70 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | C30: realistic trading costs for the deep-fall rule F (published spread table + impact law + spreads estimated from the archive's own bars) and a 35-cell stop x holding-period grid (Michael: "can you do research and find out what the real liquidity costs are on stocks below $10 million ... use a stop ... did 2 months turn out to be the best? And should we try more stops?") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL (new spread, cost and grid code; entry, ATR and cohort conventions as Entries 28 and 66-69; reproduces Entry 69 plain 21 and 42 exactly); every COST is a MODEL ESTIMATE (no quote or fill data exists in the repo); POST HOC and exploratory (menu declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C30 and the script docstring before the run)

Script `research/canonical/cost_stops_f.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/cost-stops-f.yml` (id 411932459), branch `claude/cost-stops-20261109` merged as PR #117, run **42599117568** (summary job 129099982312, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42599117568). Development entries only (D-068); no 2021+ entry simulated; no sealed return read. Cohort n=1,275, F n=269, SF2 n=176. Reproduction: F plain 21 stock +0.817 / SPY +0.218, plain 42 +1.559 / +0.435 = Entry 69.
**Sources for the cost model (web, 9 Nov 2026).** (1) Collver, SEC Division of Trading and Markets, "A characterization of market quality for small capitalization US equities" (Sept 2014, 2013 data), Tables 4-5: mean relative effective spread by price row and market-cap bin with the mean daily dollar volume of each bin, e.g. $6-9.99 stocks at $4.2M a day 0.144%, at $1.5M 0.237%, at $0.5M 0.52%; $10-19.99 at $3.9M 0.139%, at $9.7M 0.088%. (2) Toth, Eisler, Bouchaud et al. square-root impact law I = Y sigma sqrt(Q/V), Y of order one (arXiv 1602.03043; Physical Review X 2011). (3) Frazzini, Israel, Moskowitz, "Trading Costs": market impact about 9 bps on average, almost 19 bps for small caps, for a patient institutional trader. (4) Corwin-Schultz (2012) and Abdi-Ranaldo (2017) estimators (mean absolute error of the high-low estimator about 1% against TAQ effective spreads). (5) Challenge searches: Bessembinder 2003 (post-decimalization effective spreads about 0.33% of price, volume-weighted); a Nasdaq analysis (a doubling of volatility goes with roughly +50% in spreads); BLS PPI for dealer equity transactions (up tens of percent in crisis months). No source found for spreads of deep-fallen stocks specifically.
**F profile.** Entry price median $10.20; ATR as % of price median 7.3% (quartiles 4.8-11.9%); 15-session dollar volume median $16.5M; under $5M n=41, $5-10M 61, $10-25M 56, $25-100M 80, $100M+ 31. In the three episodes (n=41) ATR is 17.4% of price (outside 6.4%).
**A/B. Spread evidence.** Published 2013 table, matched to each event's price and dollar volume: full effective spread mean 0.14% of price = 0.019 ATR round trip (by band, under $5M to $100M+: 0.026 / 0.018 / 0.019 / 0.017 / 0.016 ATR). Estimated from this archive's daily bars (F, 20 sessions before entry / sessions 1-21 / 22-42): Corwin-Schultz floored 1.65% / 1.74% / 1.66%; unfloored -0.14% / +0.25% / +0.28%; Abdi-Ranaldo 1.25% / 1.24% / 1.15%. These estimators cannot resolve a spread this small next to a 7% daily range: the floored version is biased up by the noise and the unfloored one is centred near zero, so they bracket the truth between about 0% and 1.7% and are NOT a measurement; they are shown because the instruction was to check the archive. Floored CS in the episodes 2.70% / 3.11% (outside 1.47% / 1.49%), a hint of wider spreads in crashes, but ATR is also 2.7 times larger there, so a given spread is fewer ATR.
**C. Round-trip cost in ATR (F; scenarios declared first).** Entry 45 price tiers (0.5% / 1% / 2% / 4% of price) = mean 0.234 ATR; flat 0.10 / 0.25 used in Entries 28, 45, 69. MILD (table x1 + impact at 0.1% of volume, Y 0.5) mean 0.043 ATR (0.37% of price); REALISTIC (table x2 + impact at 1% of volume, Y 0.5) 0.106 ATR (0.92%; p90 0.141); HARSH (max of table x2 and the floored bar estimate, impact at 5% of volume, Y 1) 0.500 ATR (4.55%) - HARSH is driven by the noise-biased floored estimate, so it is an upper bound, not an estimate. Impact alone per round trip: 0.021 ATR (0.1% of volume), 0.067 (1%), 0.149 (5%); with Y = 1: 0.133 (1%), 0.298 (5%). In REALISTIC about two thirds of the cost is impact, not spread, so position size matters more than the quoted spread. 1% of daily volume = $37K (under $5M band median), $65K ($5-10M), $159K ($10-25M), $491K ($25-100M).
**C2. Plain holds on F, excess over SPY gross -> net (MILD / REALISTIC / HARSH).** 21 sessions +0.599 -> +0.556 / +0.493 / +0.100; 42 +1.123 -> +1.080 / +1.017 / +0.623; 63 +1.122 -> +1.079 / +1.016 / +0.622; 84 +1.089 -> +1.046 / +0.983 / +0.589; 126 +1.412 -> +1.369 / +1.306 / +0.912. By dollar-volume band, 21 sessions gross -> REALISTIC / HARSH: under $5M (41) +1.184 -> +1.064 / +0.692; $5-10M (61) +0.683 -> +0.579 / +0.172; $10-25M (56) +0.662 -> +0.557 / +0.157; $25-100M (80) +0.299 -> +0.195 / -0.200; $100M+ (31) +0.326 -> +0.227 / -0.159. At 42 sessions: +1.763 -> +1.643 / +1.271; +2.050 -> +1.947 / +1.540; +1.069 -> +0.964 / +0.564; +0.677 -> +0.574 / +0.179; -0.299 -> -0.399 / -0.784.
**D. Grid on F (excess over SPY, ATR; 21 / 42 / 63 / 84 / 126 sessions).** none +0.599 / +1.123 / +1.122 / +1.089 / +1.412; fixed 3 ATR +0.564 / +0.960 / +0.771 / +0.869 / +1.186; fixed 5 +0.612 / +1.012 / +0.893 / +0.847 / +1.243; trail 3 +0.484 / +0.693 / +0.721 / +0.719 / +0.728; trail 4 +0.604 / +0.924 / +0.815 / +0.675 / +0.756; trail 5 +0.609 / +0.895 / +0.798 / +0.607 / +0.767; trail 6 +0.589 / +1.021 / +0.842 / +0.709 / +0.854. No stop beats the plain hold at 42 sessions or longer; at 21 sessions fixed 5 is +0.013 above plain (noise). Stopped out at 42 sessions: fixed 3 24%, fixed 5 9%, trail 3 70%, trail 4 42%, trail 5 27%, trail 6 15%. Tail, plain hold: worst loss -10.0 / -9.1 / -12.4 / -15.0 / -21.2 ATR and 7% / 9% / 16% / 20% / 25% of trades ending worse than -3 ATR at 21 / 42 / 63 / 84 / 126 sessions; the stops cap the worst loss at about -3.4 to -6 ATR (-8.7 for fixed 5 at 84 sessions or more; fills at the stop price, gaps at the open). Net of REALISTIC cost the ranking is unchanged (plain 42 +1.017, trail 6 at 42 +0.915).
**Is 2 months the best hold?** Excess: 21 sessions +0.599, 42 +1.123, 63 +1.122, 84 +1.089, 126 +1.412. The pay builds over about two months and then is flat to 4 months; 6 months is higher but only because of 2006-10. Era split (n 108 / 161): the best cell in 2006-10 (plain 126, +3.152) is +0.245 in 2011-20 (rank 6 of 35); the best cell in 2011-20 (plain 84, +0.676) is +1.705 in 2006-10 (rank 27 of 35); Spearman of the 35 cell means between the eras -0.19 (SF2: -0.41, n 83 / 93). So no cell can be called best from this data; 2 months is where the curve stops rising, not a proven optimum. Week-block 95% ranges of the mean excess: plain 21 [+0.282, +0.896]; plain 42 [+0.616, +1.650]; best cell (plain 126) [+0.325, +2.588].
**SF2 grid (n=176), excess none / fixed 5 / trail 6 at 21, 42, 63, 84, 126.** none +0.710 / +0.931 / +0.956 / +0.711 / +1.187; fixed 5 +0.723 / +0.940 / +0.934 / +0.746 / +1.045; trail 6 +0.708 / +0.923 / +0.947 / +0.762 / +1.024; tighter stops lower (trail 3 +0.530 to +0.715). Same reading as F.
**Reading.** (1) Michael's objection stands for the earlier cost numbers: a flat quarter ATR (about 1.8% of price at F's median ATR of 7.3%) or the tier table (mean 0.234 ATR) is several times what published effective spreads imply for stocks trading $3M or more a day (about 0.1-0.3% of price, 0.02 ATR); the flat figures were assumptions without data and Entry 45's conclusion that costs leave "near +0.07" rested on them. (2) With the model costs the F lead keeps most of its gross pay: +0.49 (21 sessions) and +1.02 (42 sessions) net of REALISTIC, still positive in the under $5M and $5-10M bands under HARSH. Cost is therefore not what separates the lead from an edge; the binding doubts are survivorship and wipe-outs (Entry 65: only a second price source with delisted history settles it), concentration in 2008-09 and 2020 (Entries 66-67), and that F is post hoc. (3) The model cannot see: real quotes and fills in these names (especially at the opening auction, where the study buys), halts, wider spreads in crashes beyond the x2 allowance, position size (impact dominates the spread above about 0.5% of daily volume), and stop slippage in thin names. (4) Stops: on F they cost pay and do not improve the plain hold; their value is capping the worst loss, and the wide ones (fixed 5 ATR, trail 6 ATR, about 36% and 44% of price at the median ATR) cost about 0.1 ATR of excess at 42 sessions for that. A 3-4 ATR trailing stop gives up 0.2-0.4 ATR. (5) The hold-length question has no robust answer in 15 years of data. Status: C30 descriptive and post hoc; F stays a lead; costs are now a model range (0.04-0.11 ATR) rather than an assumption of 0.10-0.25.
**Next (recommended).** (a) Decide the exit rule for F in Michael's words: plain 42-session hold, with or without a wide protective stop (fixed 5 ATR or trail 6 ATR) as tail insurance against the wipe-outs the archive cannot show; my pick is plain 42 plus the 5 ATR fixed stop as an option, because it is the cheapest cap on the loss tail. (b) Get a second price source with delisted history (Entry 65), the one input that can still kill the lead. (c) Replace the spread model with real fills: a small paper or live record of opening-auction fills in F-type names would pin the cost down better than any table.

## Entry 73 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | C32 RESULT: buy-stop at the 10-session high after the four-buyer signal versus buying at the next open (Michael: "after the fourth person buys, wait to buy on a ten day high stop") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL (self-tested fill logic; baseline reproduces Entries 69-70 exactly); POST HOC and exploratory (menu declared in research_docs/CANDIDATES.md C32 and the script docstring before the run); gross of cost

Script `research/canonical/breakout_entry.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/breakout-entry.yml` (id 411957080), run **42602611311** (summary job 129126478371 after the billing block of Entry 72; https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42602611311), branch main. Development entries only (D-068). Reproduction: deep-fall baseline excess +0.599 at 21 sessions and +1.123 at 42 (n=269) = Entries 69-70.
Rule: buy-stop at the highest high of the 10 sessions before the entry session, live 5, 10 or 20 sessions from the entry session, filled at the level or at the open if gapped above; hold 21 or 42 sessions from the fill; unfilled signals skipped. Excess over SPY in ATR, signal-ATR units.
**Deep-fall rule (down 15+ ATR over 12 months), 42-session hold.** Baseline (next open) +1.123. Wait 5: filled 69 of 269 (26%), breakout +1.229 on the filled versus +2.026 for the same trades at the next open (difference -0.797, week-block range [-1.029, -0.570]); unfilled signals' baseline +0.812 (n=200); per signal with unfilled as cash +0.315 versus +1.123. Wait 10: filled 100 (37%), +0.862 vs +2.059 (-1.197, [-1.534, -0.845]); unfilled baseline +0.569; per signal +0.321. Wait 20: filled 135 (50%), +0.547 vs +2.008 (-1.462, [-1.812, -1.096]); unfilled baseline +0.231; per signal +0.274. The buy-stop level sat +0.75 / +1.16 / +1.58 ATR above the entry open (wait 5 / 10 / 20); mean wait 1.0 / 3.0 / 5.7 sessions; gap fills 12-14%.
**21-session hold:** baseline +0.599; per signal breakout +0.141 / +0.160 / +0.127 (wait 5 / 10 / 20); on the filled trades the difference to the next-open entry is -0.606 / -0.835 / -1.272, every week-block range below zero.
**Score subset (n=176), 42 sessions:** baseline +0.931; per signal breakout +0.263 / +0.372 / +0.422; difference on filled -0.629 / -0.934 / -1.162. **ALL signals (n=1,275), 42 sessions:** baseline +0.209; per signal +0.062 / +0.051 / -0.024; fill rate 25% / 33% / 43%. **Outside the deep-fall rule (n=1,006), 42 sessions:** baseline -0.035; per signal -0.006 / -0.021 / -0.104.
**Reading.** The breakout wait is worse than the next-open entry in every group, hold, and wait length, with a week-block range entirely below zero on the filled trades. Two reasons visible in the numbers: (1) the trades that break the 10-session high quickly are the ones that bounced right away, so the next-open entry catches the move (those same trades earn about +2.0 at the next open) while the buy-stop pays 0.75 to 1.6 ATR more; (2) the wait skips signals that still earn money (unfilled baseline +0.23 to +0.81 on the deep-fall rule). No cost was applied; thin-name slippage on a stop fill would make the breakout arm worse. Sample: 269 signals; post hoc; one entry-timing idea, not a search. Status: NOT SUPPORTED as an improvement; the next-open entry stands.

Script `research/canonical/delisting_check.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/delisting-check.yml` (id 411990520), run **42607866511** (job 129128331336, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42607866511), branch main. Source: SEC submissions index per company (2,059 companies; all found), SEC_USER_AGENT. Population: 4,462 refined development events (3,608 priced, 854 no-price = 19.1% here). Classes first-match: 8-K Item 1.03 bankruptcy or receivership; acquired (Item 2.01, or merger/tender document plus Form 15/25); delisted or deregistered (Item 3.01 or Form 15/25); active.
**Within 59 days of entry (about the 42-session hold).** Priced (n=3,608): bankruptcy 0, acquired 133 (3.7%), delisted/deregistered 68 (1.9%), active 94.4%. No-price (n=854): bankruptcy 0, acquired 31 (3.6%), delisted/deregistered 41 (4.8%), active 91.6%. **Zero of 4,462 events** had an Item 1.03 filing within 59 days; 3 had one within 120 days (priced 2, no-price 1).
**Within 365 days.** Priced: bankruptcy 19 (0.5%), acquired 569 (15.8%), delisted/deregistered 273 (7.6%). No-price: bankruptcy 23 (2.7%), acquired 144 (16.9%), delisted/deregistered 116 (13.6%), active 66.9%. By entry year the no-price bankruptcies within 365 days: 2007 1, 2008 17, 2009 2, 2011 1, 2019 2, all other years 0 (priced events: 2008 7, 2011 3, 2019 3, 2007, 2009, 2013, 2015, 2016, 2017 one each); no-price counts per year as in Entry 38 (2008: 208).
**Bound section.** B, the no-price events bankrupt within 59 days, is 0 (all years and 2011-20), so every scenario returns the unchanged deep-fall excess (+1.123, and +0.600 for 2011-20). Spot-check lists of class-1 tickers within 59 days are empty.
**Reading.** (1) Chapter 11 or receivership filings inside the 42-session hold are essentially absent from the whole signal population, priced or not (0 of 4,462; the simple upper bound if the true rate were higher is about 3 in 4,462, 0.07%). The wipe-out rate Entry 65 had to ASSUME at 1-5% is, for bankruptcies, measured near zero; the break-even of about 5% is far away. (2) The missing events are not where the bankruptcies are inside the hold, but they do hold more failures later: within 365 days 2.7% of no-price events went bankrupt versus 0.5% of priced, 17 of 23 in 2008, which is after a 42-session hold would have ended. (3) NOT covered: a bank closed by regulators and a holding company that simply stops filing (no 1.03), and sudden collapses without a bankruptcy 8-K. The pocket that remains: 41 no-price events (and 68 priced ones, whose prices the archive does hold) delisted or deregistered within 59 days with no bankruptcy filing; these mix buy-outs, going-private deals, shells and possibly bank failures. Entry 65: about 9 to 12 deep-fall wipe-outs would be needed to erase the edge; whether any of the 41 were deep-fall names above the $3M floor is not measured here. (4) The result lowers the survivorship risk; it does not close it. Next: classify the 41 (and the 2008-09 no-price names) by name, e.g. which are banks, and check the no-price delisted/deregistered group against a list of bank failures if one can be obtained. Status: survivorship risk reduced, not cleared; lead stays post hoc.

## Entry 75 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | Sizing ladder, second version (Michael: base position 0.25% volatility; how much to add per layer: no CEO/CFO, Nasdaq lead/lag, down 15 ATR, and any other edge) | PROVISIONAL (derived from logged numbers in Entries 51, 64, 65, 67, 69, 70, 72-74; no new code, no workflow run, no new return computed); sizing multiples are Claude's judgment, not a test result and not a Michael determination; POST HOC leads throughout

Replaces the ladder in Entry 64 (a new entry, Entry 64 is not edited). Branch `claude/sizing-ladder-v2-20261109` (documentation only). No script and no run of its own; every number below is read from: Entry 51 run 42516534558 (score by points), Entry 67 run 42536840085 (F, P, N, score S_F, liquidity, break-evens), Entry 70 run 42599117568 (costs, stops), Entry 72 run 42602755958 (year by year), Entry 74 run 42607866511 (bankruptcy/delisting counts).
**Unit.** U = 0.25% of equity per 1 ATR of the stock's move (position dollars = 0.25% of equity / (ATR as a fraction of price)), 21-session hold as in the rules in force. Multiples hold for any base.
**Method.** Size proportional to the expected net excess, with each logged excess (a) halved for post hoc selection (Entry 64's judgment, not measured), (b) reduced by the Entry 70 round-trip cost model (0.04 mild to 0.11 realistic ATR), (c) capped by total risk and by a dollar-volume limit. Proportional-to-edge sizing assumes similar variance per ATR across groups; no variance by group was computed here, and the deep-fall group has fat tails (plain worst about -9 ATR at 42 sessions, -4 ATR excursion in 16% of trades within 2 months; Entry 69-70), which argues for the low end.
**Net expected excess after shrink and realistic cost (ATR over SPY, 21 sessions).** Cluster only (score 0 in Entry 51, n=595: -0.041 raw; whole base rule +0.154 raw) about 0 or slightly negative. One of N or F (raw +0.596 / +0.599; n=255 / 269): about +0.19. N and D1 together (raw +1.434, n=52): about +0.61 (proportional math would say about 3x the single layer, capped below). R1 (no CEO and no CFO): +0.11 raw, range [-0.13, +0.37]: about 0. P (down 50%+) on top of F: +0.33 raw, chance alone 66.75% (Entry 67): about 0.
**Ladder (total size, multiples of U).** Cluster only: 0.5U (token, or skip; no demonstrated net edge). F only or N only: 1.5U (add +1.0U). F and N together: 3.0U (second layer adds +1.5U; HARD CAP, the group is 52 events and the score-3 cell 17 events in 6 spells). Zero extra for: no CEO/CFO, no CFO alone, P on top of F, basing (C23), Nasdaq-leading side, purchase size, director count, titles, SPY regime. If forced to rank the two paid layers, F ranks above N (F has a stated mechanism and chance rate 0.04%; N has no mechanism and is weaker in 2011-2020), e.g. F +1.25U / N +0.75U, same 3.0U total.
**Limits.** (1) Dollar volume is a capacity limit, not a bonus: position dollars at most 1% of average daily dollar volume (the Entry 70 impact assumption); the F pay is largest under $5M a day (+1.18, n=41) where this cap binds first. (2) Total open size at most 8U (2% of equity per 1 ATR), because F signals cluster in crashes (2008 54, 2009 36 events); this cap is judgment and overlapping positions have NOT been simulated. (3) Loss arithmetic (mine): at 3.0U (0.75% per ATR) a plain-hold -9 ATR trade is -6.75% of equity, a 5 ATR fixed stop capping the worst near -6 ATR gives -4.5%; at 1.5U they are -3.4% and -2.25%. (4) Exit rule for F still undecided by Michael (plain 42-session hold with optional 5 ATR stop recommended in Entry 70); the ladder is unchanged by that choice, the expected excess is larger at 42 sessions (+1.12 vs +0.60 raw).
**What moved since Entry 64.** Costs lower than assumed (helps the thin leads); zero bankruptcy filings inside the hold in 4,462 events (Entry 74) lowers the wipe-out risk but does not close it; F is near zero in 2011-2017 at 42 sessions (+0.136, n=97, Entry 72) and 2008, 2009, 2020 hold more than half of the pooled pay (weakens it); breakout entry and stops do not improve (Entries 70, 73). Net: the 0.5 / 1.5 / 3.0 ladder stands.
Status: sizing proposal, not a tested result. Nothing here has been run on held-out data; the sealed years stay closed.
Next (recommended): simulate the ladder as a portfolio on the committed development paths (overlapping positions, the 8U cap and the 1% volume cap) to see the real peak open risk and the worst drawdown in 2008-09 and 2020; declare the test first. Then Michael decides the F exit rule and whether to buy a second price source.

**Reading.** The missing 2008-2009 banks are not a hidden cluster of failures inside the hold: 85% of them (117 of 137) were still filing a year later and none matched a failed bank within 59 days. The name match is conservative (a holding company with a different name from its bank would be missed), so this lowers the worry without clearing it. The remaining pocket is small and sits in 2008-2009 non-bank micro-caps: 16 with a delisting, deregistration or bankruptcy filing inside 59 days, mostly exchange deficiency notices; whether any were deep-fall names above the $3M floor, and what the stock did afterwards, needs prices. Entry 65: about 9 to 12 deep-fall wipe-outs would be needed to erase the edge. Status: survivorship risk reduced to a small, bounded pocket; not cleared; the standing caveat stays ('survivorship-exposed, 2008-09 micro-caps unpriced'). A second price source would only be needed for that pocket (about 40 tickers), not a full vendor feed.

## Entry 77 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | CORRECTION to the sizing-ladder entry (the FIRST of the two entries numbered 75 on 9 Nov, the one headed "Sizing ladder, second version"; the numbers 75 and 76 were taken twice by parallel chats): "cluster only" was mislabelled, the halving was applied to the base rule when it should not be, and R1 was given zero when its point estimate is small and positive (Michael challenged all three) | PROVISIONAL (derived from logged numbers, no new code, no run); sizing multiples are Claude's judgment

The sizing-ladder entry is not edited; this entry replaces its ladder. Inputs read: Entry 51 run 42516534558, Entry 64, Entry 67 run 42536840085, Entry 70 run 42599117568.
**1. "Cluster only" was the wrong label.** That entry used the score-0 cell of Entry 51 (n=595, -0.041): cluster events with NONE of R1, N, D1. That is the remainder left after the flagged groups are pulled out, so it looks worse by construction (the flags were found after looking at the data). The whole base rule (every four-buyer cluster, n=1,372) is +0.154 raw, gross. The -0.041 must not be used to size down the base position. Both numbers are true; they are different groups.
**2. Halving.** The haircut (half the measured excess, Entry 64's judgment, not measured) is for leads found by searching the data (N, F, R1, P). The base rule is Michael's declared hypothesis (D-064/065/066/068), so it is not halved. Base +0.154 raw minus realistic cost 0.11 = about +0.04 (mild cost 0.04: +0.11). Cost for the base-rule names, which are more liquid than F, was not tiered (Entry 70 priced F only); the net is thin but not negative.
**3. R1 (no CEO and no CFO).** Raw alone +0.281 (n=358, Entry 64); adjusted for N and D1 +0.108 [-0.137, +0.364] (Entry 51), +0.116 in Entry 67. Point estimate positive, range includes zero, most of the raw gap is overlap with N and D1. A noisy estimate is shrunk harder, so "zero" was a heavy shrink and not a finding of no edge. Marginal shrunk edge +0.055 against about +0.25 for N or F, which is about +0.2U.
**Replacement ladder (total size in multiples of U = 0.25% per ATR).** Base cluster 1.0U. Add N +1.0U, F +1.0U, R1 +0.25U, P on top of F +0.25U (unproven, chance 66.75%, Entry 67). Hard cap 3.0U total (N and F together already reach it). Limits from the sizing-ladder entry unchanged (1% of daily dollar volume, 8U total open, loss arithmetic).
Next (recommended): the portfolio simulation named in the sizing-ladder entry, declared first.

## Entry 78 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | Continuous sizing: each layer added in proportion to its own edge, no rounding (Michael: "scale it exactly according to the edge"; replaces the unit ladder in Entry 77) | PROVISIONAL (new arithmetic script run locally, NO workflow run and NO run link; inputs are logged numbers from Entries 51 and 67; no return computed); weights are Claude's judgment on post hoc inputs, not a test result and not a Michael determination

Script `research/canonical/sizing_weights.py` (branch `claude/sizing-continuous-20261109`), run locally with `python3 -I`; reads no price data. Inputs: Entry 67 regression R1+N+P+F (run 42536840085) and Entry 51 regression R1+N+D1 (run 42516534558), estimates and quarter-block 95% ranges as logged.
**Method.** SE of a term = range width / (2 x 1.96). Noise reliability w = est^2 / (est^2 + SE^2). Selection haircut 0.5 on found-by-search layers (Entry 64's judgment, not measured; it sets the overall scale only, the relative weights come from est and w). Add-on in U = 0.5 x w x est / 0.25 = 2 x w x est ATR. Base cluster = 1.0U, not haircut (Entry 77). Additive total.
**Add-ons (Entry 67 model).** R1 +0.11U (est +0.116, SE 0.127, w 0.45); N +0.85U (+0.498, 0.209, 0.85); P +0.34U (+0.262, 0.194, 0.65); F +0.55U (+0.359, 0.196, 0.77). **Entry 51 model:** R1 +0.09U; N +0.87U; D1 +0.84U. Totals (U; per-ATR risk): cluster only 1.00 (0.250%); N 1.85 (0.462%); F 1.55; P 1.34; R1+N 1.95; N+F 2.40; P+F 1.89; R1+N+F 2.51; N+P+F 2.74; all four 2.84 (0.711%); Entry 51 model N+D1 2.71, R1+N+D1 2.80. All flag combinations are in the script output. No separate 3.0U cap is needed: the largest total is 2.84U.
**Graded fall (derived from the cumulative threshold table in Entry 67, not a script output of the study; raw, not adjusted for N or R1; ranges not computed).** Excess by depth of the 12-month fall: 10-12 ATR n=91 +0.270; 12-15 n=121 +0.322; 15-20 n=113 +0.497; 20-25 n=65 +0.824; more than 25 n=91 +0.565. The edge rises with depth to about 25 ATR and then eases. Not used for sizing yet: post hoc, unadjusted, ranges unknown.
**Limits unchanged.** 1% of average daily dollar volume per position; 8U total open (not simulated); worst-case loss arithmetic scales with the per-ATR risk (at 0.71% per ATR a plain -9 ATR trade is about -6.4% of equity, a 5 ATR stop capping near -6 ATR about -4.3%).
Status: sizing proposal. Next (recommended): declare then run a graded-fall regression (excess on fall depth, N and R1, with ranges) so the fall layer can be sized by depth instead of a step; then the portfolio simulation with the caps.

## Entry 84 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | PROPOSAL: top 10 research ideas to raise or falsify the edge (Michael: "look at every variable we have not changed since the beginning") | CONFIRMED (documentation only; no code run, no numbers produced); every idea is UNTESTED

No script, no run. Read: LEADS_DIGEST, AGENTS, MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS, Entries 80-83. Facts relied on (from those entries): base rule +0.154 ATR over SPY gross (Entry 28); flagged-only portfolio profit sits 58% in 2008-09 and 2020 (Entry 83, run 42627292601); deep fall other-years +0.378 (n=160) vs 2008-09 +1.803 (n=90) (Entry 83); wipe-out rate is the main open risk (Entry 65).
Ideas, ranked by information gained per unit of work (proposed menu for a future declared exploration; none chosen by Michael yet):
1. Matched-control test: does the four-buyer cluster add anything beyond "stock that fell a lot"? Controls matched on 12-month fall, liquidity, date, beta, no cluster.
2. Beta and factor decomposition of the excess; beta-hedged version (index futures) of the same trades.
3. Dose-response below four buyers (1, 2, 3, 4, 5+ distinct buyers) and its interaction with deep fall.
4. Solvency screen from SEC XBRL (point-in-time by filing date) inside the deep-fall group, aimed at the wipe-out risk.
5. Insider-level history: first purchase in years, prior track record, bought-after-selling (opportunistic vs routine, beyond D-064).
6. Cluster tempo: days from first to fourth purchase, purchase-to-filing lag, price move since the first purchase.
7. Earnings-cycle position and where in the hold the excess is earned (earnings day vs the rest).
8. Date-demeaned excess (stock selection within a date) and market-wide insider-buying breadth as a state variable.
9. Short interest (FINRA twice-monthly) as a two-sided modifier.
10. Information-driven exit and add: follow-on buyers or a cluster member selling after entry.
Next (recommended): ideas 1, 2 and 8 as one script (C41), because they decide whether the lead is about insiders at all. Needs Michael's go.

## Entry 85 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | C41a RESULT: insider-level history of the cluster buyers - planned-buy pattern, net seller before buying, return of the buyers' earlier buys (Michael: "do all of those at the same time") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran twice with identical numbers; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL (new SEC extraction and earlier-buy pricing; the cluster returns are the Entry 28 results); POST HOC (menu of 16 declared before the run); gross of cost; development years only

Script `research/canonical/insider_history.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/insider-history.yml` (PRs #146, then two fixes merged to main), branch main, **clean run 42632725951** (summary job 129214982813, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42632725951); an earlier run 42631495721 printed sections A-E with identical numbers and then stopped on a missing-key bug in section F (fixed). Extraction run 42631495721 job 129208668321: 60 quarterly SEC zips (2006q1-2020q4, filings through 2020-12-31), 158,345 purchase and 137,754 sale rows for the 12,566 buyers; 56,488 earlier-buy episodes (one per owner and issuer per 21 sessions), 43,622 distinct symbol-entries priced, 30,356 complete (13,125 had no ATR bars: not in the price archive).
**Definitions (all point in time: filings public before the entry, trades dated before the cluster's first counted purchase).** A planned buyer: bought at the SAME issuer one cycle earlier (weak) or one and two cycles earlier (strict) at the same calendar slot as his first counted purchase; cycles 1, 3, 6, 12 months, tolerance 4/7/7/7 days; planned buyers are removed from the count and the event survives only if four others remain. Net seller: same issuer, 365 days, dollars sold (code S) greater than dollars bought; primary ignores sales flagged 10b5-1, variant counts all sales. Earlier-buy score: mean excess over SPY in ATR (the project's 21-session method) of each buyer's earlier buys at any issuer whose hold had ended before the entry (needs 2, variant 3), averaged over scored buyers (needs 2).
**Cohort:** entries 2008-04-01 to 2020-12-31 (the SEC archive starts 2006Q1; that gives every event 24 months of look-back): n=1,165, stock +0.358, SPY +0.228, excess **+0.130**, 7.61 a month, 724 issuers, 747 dates, 375 weeks, week-block 95% -0.060 to +0.322. Every comparison below is inside this cohort.
**A. Planned pattern.** Strict filter (two earlier cycles): only 58 events have a planned buyer; survivors n=1,112 +0.129 vs removed n=53 +0.158: no effect. Weak filter (one earlier cycle): 408 events have a planned buyer; survivors n=823 **+0.080**, removed n=342 **+0.253** (without the three large episodes the difference flips to survivors +0.065 better). So removing regular buyers does NOT raise the edge; if anything the regular buyers did as well or better. Per cycle (weak): 12-month +0.348 (n=146) vs +0.099; 6-month +0.197 vs +0.121; 3-month +0.187 vs +0.118; 1-month n=34 -0.219. Single-question chances 24-87%.
**B. Net seller.** Sales ex-10b5-1, at least one such buyer: n=183 +0.208 vs n=982 +0.116 (difference +0.092; chance 55.7%); two or more: n=50 +0.375 vs +0.119. All sales (10b5-1 included): n=216 stock +0.602 / SPY +0.224 = excess **+0.378** vs n=949 +0.074 (difference +0.304; without the three episodes +0.198; week-block range for the YES group +0.089 to +0.686; single-question chance 1.1%, chance the best of the 14 A+B flags is that small by luck **13.1%**). Only 6.9% of buyer slots had any same-issuer sale in the prior 365 days. The variant that counts plan sales did better than the one that ignores them, the opposite of what I expected.
**C. Earlier-buy score.** Scored events: 490 (min 2) and 316 (min 3) of 1,165 (42% and 27%). Score above 0 (min 2): n=308 +0.274 vs n=182 -0.112 (difference +0.386; without episodes +0.357); min 3: n=196 +0.156 vs n=120 -0.417. Rank correlation only +0.053 (min 2) and +0.086 (min 3); thirds are not monotone (min 2: lowest -0.161, middle +0.359, top +0.193). Single-question chance 23.4% (min 2) and 12.5% (min 3).
**D. Together.** No planned buyer AND at least one net seller (ex-10b5-1): n=179 +0.254 vs rest n=986 +0.108 (without episodes difference +0.067). The 12 three-way cells are mostly under 30 (largest: no planned / no seller / score > 0, n=239 +0.244; same with score <= 0, n=156 -0.188).
**F. Inside the deep-fall rule (down 15+ ATR over 12 months, same cohort, n=243, excess +0.562):** earlier-buy score > 0 n=56 +0.930 vs n=33 -0.138 (post hoc, thin); net seller ex-10b5-1 n=41 +0.258 vs n=202 +0.623 (the seller flag is NOT additive there); strict planned filter removes 7 events.
**Reading.** Of the three ideas, only the earlier-buy score and the all-sales net-seller flag point the way Michael expected, and neither clears a best-of-menu test (best of 14 = 13.1%; the two score splits 23% and 12.5%). The planned-buy filter points the wrong way. Caveats: the score is noisy (21-session excess of one stock has a spread of about 2 ATR, 2-5 earlier buys per buyer), covers a minority of events, drops earlier buys of names missing from the price archive (survivors only), and good-score buyers may simply have bought in good market stretches. Amendments are de-duplicated by owner, issuer, date, code, shares and price, so a corrected row can count twice. Nothing here is an edge.
Next (recommended): leave the planned-buy filter out; carry "buyers' earlier-buy score > 0" and "any net-seller buyer (all sales)" as two named leads (C41b, C41c) and test them where an out-of-sample read is possible without the sealed years: split 2008-04 to 2014 vs 2015-2020 and a week-block bootstrap (a cheap script on the rows already built). Decision for Michael: whether those two get a place in the score idea (MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS item 1).

## Entry 86 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | C41b RESULT: robustness of the two Entry 85 leads - buyers' earlier-buy score and the all-sales net-seller flag (Michael: "explain what they are and why they would make sense to have an edge? But test it.") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL (same code family as Entry 85; the loader was refactored and the run reproduces the Entry 85 headline numbers: cohort 1,165, +0.130; net seller +0.378 vs +0.074; score > 0 +0.274 vs -0.112); POST HOC (both flags were found on this same cohort, so the era split is a consistency check, NOT an out-of-sample test); gross of cost; development years only

Script `research/canonical/insider_history.py` (new `robust` subcommand, PR merged to main), workflow `.github/workflows/insider-history-robust.yml`, run **42635322160** (job 129221836125, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42635322160); it reuses the artifacts of Entry 85's clean run 42632725951. The PASS RULE was fixed in the script before the run: a flag must pass all five of (1) positive difference in both eras (2008-04..2014-12, 2015-01..2020-12); (2) positive in at least two thirds of calendar years with >= 5 flagged and >= 5 other trades; (3) positive difference inside the SAME entry month with the month-block 95% range above 0; (4) positive outside the deep-fall group F with >= 30 flagged trades; (5) robust to its flavor (earlier-buy: both variants, earlier buys finished >= 365 days before the cluster and other issuers only; net seller: plan-sales-only events dropped).
**Earlier-buy score above 0 (min 2 earlier buys; scored events n=490 of 1,165): PASSES ALL FIVE.** All: n=308 +0.274 vs n=182 -0.112 (SPY +0.262 / +0.257 beside them), difference +0.386. Eras: 2008-14 n=136 +0.352 vs n=58 -0.221 (difference +0.573); 2015-20 n=172 +0.213 vs n=124 -0.061 (+0.274; the flagged group's week-block range -0.155 to +0.643 includes zero). Years: 10 of 13 positive (2018 -0.928, 2019 -0.163, 2010 -0.103); leave-one-year-out +0.232 to +0.544. Same-month: +0.537, month-block 95% +0.147 to +0.967 (63 months). Outside F: n=248 +0.130 vs n=143 +0.008 (+0.122); inside F: n=56 +0.930 vs n=33 -0.138. Variants: earlier buys ended >= 365 days before: n=251 +0.298 vs n=111 -0.337 (+0.635); other issuers only: n=51 +0.379 vs n=31 -0.767 (+1.147, thin). Arithmetic from the run figures: the scored group's mean equals the cohort's (+0.131 vs +0.130), so the lead is mostly an AVOID signal: skipping the 182 events whose buyers have a non-positive record would lift the cohort from +0.130 to about +0.175 (983 events), before costs. Entry 85's week-shuffle chance for this split was 23.4% (12.5% for the min-3 version), so the chance test and the pass rule disagree on strength: evidence is moderate, not strong.
**Net seller (all sales, >= 1 buyer): FAILS (2 of 5 tests).** Era 1 n=94 +0.054 vs +0.214 (difference -0.160); era 2 n=122 +0.628 vs -0.041 (+0.669). Years: 8 of 12 positive. Same-month +0.384, range -0.061 to +0.834. Outside F: +0.298 (n=160). Makeup: net seller on discretionary (non-plan) sales n=183 +0.208 (SPY +0.227); net seller ONLY when plan (10b5-1) sales are counted n=33 stock +1.528 / SPY +0.209 = excess **+1.319** (week-block +0.628 to +2.093); not a net seller n=949 +0.074. With the 33 plan-only events dropped the difference is +0.134 (without the three episodes +0.031). So the Entry 85 net-seller lead is essentially one 33-trade cell; the discretionary-seller version is about zero outside the big episodes. Count of net-seller buyers (all sales): 0 = +0.074 (n=949), 1 = +0.341 (n=144), 2+ = +0.452 (n=72).
Both together (scored events): score > 0 and seller n=57 +0.352; score > 0, not seller n=251 +0.257; score <= 0 and seller n=18 +1.004; neither n=164 -0.234.
**Reading.** The earlier-buy score is the one result from the insider-history work that survives a stricter look, as an avoid-the-bad-record filter that applies to about 42% of events; it is still a lead found on the cohort it was tested on, with noisy inputs (one stock's 21-session excess is a coin of wide spread, 2-5 earlier buys per buyer), survivors-only pricing of earlier buys, and no out-of-sample read (sealed years closed). The net-seller flag is not supported. The planned-buy filter stays unsupported (Entry 85), which differs from the published routine-versus-opportunistic result (Cohen, Malloy and Pomorski 2012) probably because a four-distinct-buyer cluster plus D-064 already removes most routine buying; that is a guess, not a finding.
Next (recommended): put "skip the trade when the buyers' average earlier-buy score is <= 0 (when scored)" through the Entry 82/83 portfolio engine to see what it does to return, drawdown and trade count after costs; keep the sealed-year read for when Michael is ready. Decision for Michael: whether the score belongs in his scoring idea (MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS item 1) as a filter rather than a bonus.

## Entry 87 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | C42 RESULT: base case rebuilt with a "different purchase dates" condition on the four buyers (Michael: "let's see what happens to the base case when we require different dates") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run logs); date-rule code is NEW, PROVISIONAL (selftests pass; the base set still reproduced `cluster_counts_by_year.find_events` in every run); POST HOC (three variants); gross of cost; development years only
Id note: the code, workflow and PR titles call this "C41" (named before Entries 85-86 took C41a/b/c); the candidate id is C42.
Scripts `research/canonical/refined_study.py` (new `DATE_RULE`), `research/canonical/distinct_dates_compare.py`, `research/canonical/refined_baseline.py` (Entry 25 count check skipped when a date rule is set); workflow `.github/workflows/distinct-dates-variant.yml` (id 412139788); branches `claude/distinct-dates-20261109` (PR #145) and `claude/distinct-dates-fix-20261109` (PR #149), both merged. Runs: **m4 42632884044** (summary job 129222434214), **d3 42632889166** (job 129224944304), **d2 42632894688** (job 129225944161); first attempts (42629714987, 42629718542, 42629722386) failed or were cancelled on the Entry 25 count check, no results from them. Compared with the rule-in-force set from run 42490748366 (Entry 28). Menu declared in the script docstring before any number was computed: m4 = the four buyers can each be given a DIFFERENT purchase date (Michael's wording; matching test, so a buyer with two purchase dates can fill a gap), d3 = the counted purchases span 3+ distinct dates, d2 = 2+ distinct dates. The entry is the next open after the filing that makes both "four distinct buyers" and the date condition true, so a cluster can enter later instead of being lost. Everything else unchanged (D-064 routine exclusion, identity test, $3M floor, one event per issuer per hold, 21-session price-only hold). Development entries (<= 2020-12-31) only; no sealed return read.
**Results (excess = stock minus same-window SPY, in the stock's ATR; SPY mean shown):**
| set | n | stock | SPY | excess | beat SPY | week-block 95% range | entry dates / weeks |
| rule in force | 1,372 | +0.348 | +0.194 | +0.154 | 747 | -0.010 to +0.327 | not re-printed |
| d2 (2+ dates) | 1,313 | +0.319 | +0.199 | +0.121 | 714 | -0.058 to +0.299 | 859 / 445 |
| d3 (3+ dates) | 1,105 | +0.333 | +0.218 | +0.115 | 593 | -0.077 to +0.299 | 728 / 405 |
| m4 (four different dates) | 686 | +0.297 | +0.240 | +0.057 | 366 | -0.145 to +0.273 | 489 / 290 |
**What the date rule removed (OLD trades whose issuer and entry date are not in the new set):** d2 n=159 excess +0.272 (range -0.074 to +0.601); d3 n=524 +0.140; m4 n=1,005 +0.183 (range +0.012 to +0.358). In every variant the removed trades did at least as well as the kept ones (kept with the same issuer and entry date: d2 +0.139, d3 +0.163, m4 +0.075). Caution: "removed" includes clusters that re-enter on a later date (the OTHER group: d2 n=100 -0.098, d3 n=257 -0.043, m4 n=319 +0.036), so the removed group is not purely all-one-day clusters; only d2 is close to that. About 12% of the rule-in-force trades (159 of 1,372) are clusters whose counted purchases all fall on one date.
**Without the three large episodes (2008Q4, 2011Q3, 2020Q1):** rule in force n=1,097 +0.309; d2 n=1,047 +0.279; d3 n=863 +0.304; m4 n=515 +0.169 (range -0.049 to +0.379). m4 excess by year swings from +1.235 (2013) to -0.654 (2015); m4 concentration: mean excess without the top 5 trades is -0.001 and the top 10 trades are 172% of total excess. At 0.10 ATR cost: d2 +0.021, d3 +0.015, m4 -0.043.
**Reading.** Requiring different purchase dates does not improve the base case; the more strictly it is applied, the lower the excess, and the trades it removes look the same or better than those it keeps. This does not support "same-day buying means a plan, so it is weaker"; a plausible reason (a guess, not a finding) is that D-064 already removes the same-date-same-price plan-like clusters, and what is left on one date is open-market buying at different prices (for example when the trading window reopens). All differences are inside the week-block ranges, so this says "no evidence the rule helps", not "the rule hurts". Not carried as a lead.
Next (recommended): none needed on this idea. If Michael wants a descriptive check, split the rule-in-force trades by "all on one date" versus "spread" with the Entry 85 plan-buy flags; otherwise leave the base rule as is.


## Entry 88 | 9 Nov 2026 | Claude | C43 RESULT (script header says C42; another chat used C42 for Entry 87): earlier-buy VETO and BONUS through the portfolio engine (Michael: "do the score less than 0 as a filter and the score greater than 0 as a bonus ... run those variants") | CONFIRMED (committed code ran; numbers from run log; the baseline reproduces Entry 82 to the dollar); NEW CODE, PROVISIONAL (new wrapper around the unchanged Entry 82 engine and the Entry 85 score); POST HOC (the flag was found on this cohort; menu of 5 variants declared before the run, chosen after Entry 86); development years only

Script `research/canonical/earlier_buy_portfolio.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/earlier-buy-portfolio.yml` (id 412193140), branch `claude/earlier-buy-portfolio-20261109` (PR #153, merged), run **42637754861** (job 129230354923, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/42637754861). It reuses the Entry 82 per-trade paths (run 42624583101) and the Entry 85 history artifacts (run 42632725951). Same engine: $100,000, 0.1% of equity per 1 ATR at 1.0U, 3 ATR stop, 42 sessions, 0.10 ATR round trip, Entry 78 size-ups, 8U cap not enforced. Tiers: GOOD = earlier-buy score > 0 (n=304 of the 1,273 trades), BAD = score <= 0 (176), NEUTRAL = not scoreable or entered before 2008-04 (793). V2 bonus = +0.105U (the R1 add-on, declared, not tuned); S1 sensitivity = +0.846U (the N add-on).
**Reproduction:** V0 baseline end $239,509, CAGR +5.98%, max drawdown -21.7%, identical to Entry 82.
**Portfolio (cost 0.10 ATR):** V0 baseline CAGR +5.98%, Sharpe 0.49, max DD -21.7%, 6.9 entries a month. V1 veto (skip BAD): CAGR +5.08%, Sharpe 0.46, max DD -20.8%, 6.0 a month, end $210,446. V2 bonus only: CAGR +6.04%, Sharpe 0.49, max DD -21.9%, end $241,390. V3 both: CAGR +5.19%, Sharpe 0.46, max DD -21.3%, end $213,902. S1 veto plus the big bonus: CAGR +5.58%, max DD -24.5%. SPY: CAGR +7.45%, max DD -56.5%. Gross (cost 0): baseline +7.22%, veto +6.15%, both +6.27%. Cost 0.20: baseline +4.78%, veto +4.03%, both +4.12%. The veto lowers the dollar return in every cost case and trims the drawdown by about one point. By calendar year the veto beats the baseline in 5 of 16 years, ties in 2 (2006-07, before the history starts) and loses in 9; its biggest cost is 2019 (+15.0% vs +25.7%).
**Why the Entry 86 edge does not turn into dollars here:** gross excess over SPY by tier on these 42-session stop paths (stock / SPY legs): GOOD +0.243 (n=304), BAD +0.109 (n=176, stock +0.578 / SPY +0.469), NEUTRAL +0.190 (n=793), all +0.191. The gap between GOOD and BAD is +0.13 here against +0.39 in Entry 86 (21-session hold, no stop, 1,165 events), and NEUTRAL sits between them. Inside F: GOOD +1.052 (n=56), BAD +0.295 (n=33), NEUTRAL +1.053 (n=180). Outside F: GOOD +0.061 (n=248), BAD +0.066 (n=143), NEUTRAL -0.063 (n=613). So on this path the flag separates only inside F, and only as an avoid-the-bad-record signal (GOOD equals NEUTRAL inside F); outside F GOOD and BAD are the same. The BAD trades also earned a positive absolute return (+0.58 ATR) in a long-only account, so skipping them removes profit even where they lagged SPY. The portfolio measures dollars, not excess over SPY.
**Veto versus a random skip:** 300 random draws skipping the same number (176) from the same 480 scoreable trades: mean end $214,257 (5-95%: $198,891 to $231,935) against the real veto's $210,446; 64% of random draws ended higher, 69% had a higher Sharpe, 28% kept trades with a higher mean excess. The veto is not distinguishable from a random skip of the same size in dollars.
**Reading.** In Michael's portfolio the earlier-buy flag does not help: as a veto it costs about 0.9 point of CAGR for a one-point smaller drawdown, and as a bonus at the declared size it changes almost nothing (+0.06 point of CAGR; better than baseline in 10 of 16 years, which is a near-coin flip). The Entry 86 lead stays a lead; this test does not support putting it in the sizing. The one place it still separates is inside F (33 BAD trades at +0.295 against +1.05), which is a small cell chosen after seeing this result and is untested.
Caveats: in-sample (the flag was found on this cohort); the 8U cap is not enforced (peak open 123-129U in 2008-09); survivorship and wipe-outs are not in the archive (Entry 65); costs are 0.10 ATR by instruction; one path.
Next (only if Michael wants it, POST HOC): veto applied inside F only (the 33 trades), with the same placebo draw. Otherwise leave the earlier-buy score out of the sizing.

Script `research/canonical/portfolio_sim.py` (PR #168), workflow `portfolio-sim.yml`, run **43469153531** (summary job 132038864697, https://github.com/kidcharlema9ne/insider-buying-research/actions/runs/43469153531). The "0.6 ratio" was read as the Sharpe of 0.66 of the flagged-only portfolio (Entry 83); that portfolio has a 3 ATR FIXED stop and a 42-session time exit, NO trailing stop (the 4 ATR trailing stop was a separate test on the deep-fall group, Entries 69-70). Gate: vol20 and vol90 = stdev of the last 20 / 90 daily log returns up to the close before entry; skip if vol20 > vol90; 2 of 1,273 trades had no reading and were taken.
**What the gate does:** it skips 78% of trades (993 of 1,273; flagged 642 of 823), and 99% of the trades entered in the three crash episodes (260 of 263), because volatility has just spiked when the insiders buy into a fall. Kept trades are 12-18 a year.
**Portfolio, 3 ATR stop, 42 sessions ($100,000, same sizes, 0.10 ATR):** FLAGGED only no gate: CAGR +7.26%, vol 11.8%, Sharpe 0.66, Sortino 1.00, max DD -20.0%, Calmar 0.36 (804 trades, 22% invested). FLAGGED + gate: CAGR +3.56%, vol 4.3%, Sharpe 0.84, Sortino 1.42, max DD -8.1%, Calmar 0.44 (181 trades, 7% invested, peak 61%). ALL trades: no gate Sharpe 0.49 / DD -21.7% / CAGR +5.98%; gate Sharpe 0.78 / DD -8.7% / CAGR +3.62% / Calmar 0.42 (280 trades). Skipped trades alone: flagged Sharpe 0.45, max DD -17.5%, CAGR +3.99%; all Sharpe 0.29. SPY: CAGR +7.45%, Sharpe 0.46, max DD -56.5%. No-stop exit: flagged Sharpe 0.57 -> 0.83, max DD -24.6% -> -8.5%.
**Per trade (gross excess over SPY, ATR, SPY beside it, week-block 95% range):** flagged kept n=181 +1.139 [+0.561, +1.687] (SPY +0.581); skipped n=642 +0.313 [+0.081, +0.533] (SPY +0.290). All kept n=280 +0.643 [+0.178, +1.099] (SPY +0.536); skipped n=993 +0.064 [-0.129, +0.259] (SPY +0.195).
**Random skip of the same count (642 of 823 flagged, 200 draws):** Sharpe median 0.52 (5th-95th 0.29-0.75); the gate's 0.84 was matched or beaten by 2% of draws. Max drawdown: random median -6.8% (5th-95th -9.6% to -5.3%); the gate's -8.1% was matched or beaten by 80% of draws, so the drawdown improvement is exposure (7% invested), not skill.
**Reading:** Sharpe and Calmar improve and beat chance on Sharpe, but the return roughly halves because the account sits in cash (no interest) 93% of the time; sizes were not scaled up for the lower trade count. The gate is mostly "do not buy during a volatility spike", which removes the 2008-09 and 2020 crash entries that carry the large Entry 83 gains in the flagged group. Status: descriptive, post hoc, one gate tested (best-of-one, but chosen after Entries 82-83 were seen); not an edge. Caveats: 181 kept trades, tens of independent periods, survivorship (Entry 65) and costs unchanged.
Next (recommended): rerun the gated flagged portfolio with base size scaled up so average exposure matches the ungated one (about 2.5x), so CAGR and drawdown can be compared at equal risk; that is the test that settles whether the gate helps or just trades less.

Volatility gate for comparison: kept +1.139, skipped +0.313 (Entry 98).

ATR percent gate Sharpe 0.65 vs random median 0.48, 16% of draws as good; max DD -6.9% vs random median -5.6%, 86% as good or better. Volatility gate (Entry 98): Sharpe 2% of draws, max DD 80%.
**Reading:** historical volatility is the only reading whose Sharpe gain clearly beats a random skip of the same size. The ATR readings give smaller Sharpe gains (0.71 and 0.65 vs 0.66 ungated, the percent version none) that chance matches 10-16% of the time, and their drawdown gain is an exposure effect, as in Entry 98. All three gates do much the same thing (avoid buying after a volatility spike, i.e. the crash episodes) and cut the return roughly in half to two thirds because the account sits in cash (no interest, sizes not scaled up). Status: descriptive, post hoc; not an edge. Two readings tested here plus one in Entry 98, so the best of three is the volatility gate and that one is not independent evidence.
Next (recommended): the equal-exposure rerun (sizes scaled so average invested matches the ungated 22%) for the historical-volatility gate, and, if wanted, the raw ATR gate beside it.

**PRIMARY, full period (k: skipped, trades, Sharpe, Calmar, max DD, CAGR, invested):** none 0%, 823, 0.66, 0.36, -20.0%, +7.26%, 22%. 1.0 78%, 181, 0.84, 0.44, -8.1%, +3.56%, 7%. 1.2 61%, 325, 0.79, 0.48, -10.4%, +4.96%, 12%. **1.3 52%, 399, 0.84, 0.54, -10.8%, +5.81%, 15%.** 1.4 43%, 468, 0.79, 0.44, -13.7%, +6.10%, 16%. 1.5 37%, 518, 0.75, 0.43, -14.2%, +6.12%, 17%. 1.6 30%, 580, 0.69, 0.38, -16.3%, +6.11%, 19%. 1.7 22%, 639, 0.65, 0.36, -17.2%, +6.15%, 20%. 1.8 14%, 705, 0.65, 0.35, -19.2%, +6.62%, 21%. 1.9 8%, 758, 0.65, 0.35, -19.5%, +6.89%, 21%. 2.0 3%, 797, 0.64, 0.36, -19.6%, +6.99%, 22%. Kept-trade gross excess over SPY (ATR): 1.0 +1.139, 1.3 +0.824, 1.5 +0.685, 2.0 +0.498, none +0.495. Shape: Sharpe is highest at 1.0-1.3 (0.84), falls steadily to the ungated 0.65 by k 1.7, no isolated spike. Best Sharpe on the grid is k=1.0 (random skip of 642 trades: 2% of draws as good, Entry 98); best Calmar is k=1.3.
**PRIMARY, out of era (PASS RULE declared: the k chosen in each era must beat no gate on BOTH Sharpe and Calmar in the other era):** fit on 2006-2010 -> k=1.0 (Sharpe 1.02 vs 0.76); on 2011-2020 gated Sharpe 0.77, Calmar 0.32, max DD -6.8%, CAGR +2.18% vs no gate 0.61, 0.29, -18.0%, +5.19%: beats on both. Fit on 2011-2020 -> k=1.3 (0.85 vs 0.61); on 2006-2010 gated Sharpe 0.94, Calmar 0.94, max DD -10.2%, CAGR +9.52% vs no gate 0.76, 0.57, -20.0%, +11.35%: beats on both. **PASS (2 of 2).** Era Sharpe by k, 2006-2010: none 0.76, 1.0 1.02, 1.2 0.90, 1.3 0.94, 1.5 0.87, 1.7 0.77, 2.0 0.76; 2011-2020: none 0.61, 1.0 0.77, 1.2 0.80, 1.3 0.85, 1.5 0.71, 1.7 0.61, 2.0 0.59.
**SECONDARY (literal wording, skip if vol90 > k x vol20), full period:** every k is at or below the ungated Sharpe 0.66 (k 1.0: 0.45; 1.4: 0.61; 2.0: 0.62); NOT SHOWN (0 of 2 out-of-era tests).
**Reading:** the looser gate keeps most of the Sharpe gain until about k 1.3 and returns much more than the strict gate (CAGR +5.81% vs +3.56%, Calmar 0.54 vs 0.44) because it skips fewer trades (52% vs 78%); beyond k 1.5 the gate stops doing anything. Passes the declared out-of-era rule, but: 18 looks (9 k x 2 readings) plus references, the eras are not independent (2008-09 and 2020 sit in both families of trades the gate removes), the gate mostly removes the crash-quarter entries, and the return is still below the ungated +7.26% (the account holds more cash). Status: lead, post hoc, not an edge; k 1.3 is a plateau-edge value, not a fitted constant to trade.
Next (recommended): the equal-exposure rerun (scale sizes so average invested matches the ungated 22%) for k 1.0 and k 1.3 side by side, since the choice between them is return versus drawdown and only equal exposure compares them fairly.

Fit 2011-2020 -> k 2.0 (0.54 vs 0.57); on 2006-2010 gated 0.67 / 0.38 vs 0.66 / 0.37: beats by a hair. **NOT SHOWN (1 of 2).** Literal reading (skip if vol90 > k x vol20): every k at or below the ungated Sharpe; NOT SHOWN (0 of 2).

**Reading:** the Entries 98-100 gate result depends on the exit. With the 3 ATR fixed stop and 42-session exit the strict gate lifted Sharpe 0.66 to 0.84 and passed the out-of-era rule; with the 3 ATR / 4 ATR trailing exit it lifts Sharpe only 0.61 to 0.65, is matched by 13% of random skips, and fails the out-of-era rule (it makes 2011-2020 worse). The drawdown cut is exposure again (10% invested vs 40%). The gate is therefore NOT a robust improvement; the earlier lead should be read as specific to one exit and one set of eras. The trailing exit itself earns more per dollar at higher exposure (CAGR +8.08% vs +7.26% flagged) but with a deeper drawdown (-30.1% vs -20.0%) and a lower Sharpe (0.61 vs 0.66). Status: descriptive, post hoc, not an edge.
Next (recommended): Michael to confirm which exit is THE exit (3 ATR fixed + 42 sessions, which is what he instructed in Entry 82, or 3 ATR initial + 4 ATR trailing with a 252-session cap, or another), and record it in research_docs/MICHAEL_DETERMINATIONS.md as a dated item in his words; then run any further gate or sizing work only on that exit.
